# Changelog

All notable changes to Marinara-RPG-Extension are documented here.

This project follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Version numbers follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) once a 1.0
release is cut; pre-1.0 minor bumps may include breaking changes (called out
explicitly when they do).

## [Unreleased]

- **B19 — cross-game character continuity.** Activating a character now
  stamps a per-ruleset "last played" pointer (synced across your browsers).
  Opening a chat that's bound to that ruleset but has no character yet shows
  an explicit "Continue as <name>" offer — one click adopts them into the
  chat (replacing the untouched bootstrap placeholder), "Not now" saves
  nothing and never nags past the session. Never auto-adopts. Stamping is
  gated on the round-24 ruleset-confirmation latch so the pointer can't be
  filed under the wrong system. Round 30 (live-repro'd on the smoke test):
  the pointer now stamps at the confirmation chokepoint (activation always
  preceded confirmation, so it never stamped in a normal session), the
  empty-roster check tolerates the pristine bootstrap record the round-24
  deferred-save replay materializes (structural compare against a fresh
  blank sheet — one edited field withdraws the offer), a confirm-time stamp
  never clobbers the pointer with the untouched placeholder, and adopting
  retracts the placeholder's blank record (adapter tombstone) instead of
  orphaning it. (20-probe eval-extracted harness, 4 negative controls, incl.
  a lifecycle probe replaying the live log's exact event order.) Round 31
  (second live repro): a chat that becomes bound AFTER first entry — the
  normal create-chat-then-assign-agents flow — now notifies B19 the moment
  any stamp writer succeeds (one choke-point helper on all three writers,
  cached only on PATCH success), so the offer appears in the same session
  with no chat re-entry; plus three B19 console lines (pointer stamped /
  chat bound / offer shown) so the chain is traceable. (23 probes.) Round
  32: the pointer can no longer be poisoned — stamping declines for a
  character whose stored sheet belongs to another ruleset (same rule as the
  round-24 write guard) and for legacy untouched "Player" placeholders; and
  "untouched roster" is now an explicit bootstrap flag cleared only by
  deliberate use (sheet edits, rename, bundle apply, state mutations,
  adopt) instead of a byte-compare that per-ruleset normalizers defeated.
  All stamp declines log their reason. (33 probes.) Round 33 (Corey
  ruling): **virgin chats stay unbound** — entering a chat with no ruleset
  stamp and nothing to derive no longer claims it for the ambient active
  ruleset: no stamp write, no agent auto-add, sheet saves held (edits made
  while unbound replay under the ruleset you then pick; leaving without
  binding drops them — there is no system to file them under). Deliberate
  activation is the binding moment, and the Library's active-ruleset row
  gains a "Use in this chat" button so a virgin chat can bind to the
  already-active system without a fake switch. Chats with managed agents
  still derive their binding; stamped chats are byte-identical to before
  (probed by two-fragment comparison). (8 new probes + 33 regression.)
- **Per-system character records (Round A).** Every character now stores one
  sheet PER SYSTEM (`(character, ruleset)` composite records). Existing
  records migrate automatically on first load in a confirmed chat — each to
  the system its sheet already belonged to, which permanently resolves the
  "same character in two systems freezes the sheet" collision — with a
  visible, dismissible notice when it happens. Copy-verify-then-retract
  migration: a failed write or failed verification always leaves the
  original untouched. Bundles with '@' in character ids are now rejected
  (reserved separator). (20 new probes incl. a negative control that
  reproduces the old freeze; 48 regression probes across 3 suites.)
- **Sheet ↔ card/persona binding (Round B).** Bind a character sheet to an
  engine character card or your persona from a new "Bound to" dropdown on
  the sheet's identity row. Bound sheets AUTO-RESTORE into any chat where
  that card or persona is present — new chats, conversation branches, game
  session rotations — reconstituting a whole party with zero clicks (an
  untouched placeholder is replaced by a lone restored character
  automatically). Works in both RP and game mode. Unbound characters keep
  the explicit "Continue as" offer. A deleted card never deletes your
  sheet; if a same-named card appears, a one-line prompt offers re-binding
  (never automatic). The extension never writes to engine cards or
  personas — bindings are local to your server by design. (37 new probes
  incl. a whole-flow proof that the only non-GET engine request is one
  read-only name lookup; 68 regression probes across 4 suites.)
- **Dead-plumbing tombstones trimmed** (−657 B source): the round-26/27
  `isChildListOrigin` removal narration is gone; the load-bearing lesson
  survives as a forward invariant — never rewrite a message element's
  innerHTML (it detaches React's handlers; the dead-turn-buttons bug).
- **GM dice doctrine completed for the last four rulesets** (fate-core,
  blades-in-the-dark, genesys, lasers-and-feelings): tool-first `roll_dice`
  rolls, never-invent, player rolls stay authoritative, fresh rolls on every
  regenerate — adapted honestly to each system's dice (fate rolls `4d3-8`
  with the fudge mapping stated aloud; genesys maps symbol dice to numeric
  faces with the printed distributions quoted, or labels estimates plainly;
  blades and L&F read per-die results, never sums).
- **Authoring docs completed for the roll-high open-ended d100 fields**
  (`direction`, `bonusFormula`, `openEnded`, `unusualFaces`) across
  AGENTS.md, AUTHORING-PROMPT.md, README, and the docs/ authoring set —
  the fields shipped with Rolemaster in v1.1.0; the docs now match the schema.
- **Rolemaster**: GM agent gained the Resistance-Roll target-number shape
  guidance, user-lorebook precedence (your table rows beat its estimates,
  your house rules beat its bundled rules, and it names which it applied),
  and the guide now warns about the engine's 100-entry-per-lorebook default
  cap and token-budget sizing for very large lorebooks.

## v1.1.0 (2026-08-24)

Everything below is written for people who play with this thing. The
per-round engineering entries further down are the detail record and stay
where they are.

**Still requires Marinara Engine 2.4.3 or newer**, and it is still a zip
import (`releases/1.1.0/Marinara-RPG-Extension.extension.zip`), never a
loose `.js` file.

### Upgrading from 1.0.0 — the new ruleset bundles are REQUIRED

Re-importing your ruleset bundle is not an optional tidy-up step this
release; it is part of the fix. The compatibility work below is split
across the extension and the ruleset bundles, so a bundle downloaded with
1.0.0 will not behave correctly against the 1.1.0 extension: its
state-writing agent still runs on the old pre-narration timing and will
invent dice results instead of copying them out of the narration, it lacks
the GM dice doctrine that routes rolls through the engine's real RNG, and
it predates the self-repairing preset reconciliation. **Re-download and
re-import the current `bundle.json` for every system you play** — fresh
copies of all sixteen ship in `releases/1.1.0/install-files/`. The order
is: import the new zip, click **Review and Run** on the new code hash,
then re-import each bundle from the Ruleset dialog. Your characters and
sheets are untouched by any of it, and once the new bundle is in the
extension repoints your preset's agent sections by itself.

### The headline four

- **Compatibility fixes for the problems 1.0 shipped with.** Three of them,
  all engine-2.4.x behavior that 1.0 predated, and all self-healing now.
  *Preset-owned agent placement:* since engine 2.4.0 a roleplay preset
  decides where an agent's output goes, and an agent with no matching
  **Agent Data** section had its output thrown away silently — the agents
  ran, cost tokens, looked healthy in their run history, and the narrator
  never saw a word. *Agent type collision on reinstall:* Marinara can never
  change an existing agent's `type`, so re-importing a bundle created the
  MRR agents fresh and orphaned every preset section you had added — the
  same silent discard, arriving the moment you updated. *Chat-preset
  metadata wipes:* applying a chat preset could erase the chat's ruleset
  stamp, and the sheet would come back blank. The extension now repoints
  orphaned agent sections after every bundle import, re-derives a wiped
  ruleset stamp from the chat's own enabled agents, and logs one line per
  repair to the browser console. First-time setup still uses **Manage MRR
  Agents → Add agent sections to active preset**; only the re-run became
  automatic.
- **Character sheets now save to the server, not just your browser.** Sheets,
  the per-chat character roster, the active-character pointer, the active
  ruleset, and the spellbook cache sync through Marinara's own extension
  storage on 2.4.x engines, with your browser's local copy kept as an
  always-written mirror. Every stored value carries a timestamp, and on load
  the newer of the two sides wins per key — so a browser that saved and then
  crashed before the upload landed reclaims its own edit instead of losing it
  to a stale server copy. In practice: clearing site data no longer wipes
  your characters, and the same characters show up in a second browser. Some
  things stay deliberately device-local (panel position and size, the ruleset
  library). If storage degrades — a sheet too large to sync, a failed write —
  a warning strip appears at the top of the sheet instead of failing quietly.
- **Chats now remember their character sheets and their ruleset.** Entering a
  chat activates that chat's ruleset automatically, and the sheet and dice
  widget follow it. Sheets are held per character rather than smeared across
  whatever chat you happened to open, with protection against one system's
  values bleeding into another's game. (One honest residual: the same library
  character *deliberately* loaded under two different rulesets still shares
  one record. Accidental bleed is impossible; that deliberate case is known
  design debt.)
- **A ruleset can now offer more than one dice mechanic.** A system is no
  longer locked to a single resolution mode: it can declare named alternate
  mechanics, bind individual skills to them, and let you pick one from a
  selector on the dice widget — and the resulting roll tag names the mechanic
  it used, so the GM reads what actually happened. **Dice-pool and roll-under
  are the two modes wired end to end**, with Old School Essentials shipping
  as the pilot (percentile Thief skills and X-in-6 door checks alongside its
  d20 attacks and saves). This is the foundation; more mechanics get
  converted in later releases.

### Also worth your attention

- **Real dice, end to end.** With the chat's **Enable Tool Use** toggle on,
  the GM resolves random outcomes through Marinara's server-side `roll_dice`
  tool — a true RNG — and narrates the number it returned. A model that
  invents dice is a biased die, and reliably a kinder one than the real
  thing. The other half of the chain: the piece that writes to your sheet now
  runs *after* the narration instead of before it, so it copies numbers out
  of the story instead of guessing at numbers that did not exist yet, and it
  cites where each one came from (`reason="GM narrated 15 poison damage"`) so
  you can audit any value that looks wrong. In 1.0 the sheet and the story
  disagreed on every random number; they agree now.
- **Full swipe support.** Each swipe of a reply carries its own state. Swiping
  away from a variant reverts the changes it made; swiping back reapplies
  them; regenerating rolls fresh dice rather than reusing the last version's
  numbers. Save-scumming your way through a bad round is now a supported
  feature rather than an accident.
- **The turn-editing buttons work again.** Regenerate, the swipe arrows, and
  edit had been dead in every chat with the extension installed — a
  long-standing bug that earlier sessions kept misattributing to whatever
  else was on fire. They render, click, and work.
- **Install and reinstall repair themselves** (see the compatibility item
  above). The one preset the extension cannot repair is the stock read-only
  **Marinara Universal** — save a copy, select the copy for your chat, and
  run the one-click assist against that.
- **Two more systems: Old School Essentials and Rolemaster (RMFRP)**, taking
  the shipped count from 14 to 16.
- **A smaller download.** The shipped loader is stripped of its comments at
  build time — a transform that removes comments and nothing else, with every
  string, number, and pattern in the file verified identical afterward. The
  packaged loader drops from 760 KB in 1.0.0 to 623 KB, well clear of the
  engine's 1 MiB ceiling.
- **Docs overhaul.** Five rulesets that shipped without an install guide
  (Call of Cthulhu 7e, GURPS Lite, Pathfinder 2e, The Stewpot, Trophy Dark)
  have one; all 16 guides now document the **Enable Tool Use** toggle and the
  preset agent-sections step; and six screenshots walk the install flow —
  enabling extensions, confirming the import, the permissions the extension
  asks for, the preset agent-sections dialog, the dice-tool toggle, and the
  dice widget itself.
- **Download verification.** Both release-folder guides publish SHA-256
  hashes for the importable zip and the loose loader, plus the exact code
  hash Marinara's own **Review and Run** dialog should show you. You are
  about to grant a file full page access inside your own engine; thirty
  seconds of checking is worth it.

### Known notes

- **Multi-mechanic dice is wiring and preview, not a finished feature.** Two
  of the nine resolution modes are converted (dice-pool, roll-under) and one
  ruleset pilots it (OSE). The remaining seven modes cannot be used as
  alternate mechanics yet, and the schema rejects them rather than letting
  them fail silently.
- **Game mode is untested this release.** The live-fire work behind these
  fixes ran in roleplay mode. Game mode is expected to work — it skips the
  preset assembler entirely and keeps the older injection path — but nobody
  played a session in it before shipping.
- **Vampire: The Masquerade 20th, Werewolf: The Apocalypse 20th, and Exalted
  Versus World of Darkness still run the previous-generation state flow.**
  Their state-writing agent stays on the older pre-narration timing, which
  means it can still guess at dice results, and their chat transcripts still
  show raw `[mrr-state: ...]` tags. Each needs its own pass; none of them has
  had one yet.
- Historical `releases/1.0.0/...` paths in the per-round entries below are
  left as written — they record where those files lived at the time. The
  current release folder is `releases/1.1.0/`.

### Added (overnight docs+tooling round, 2026-08-24)
- **Artifact freshness gate (`tools/check-freshness.mjs`, `npm run check:freshness`).** Re-runs the full build pipeline — every ruleset bundle and agents file, the loader's embedded CSS, and the release package — against the current working tree and asserts a zero diff. Clean tree in, clean tree out. Committed sources can no longer silently drift from committed artifacts. Refuses to judge a tree whose artifact paths are already dirty (exit 2; `--allow-dirty` overrides), leaves rebuilt files in place on failure so `git diff` shows the drift, and holds the two non-reproducible values constant (release-zip mtimes excluded — its payload is byte-checked separately; `exportedAt` pinned to the committed value). Verified FRESH at `edd01d4`.
- **npm scripts for every tool that lacked one** — `build-extension-package`, `validate-extension-package`, `build-character-card`, and the five preview CLIs.
- **INSTALL.md for `coc7e`, `gurps-lite`, `pathfinder2e`, `stewpot`, `trophy-dark`** — per-ruleset facts read from each `bundle.json`, sanity-check tags taken from the loader's actual output (the authored `diceTagFormat` examples disagree in three places).
- **`COUPLINGS.md` "Ledger discipline."** A `confirmed` row now carries a verification date + engine version and expires to `stale-recheck` on any engine minor bump (round 21 found three `confirmed` rows false; nothing in the ledger forced a re-look).

### Changed (pre-push documentation truth pass, 2026-08-24)

Docs-only round: no code, no schema, no generated artifact source changed. `npm run check:freshness` re-verified **FRESH** after the pass.

- **"Enable Tool Use" now documented in all 16 ruleset guides.** The chat-level toggle (**Chat Settings → Function Calling → "Enable Tool Use"**) is what hands the GM Marinara's server-side `roll_dice` tool — a true RNG instead of a model inventing plausible-looking numbers. Seven guides never mentioned it: `blades-in-the-dark`, `exwod`, `fate-core`, `genesys`, `lasers-and-feelings`, `vtmv20`, `w20`. Each now carries the canonical section (matching `ose`'s wording), with the per-system stakes named in that system's own terms and the "you do not need to grant `roll_dice` to the MRR agents themselves" note intact. `rg -l "Enable Tool Use" rulesets/*/INSTALL.md` = 16/16.
- **The root `README.md` mentioned dice tools nowhere.** New **Step 6c** in the install flow: why the toggle matters (proper dice probabilities — the GM rolls real dice server-side instead of inventing numbers) and exactly where it lives.
- **Round-28 auto-reconciliation was user-facing nowhere but `docs/INSTALL.md`'s preset step.** Documented that a bundle reinstall now repairs its own orphaned preset agent-sections (console: `reconciled N orphaned agent marker(s)`) and re-derives a chat's ruleset stamp when a chat-preset apply wipes it — in `README.md`'s preset step, the Updating sections of `rulesets/{dnd5e,exalted3e,ose,rolemaster}/INSTALL.md`, and `docs/INSTALL.md`'s Updates section. Every mention states the boundary explicitly: **first-time setup still uses the "Add agent sections to active preset" button; only the reinstall re-run became automatic**, and the stock read-only "Marinara Universal" preset is the one case that cannot be repaired.
- **`rulesets/dnd5e/gm-agent.md` and `rulesets/exalted3e/gm-agent.md` still documented the retired paste flow** as the install path ("Paste ONLY the fenced block into … Create Custom Agent"). Both headers now match `ose`'s: the bundle auto-installs the GM/helper agents at 2.4.3 and you do not paste by hand. dnd5e's round-12 leak warning is kept verbatim, re-framed as the conditional it now is ("if you do paste by hand, paste ONLY the fenced block"). Prose-only edits above the fence — `build-bundle.mjs` extracts the fenced ` ```text ` block alone, so no bundle byte moved.
- **`releases/1.0.0/INSTALL-GUIDE.md` + `releases/1.0.0/README.md` brought current** (stale since round 21 — these are what a downloader actually reads). Added the Enable Tool Use step to both, the preset-sections + auto-reconcile wording to both, and a real update path: bundle re-import updates the *managed* agents in place, but **hand-pasted GM prompt copies go stale silently and must be re-pasted** from the ruleset's `gm-agent.md`. Surgical insertions into the existing flow; neither file restructured.
- **Screenshots wired into the install flow** — six shots under `docs/screenshots/`, each with descriptive alt text: `enable_extensions.png` and `extensions_enabled_installed.png` at the import step, `extension_installed_permissions.png` beside a new Permissions explanation, `import_agents_preset.png` at the preset agent-sections step, `enable_dice_tool.png` at the Enable Tool Use step, and `sheet_dice_widget.png` where player rolls are described. Referenced from `README.md` and `docs/INSTALL.md` with paths correct for each file's location. The release-folder docs get **no** image references — `tools/build-extension-package.mjs` does not copy `docs/screenshots/` into `releases/`, so they point readers at the repo docs instead.

### Fixed (documentation truth pass, round 2 — 2026-08-24)

Second docs-only round, closing the findings the first one reported plus the six real screenshots. `npm run check:freshness` re-verified **FRESH**.

- **`releases/1.0.0/README.md` was telling downloaders to do the one thing every other doc forbids.** Its Step 3 walked users into importing the loose `RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` through the retired *Settings → Extensions* screen — at 2.4.3 that builds a sandboxed Worker extension with `capabilities: []`, which reports success and then cannot run. Rewritten to the zip / folder / manifest-only flow with both engine gates named, the loose-`.js` trap called out, and the loader reframed as an inspection-and-hash artifact rather than an import target.
- **Four more stale claims in the same file, all corrected against source.** Title said `v0.4.0` (it is the 1.0.0 release folder) and the Status footer still claimed "v0.3 … tested against Marinara Engine v1.5.6"; the "What's new" list was the v0.4.0 one, replaced with the current summary (2.4.3 compliance, one extension for both modes, `post_processing` state mutator on the run-history transport plus the dice doctrine, per-chat ruleset stamping, self-repairing preset sections, comment-stripped packaging). The AI hard-requirements table demanded the **50-character reputation-cap workaround** — refuted at round 21, engine value verified at 500 on 2.4.4 — now says the cap is not the number either doc claimed and to keep action text short. The "sub-agents install disabled by default as of v0.3+" row is replaced with what the loader actually does: `enabled: ag.enabled === true` honors the bundle's per-item flag (every shipped bundle sets it), and on 2.4.3+ that flag is not what activates an agent anyway — enabling is per game, after launch.
- **Three broken image references removed from `releases/1.0.0/README.md`.** It pointed at `docs/screenshots/*.png`, but `releases/1.0.0/docs/` does not exist and the packager never creates it. The described UI is now prose, with a parenthetical pointing at the repo docs for the actual shots.
- **Preset agent-sections step added to `rulesets/{exwod,w20,lasers-and-feelings}/INSTALL.md`.** These three documented the 2.4.0 silent-discard failure mode nowhere, so a roleplay table following them got agents that ran, cost tokens, and were thrown away unread. Step copied from `ose`, including the round-28 self-repair note.
- **Six more `gm-agent.md` headers aligned off the retired paste flow** — `coc7e`, `pathfinder2e`, `gurps-lite`, `stewpot`, `trophy-dark`, `vtmv20`. (`exwod` and `w20` already carried the correcting parenthetical.) Prose above the fence only; `build-bundle.mjs` reads the fenced block alone, so no bundle byte moved.
- **"Verify your download" added to both release-folder guides.** `sha256sum` the importable zip and the loose loader against the published values before importing — the file is about to be granted full page access inside the reader's own engine. Values carry the literal `<ZIP_SHA256>` / `<LOADER_SHA256>` tokens, substituted after the final rebuild so they cannot go stale in the repo.
- **A "Permissions" explanation now accompanies the permissions screenshot** in `README.md` and `docs/INSTALL.md`: what **Full page access** covers and why MRR needs it (page access for the sheet / dice widget / Ruleset dialog; same-origin engine API for bundle install and state write-back), stated from the manifest and the engine's own lane semantics — no invented permission names. Paired with the player-vs-GM dice distinction: the widget's roll goes into *your* message and is authoritative, never re-rolled; the GM's rolls come from the engine `roll_dice` tool behind the chat toggle.


## [Unreleased]

Pending the next published release. New entries land here between releases;
each release moves the entries into a dated section below.

> The entries below target **v1.0.0**: Marinara 2.0.1 compliance + single-track
> consolidation (the GM-mode and RP-mode extensions merge into this one).

### Removed — pre-1.0.0 release folders

- Deleted `releases/v0.4.0/`, `releases/v0.4.1/`, `releases/v0.4.2/`, and `releases/v0.5.0/` (plus the loose `releases/Marinara-RPG-Extension-v0.4.2.zip` and `releases/Marinara-RPG-Extension-v0.5.0.zip` archives) — incompatible with the 2.4.3 rebuild; `releases/1.0.0/` is the only supported release folder going forward. Repo-wide stale-path sweep updated every live doc reference (including several self-references inside `releases/1.0.0/` itself) that still pointed at the removed `releases/v0.4.x/` paths.

### Added — new ruleset: Old School Essentials

- Old School Essentials (B/X-compatible retroclone), born from the authoring-docs acceptance test, promoted from `rulesets-staging/` to `rulesets/ose/` after a cleanup pass (Fighter-only XP table now embedded and labeled throughout, sub-agent lorebook entry title aligned with the shipped naming convention, stray reference to a non-shipped "Friction Log" removed). Ships with the standard 15-system pipeline (build, validate-rulesets, validate-bundles) green.

### Fixed — authoring-docs friction log (accept-test findings F1/F2/F3/F4/F6/F10/F11)

- **F1:** `AUTHORING-PROMPT.md`'s paste-into-your-chat-AI prompt block named only `AGENTS.md` + `schema/bundle.schema.json` + reference bundles, never `releases/1.0.0/docs-for-ai/`. Rewritten to point at `docs-for-ai/01` through `07` plus `docs/AUTHORING-PHASE-6.md` as the primary references, with a new "file-upload only" section listing exactly what to upload for AIs that can't browse the repo. Also reconciled the file's top intro (previously self-described as a "pre-v0.4 template" that doesn't cover current features, which was no longer true once the prompt block itself was fixed) and its stale "paste JS into Settings → Extensions" install instructions (now the actual External Extensions zip-import flow).
- **F2/F3:** `docs-for-ai/01-OVERVIEW.md`, `02-AGENTS-SYSTEM-AGNOSTIC.md`, and `06-BUILD-PIPELINE.md` all taught a stale architecture — a separate `agents.json` imported through a Marinara "Import Agents" dialog, with agents explicitly NOT included in the bundle. Ground truth (verified against `tools/build-bundle.mjs` source and the shipped `dnd5e` reference's `INSTALL.md`): role agents are embedded in `bundle.json`'s `additionalAgents[]`; the bundle is the one user-facing import; agents are enabled per game after the game launches; `build-agents.mjs`'s `agents.json` is a toolchain-parity artifact only, read by nothing in the install flow. Rewrote every affected passage in all three files (plus `07-EXAMPLE-PROMPTS.md`, which had the same defect in its copy-paste prompts and needed the same fix to avoid generating new rulesets with wrong instructions) and reconciled `AUTHORING-PROMPT.md`'s intro. `build-agents.mjs` itself is untouched; its doc sections now describe its output's real role honestly.
- **F4:** verified the reputation-tag `action` length cap against current engine HEAD (`~/Marinara-Engine`, v2.4.4, commit `b1ec60409`): `GAME_REPUTATION_ACTION_MAX_LENGTH = 500` (`packages/server/src/routes/game.routes.ts:1714`, consumed at `:9913`) — NOT the 50-character figure this project's docs previously cited, which was accurate for engine v1.5.6 but has since been widened. Wrote the verified 500-char finding into `AUTHORING-PROMPT.md`, `docs-for-ai/06-BUILD-PIPELINE.md`, and `docs/ENGINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` (whose reputation section now carries a re-verification note superseding its original "v1.5.6" framing for that one item only, with the old finding kept inline for history). Guidance everywhere now tells authors to keep `action` short rather than encode either specific number as fact.
- **F6:** `docs-for-ai/03-RULESET-SCHEMA.md` was missing several load-bearing fields present in shipped rulesets: `saves[]` (including the required `linkedAttribute` field's D&D-shaped assumption and the flat-save workaround the `ose` ruleset uses), `conditions[]`, `classOptions[]`, `header{}`, `backgrounds.textOnly`, `scenarioDefaultDerive`, and per-attribute `modifierFormula`. Documented all seven with shapes taken from the shipped `dnd5e`/`exalted3e`/`ose` ruleset.json files and the schema source. Added a new "Known schema limits" subsection: one `resolution.mode` per ruleset (multi-mechanic systems need prose for the other subsystems), single universal `xpTable` (no per-class tables), and `modifierFormula`'s arithmetic-only ceiling (no piecewise/stepped tables — B/X-style ability modifiers and RMFRP Table T-2.1 can't be expressed; documented workaround is a lorebook step-table entry + player-entered flat values).
- **F10:** `docs-for-ai/04-LOREBOOK-FORMAT.md` didn't mention that `build-bundle.mjs` auto-derives additional lorebook entries from `ruleset.json` (one per attribute/skill/condition/derived-stat, plus a difficulty-ladder entry) and merges them with hand-authored entries. Documented the merge behavior, the name-collision-favors-hand-authored rule, the `ruleset.lorebookExpansions` override escape hatch, and the expected count jump (14–25 hand-authored typically becomes 30–45 in the built bundle — confirmed against the `ose` build's own `43 entries [21 hand + 22 derived]` report).
- **F11:** the canonical Sub-Agents lorebook entry title is "Sub-Agents — what they do and how to enable them" (the shipped `dnd5e` title) — several docs and prompts instead used "Optional Sub-Agents — what they do and how to enable". Fixed in `docs-for-ai/04-LOREBOOK-FORMAT.md`, `AUTHORING-PROMPT.md`, and `docs-for-ai/07-EXAMPLE-PROMPTS.md`'s copy-paste prompts. (Note: the shipped `w20` and `exwod` rulesets still use the older title in their own `lorebook.json`/`bundle.json` — left as-is, out of scope for this doc-only pass; flagged for a future consistency sweep.)
- Gate outputs after this round: `npm run validate-rulesets` and `npm run validate-bundles` both 15/15 green; `node tools/validate-extension-package.mjs` PASS; zero non-historical "Import Agents" mentions remain in `AUTHORING-PROMPT.md` or `releases/1.0.0/docs-for-ai/`; zero `releases/v0.4`/`v0.5` path references remain outside `CHANGELOG.md`/`Plans/`.

### Added — roll-high, open-ended `d100-percentile` (Phase C, C1)

- `resolution.mode: "d100-percentile"` gains three optional sibling fields — `direction` (`"under"` default/back-compatible, or `"high"`), `bonusFormula` (descriptive only), and `openEnded` (`high`/`low` threshold + `cascadeCap` blocks, `firstRollOnly`) — extending the existing mode rather than adding a tenth one; no shipped ruleset uses `d100-percentile` today (`coc7e`/`gurps-lite` are `roll-under`), so this is zero-regression by census, and the pre-existing roll-under `rollD100()` body is preserved verbatim, byte-for-byte, under an `if (res.direction !== "high")` guard. The new `direction: "high"` path rolls d100 + a player-entered Bonus + Difficulty, with RMSS-canon open-ended cascades (high 96-100 inclusive adds and chains while the new face is also ≥96; low 01-05 subtracts, and — asymmetrically — the chain continues only on a HIGH follow-up ≥96, not on a further low one), guarded by the same `SAFETY_CAP = 100` hard bound `rollDicePoolSum`'s Wild Die explode uses. A natural (unmodified first-roll) 66 or 100 appends `um=66`/`um=100` to the `[mrr-roll: mode=d100-open ...]` tag for the GM to optionally treat as a Rolemaster-style unusual-event/unusual-success table row — the widget rolls the chain and reports the flag; it never picks a result band itself.
- Documented in `docs/AUTHORING-PHASE-6.md` §1 (new `d100-percentile` open-ended subsection) and `docs/AUTHORING.md`'s resolution-mode table. `releases/*/docs-for-ai/03-RULESET-SCHEMA.md` is a historical per-release snapshot with no live source file in this tree — not edited.

### Added — swipe-aware state-tag application (Phase 2, B2)

- Swiping an assistant reply keeps the same `data-message-id` (the engine stores the active variant as `messages.activeSwipeIndex` on one message row, not a new message), so the processed-message dedup set used to treat every swipe of one reply as "the same message" — a `[mrr-state:]` tag on swipe 1 of a reply already processed at swipe 0 was silently swallowed. The processed-set key is now `messageId + ":" + swipeIndex` when the swipe index is discoverable, falling back to plain `messageId` otherwise (network failure, or the message fell outside the fetched recent-window). A legacy plain-`messageId` entry from before this upgrade still suppresses every swipe of that message (accepted trade-off — re-applying an entire chat's history on every legacy entry would be worse than the rare case of a genuinely new swipe on an old message going unprocessed).
- **Corrected before release (round-5 adversarial review, FIX-2 — closes a replay bug):** `isMessageProcessed` now also does a prefix scan for `messageId + ":"` across the whole processed set, not just an exact plain-key or exact-composite-key match. Without it, a message already applied under `"M:0"` looked UN-processed to any observation where the swipe index couldn't be resolved that cycle (fetch failure, or the message fell outside the fetched `?limit=50` recent-window) — that observation would silently re-apply the message's mutations, replaying chat history. The same helper is now also the initial-page-load sweep gate in `watchChatMessages` (previously a raw, exact-plain-key-only check), closing the same hole there and eliminating the recurring per-observation swipe-index fetch it was triggering for already-processed messages.
- Swipe indices aren't present in the chat DOM, so they're looked up via `GET /chats/:chatId/messages?limit=50` (new `COUPLINGS.md` row 15) through the loader's attributed `apiFetch` wrapper — fetched only when a tag-bearing message needs processing, and coalesced per scan cycle: concurrent lookups share one in-flight request instead of issuing their own. The result is never cached beyond that one fetch and never polled.
- **Corrected before release (round-5 adversarial review, FIX-1 — the original claim here was wrong):** the narrator path's `applyStateTagsWithDedup` anchor is plain `messageId`, matching `extractRunAnchor` (the runs-poller's anchor, which has no swipe-index concept and never returns anything else) — NOT the composite `messageId:swipeIndex` key. An earlier build of this feature passed the composite key as the anchor, which desynced the two transports' anchors and let an identical `[mrr-state:]` tag arriving via both the narrator DOM and the runs poller apply TWICE instead of colliding. The composite key is still used, but only for the `processedMessageIds` entry (the whole-message+swipe reprocessing gate) — `applyStateTagsWithDedup` itself is unchanged, the anchor is opaque to it. Accepted consequence of sharing one plain-`messageId` anchor across swipes: identical-content tags on two different swipes of the same message now dedup to a single apply (protective — matches pre-B2 behavior); different-content tags on different swipes still each apply, stacking both swipes' mutations onto the sheet — an open design question logged for Corey, not resolved by this fix.
- The custom-agent runs poller (`pollCustomAgentRuns`) needed no anchor change — `extractRunAnchor` already only ever returned plain `messageId` — but its per-run-id dedup point stands as originally verified: a swipe/regeneration always creates a new agent-run row with a fresh id (`runId: newId()` per generation in `generate.routes.ts`), so the poller already treats each swipe's run as new work without any keying change.
- No RP/Game mode gate was added: `processChatMessage`/`watchChatMessages` have no mode branch today, so the fetch and composite keying apply uniformly in both modes rather than being restricted to Game mode.

### Added — swipe-revert mutation journal (Phase 2, B2-R; Corey, 2026-08-23: "if we can do it we should" — save-scumming sanctioned)

- Swiping a reply now reverts the sheet mutations the SWIPED-AWAY variant applied, then applies the new variant's — and swiping back restores exactly what was there before. A local-only journal (`LS_MUTATION_JOURNAL_PFX + chatId`, same per-browser observation class as the processed-message/run dedup sets — never server-synced) records each scalar mutation at the moment `applyStateMutation` writes it, as `{field, kind:"set", prev, next}` (attributes/skills/derived-stat numeric writes, and ruleset-declared states), keyed per `messageId:swipeIndex`.
- **⚠️ IMPORTANT LIMIT — READ BEFORE RELYING ON THIS FOR EXALTED-CLASS SYSTEMS (updated round 11):** swipe-revert covers numeric fields (attributes/skills/derived stats via the generic numeric path), ruleset-declared states, **(round 11, T2) typed-damage-TRACK hits** (Exalted's bashing/lethal/aggravated health boxes and any other `renderAs:"track"` derived stat — journaled as a per-cell snapshot under field `"__mrrTrack:" + trackName`, replayed via the internal `__mrrTrackRestore` sentinel; the legacy typed-counter (`sheet.track[trackName]`) is not separately snapshotted since it is fully re-derived from the cells snapshot by `syncTrackCellsToTyped`), and **(round 11, T2-EXPANDED) conditions add/remove** (journaled as a whole-array snapshot under field `"__mrrConditions"`, replayed via the internal `__mrrConditionsRestore` sentinel — a wholesale array replace, not a re-run of add/remove, so an unrelated later mutation in the same bucket can never be resurrected or re-deleted by an earlier entry's revert). It does **NOT** undo inventory adds/removes, background edits, intimacies, xp, or attunement/investiture/commitment writes — these remain array/object-shaped mutations that are not yet journaled at all, and keep stacking exactly as they did before this feature regardless of swipes. Flagged for extension, not solved this build — queued for a follow-up session.
- **Scope boundary (by design, not an oversight; updated round 11):** the journaled/reverted shapes are attributes, skills, derived stats via the generic numeric-field path, ruleset states, typed-damage-track per-cell hits (T2, an array snapshot under field `"__mrrTrack:" + trackName`), and conditions add/remove (T2-EXPANDED, a whole-array snapshot under field `"__mrrConditions"`). Inventory, backgrounds, intimacies, xp, and attunement/investiture/commitment remain array/object-shaped mutations that are NOT reverted on a swipe change and keep stacking exactly as they did before this feature (see the B2-R header comment at `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js`, in the module region after `saveProcessedMessageIds`, for the full accounting).
- Revert and swipe-back re-apply both go through `applyStateMutation` itself (constructing a synthetic delta/set write per journaled entry, with journaling suppressed for the duration) — never a second, parallel sheet-writer. Swipe-back deliberately bypasses the cross-transport dedup gate: since round 5's FIX-1 anchors both transports on plain `messageId`, an identical-content re-application through the normal path would be silently swallowed as "already applied" — the journal is the one sanctioned route around that gate, and only on an explicit swipe-back to a previously-reverted bucket.
- Runs-transport (`pollCustomAgentRuns`) mutations journal under the `messageId:swipeIndex` active at apply time, resolved via the same coalesced swipe-index fetch the narrator path uses; unresolvable runs journal under a plain-anchor bucket, which is never auto-reverted by the narrator's swipe-change detection — only superseded forward by later entries.
- **Known, accepted limits (documented, not solved):** manual sheet edits made between swipes can be clobbered by a `set`-revert or skewed by a `delta`-revert (delta-based math against the CURRENT value, not a snapshot, is intentional); the journal is per-browser, same as the processed-message sets — a second device won't revert what this browser applied. No per-chat localStorage cleanup lifecycle exists anywhere in this file for the processed-message/run sets this journal sits beside, so (consistent with them) journal entries do not get garbage-collected when a chat is deleted — not invented this round, per instruction.
- `MRR_STATE_TAG_VERSION = 1` added (declared beside `STATE_TAG_RE`/`STATE_KV_RE`) — stamps the `[mrr-state:]` grammar version per the outstanding B14 item in `COUPLINGS.md`'s Data-contract trailer. One constant, no behavior change yet.

### Fixed — round 11 live smoke-test fixes (Corey's morning findings, console-verified)

- **T1 — deterministic sole-writer AT THE TRANSPORT:** live evidence — a poll logged `applied 7/9 mutation(s)`: 7 managed agents ran on one generation and ≥3 non-mutator agents echoed the same essence-spend tag with different `reason=` wording, hashing to 3 different content sigs and triple-applying it (-15 instead of -5). `pollCustomAgentRuns` now resolves the active ruleset's managed state-mutator agent-config id (`mrrResolveMutatorConfigId`, matched via `settings.mrrAgentRole === "state-mutator"` — the installer stamps `mrrAgentRole` on every `additionalAgents[]` entry at install time, stable across rulesets, unlike display `name`) and applies/journals state tags ONLY from runs whose `agentConfigId` matches it. A non-mutator run carrying `[mrr-state:]` text gets one warn per offending agent name (`"sole-writer: ignoring state tags from non-mutator agent '<name>' — prompt leak"`) and its tags are neither applied nor journaled — but its run id is still marked processed so it's never re-scanned. If the mutator's config id can't be determined (managed install missing, `/agents` fetch fails, foreign/unmanaged agents present), the filter falls back to the pre-T1 unfiltered behavior with a one-time warn (`"sole-writer filter inactive — mutator id unknown"`) rather than silently dropping every run. See `COUPLINGS.md` row 2 for the confirmed engine row shape and full design note. **Judgment call (flagged, not authorized elsewhere):** the resolver deliberately omits the authorId check `findManagedAgent` requires — the poller has no bundle payload in scope, so any managed state-mutator for the active ruleset (regardless of which authorId's bundle installed it) is treated as the sole writer.
- **T2 — typed-damage tracks now covered by the swipe-revert journal:** live bug — 5 bashing applied via a typed-damage tag was not reverted on swipe (the documented un-journaled-branch gap). The typed-damage branch in `applyStateMutation` now journals a snapshot entry (`field: "__mrrTrack:" + trackName`, `prev`/`next` = a `.slice()` of `sheet.trackCells[trackName]` taken immediately before/after the branch's add/remove loops) via the same `mrrJournalMutation` chokepoint and `mrrCurrentApplySig` stamping the other three sites use. Only the per-cell array is snapshotted — `sheet.track[trackName]` (the legacy typed-counter) is fully re-derived from `trackCells` by `syncTrackCellsToTyped` and carries zero independent information, so it is not separately captured. Replay dispatches through a new internal sentinel, `attrs.__mrrTrackRestore = {trackId, snapshot}` (unreachable from tag text — `parseStateAttrs`'s regex only ever assigns STRING capture-group values, so even a tag literally typing `__mrrTrackRestore="..."` would fail the `typeof === "object"` check), which overwrites `sheet.trackCells[trackId]` from the snapshot and re-derives the legacy counter before finalizing. A multi-type tag (bashing+lethal in one `[mrr-state:]` tag) still produces one journal entry per `applyStateMutation` call, unchanged from round 10's 1:1 mapping. Scope-limit callouts in this file and `COUPLINGS.md` updated: typed-damage tracks are covered (and, per the T2-EXPANDED addendum below, conditions too); inventory/backgrounds/intimacies/xp/attunement/investiture/commitment remain out of scope.
- **T2-EXPANDED — conditions join the journal (addendum, same round):** Corey's D&D 5e baseline confirmed conditions apply but don't revert on swipe, and D&D leans on conditions (blinded, poisoned, prone, etc.) more than typed-damage tracks. The conditions branch in `applyStateMutation` mutates exactly ONE collection — `sheet.conditions`, a flat array of condition-name STRINGS (exact-string dedup on add, exact-string filter on remove) — so it now journals a whole-array snapshot (`field: "__mrrConditions"`, `prev`/`next` = a `.slice()` of the array before/after the branch's add/remove) via the same chokepoint, and replays through a new sentinel, `attrs.__mrrConditionsRestore = {snapshot}` (same "unreachable from tag text" proof as the other two sentinels), which wholesale-REPLACES `sheet.conditions` from the snapshot rather than re-running the original add/remove op — chosen specifically so an unrelated LATER mutation in the same journal bucket can never be resurrected or re-deleted by an earlier entry's revert (add/remove aren't safely invertible by re-running themselves backwards the way a scalar delta is). Scope-limit docs updated again: tracks + conditions covered; inventory/backgrounds/intimacies/xp/attunement/investiture/commitment remain out.
- **T3-EXPANDED — anti-placeholder hardening (addendum, same round):** live evidence from Corey's D&D 5e baseline (not exalted3e, which had partial hardening from Phase A but — flagged below — not complete either) showed the model emitting a literal `field="..."` (ellipsis, no attrs) three times and a literal `delta="-<rolled 2d10 total>"` angle-bracket template — both correctly dropped by existing validation, but the INFECTION SOURCE was the prompt's own grammar-teaching lines, which used the identical angle-bracket placeholder syntax (`field="<fieldName>"`, `delta="<+/-N>"`) to describe the grammar to a human reader — the model was echoing that syntax verbatim instead of treating it as descriptive metasyntax. Fixed in `agents/state-mutator.md` (shared baseline) and `rulesets/dnd5e/agents/state-mutator.md` (the live-reported ruleset): replaced every angle-bracket grammar line with worked, concrete examples (real field names, real computed numbers), and added an explicit "Output contract — no placeholders, ever" section naming and forbidding all three observed failure forms (letter placeholders, angle-bracket templates, ellipsis field names) plus a computation instruction ("read the `[dice: ...]` tags and narration, compute the FINAL integer yourself, then emit it — never emit a tag you can't fill with a real value"). Also added, once at each shared source, an explicit "NEVER emit `[mrr-state: ...]` tags" line for the non-mutator roles: `agents/combat-overseer.md`, `agents/context-fuser.md`, the `rulesets/rolemaster/agents/combat-overseer.md` per-ruleset override (a full override, not an inheriting copy, so it needed its own line), `tools/build-pre-input-transformer.mjs`'s single programmatic prompt generator (covers all 16 rulesets' pre-input-transformer agents from one source), and the three genuinely `phase: parallel` trackers (`rulesets/exalted3e/agents/anima-banner-monitor.md`, `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/charm-cooldown-tracker.md`, `rulesets/vtmv20/agents/blood-pool-tracker.md`). **Explicitly deferred, per this round's own cut-scope instruction:** `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md`'s Sorcery workflow section still has the same angle-bracket anti-pattern in its worked steps (`delta="-<spellCost>"`, `delta="-<currentMotes>"`) and its own "Tag forms" grammar line — it was judged live-hardened enough in practice (no live placeholder-leak report against it) to not be this round's fire, but it carries the identical structural risk and should get the same treatment in a follow-up pass; the `exwod`/`w20`/`vtmv20` state-mutator overrides and the eight `state-reminder`/`npc-bookkeeper`/`lore-query`/`combat-adjudicator` per-ruleset agent files were not audited or touched this round at all.
- **T2b — revert-trigger gap (root cause of the live "swipe didn't undo the damage" report):** `processChatMessage` used to early-return whenever the message's DOM text had no `[mrr-state:` substring — but runs-transport mutations (the State Mutator's real output, which never lands in chat DOM) journal under `msgId:swipeIndex` buckets for exactly those tag-less narrator messages. Swiping such a message never reached the swipe-change detection block, so revert never fired. Fixed by splitting the gate: a cheap in-memory scan, `mrrJournalHasBucketsFor(msgId)` (prefix-matches `mrrMutationJournal.buckets` keys — no localStorage read, no network call), now lets a tag-less message with journal buckets proceed to swipe-change detection (revert/reapply, no tag application); a message with neither tags nor buckets keeps the original zero-cost early return. **Observation-frequency choice:** reused the existing `mrrProcessedTextMemo` short-circuit for the bucket-only path instead of adding a second memo variable — "unchanged text means nothing new happened" holds identically for both paths, so one shared memo is the smaller correct diff. **Second, dependent bug found by the probe (not shipped separately — same fix, same commit):** the gate-split alone was not sufficient — `mrrMutationJournal.lastSeenIdx[msgId]` was previously stamped ONLY by `processChatMessage`'s own swipe-change block, so a message whose mutations arrived exclusively via the runs-transport poller had no `lastSeenIdx` entry for the swipe-change comparison to compare against (`priorIdx` read as `undefined`, so the `priorIdx !== undefined` guard silently skipped detection forever even after the gate reached it). `pollCustomAgentRuns` now stamps `mrrMutationJournal.lastSeenIdx[mid] = swipeMap[mid]` itself whenever a run's message id and swipe index are resolvable, mirroring what the narrator path already does whenever an index resolves. Caught by the T2b-i probe genuinely failing before this second fix was added — a real regression-simulation result, not a synthetic negative control.
- **T2c — static-cost semantics (Corey's ruling: "essence spends... are static between swipes... the mutator not firing off to revert/redo that expenditure is a good thing"):** before reverting an outgoing swipe's journal bucket, the incoming (new-swipe) tags — when the message is tag-bearing — are parsed and hashed through the exact normalize+sig pipeline `applyStateTagsWithDedup` uses (new helper `mrrComputeIncomingSigs`). `mrrRevertBucket` now takes an optional `skipSigs` set: an entry whose `entry.sig` is present there is left exactly as-is — not reverted, not cleared from the dedup-key set (`mrrClearDedupKeysForBucket` now also skips it), still counted applied/journaled — so the incoming swipe's identical tag is swallowed by the still-intact dedup key exactly as it would have been pre-B2-R, with zero intermediate revert/reapply flicker. Marked `entry.staticSkipped = true`, recomputed fresh on every revert call (same pattern as the existing `entry.revertFailed`) and excluded from `mrrReapplyBucket`'s forward replay for the same reason `revertFailed` entries are (never actually left the sheet). Entries with no matching incoming sig revert and clear as before. **Structural choice, explicitly flagged per the round's instruction (SUPERSEDED round 12, F2 — see below):** implemented the simpler `processChatMessage`-only variant (empty incoming-sig set for tag-less/runs-transport messages, which always fully reverts) rather than the fuller poller-deferred variant that would also compute incoming sigs from a NEW swipe's runs inside `pollCustomAgentRuns` itself — the latter would require duplicating swipe-change detection into the poller's already T1-modified apply loop, judged too invasive for this round's risk budget. **Accepted, documented churn case (SUPERSEDED round 12 — this was actually a live data-loss bug, not just churn):** a runs-delivered static cost (e.g. essence spent via the runs transport, not visible as DOM tag text) on a tag-less narrator message WILL revert-and-reapply on every swipe rather than staying static — net value is still correct (the amount ends up right), but there is a transient revert/reapply round-trip the tag-bearing path avoids. Flagged for a follow-up session if it proves visibly disruptive live. **It did — see round 12's F2 entry: the "simpler variant" left the poller with no swipe-transition awareness at all, and its much-higher polling frequency vs. the narrator's debounced DOM observer meant it routinely won the race to apply the new swipe's mutation BEFORE the narrator's revert of the old swipe ever fired, so the narrator's late revert then wiped the poller's freshly-applied value — net ZERO visible effect from either swipe, not just transient churn.**
- Probes added for T1/T2/T2b/T2c/T2-EXPANDED/T3-placeholder (see `b2r-revert-probes.mjs`, re-extracted to an `r11` fragment — 34 probes total in this file, up from 15 at round 10): T1 — non-mutator agent's tags ignored/unjournaled but its run marked processed with one warn per agent name (and not re-warned on a second offending run); mutator's own tags still apply; unfiltered-fallback path with its own warn-once; negative control (neuter the filter in a throwaway BUGGY fragment copy, confirm the leak reaches the sheet, delete the copy). T2 — live-shape 5-bashing apply/swipe-revert/swipe-back-reapply round trip; bashing+lethal multi-type case; a non-track mutation never journals a `__mrrTrack:` entry; negative control (remove the journal call, confirm the track survives a swipe unrevert). T2b — a runs-journaled bucket on a tag-less narrator message correctly triggers revert on swipe (this probe genuinely FAILED before the accompanying `lastSeenIdx` stamping fix was added — a real regression-simulation result, not synthetic). T2c — a same-sig entry survives a swipe with no revert while a different-sig entry in the same bucket reverts normally; a swipe-back after a static-skip does not double-apply; a D&D-shaped numeric-HP-via-runs, tag-less-narrator-message case reverts on swipe and re-damages on swipe-back. T2-EXPANDED — a condition add reverts on swipe and re-applies on swipe-back from the journal; a condition remove reverts (the condition comes back); a combined D&D-shaped case (condition + HP damage in one turn, via runs, tag-less narrator message) reverts BOTH on swipe; negative control (remove the conditions journal call, confirm the condition survives a swipe unrevert). T3-placeholder — all three observed template forms (`+N`, `-<rolled 2d10 total>`, `field="..."`) drop with a warn and apply 0 (pins the loader's PRE-EXISTING validation as correct; the prompt fix stops the model from emitting them in the first place).

### Fixed — round 12 retest findings (Corey live, logs 1787517870783 + 1787518829357)

- **F1 — rebind on ruleset activation (design gap, not a regression):** `reconcileActiveAgents`'s chat-scoped ruleset stamp existed only as a PASSIVE protection — a chat bound to a different ruleset was always skipped, with no rebind path anywhere in the file. Live evidence: `activated ruleset dnd5e on chat X` immediately followed by `bound to ruleset exalted3e — skipping`, whose own advice ("switch ruleset to update") was circular — activating a ruleset IS supposed to be the update mechanism. `reconcileActiveAgents` now takes a `rebind` argument: the PASSIVE call site (`watchRouteChanges`' chat-switch handler, no arg) is unchanged — still skips, since a passive chat switch is not a decision to rebind that chat. The ACTIVE call site (`init()`, immediately after `"activated ruleset ..."` — a deliberate user act) now calls `reconcileActiveAgents(true)`: when the chat's stamp differs from the newly-activated ruleset, it computes the OLD ruleset's managed agent ids and the NEW ruleset's managed agent ids from the SAME single `/agents` fetch already in flight, PATCHes `activeAgentIds = (existing MINUS old-managed) UNION new-managed` plus `mrrChatRulesetId` = the new ruleset and `enableAgents: true`, and logs `"rebound chat <id> from <old> to <new> (−N old agents, +M new)"`. Non-MRR / foreign agent ids in `activeAgentIds` (e.g. a "spotify" agent) are untouched either way — only ids belonging to the OLD ruleset's managed agents are removed.
- **F2 — transactional swipe-change (the wipe race; supersedes round 11's "simpler `processChatMessage`-only T2c variant"):** live evidence — D&D sheet bytes changed (mutations genuinely applied) yet the net visible effect was nothing, because round 11's poller had ZERO swipe-transition awareness of its own (T2b only added a bare `lastSeenIdx` stamp, no revert) — when the poller's far-more-frequent tick applied a NEW swipe's run before the narrator's own, much-later-debounced revert of the OLD bucket ever fired, the two writes landed out of order and the net result was wrong. Fixed by extracting ONE shared transition owner, `mrrHandleSwipeTransition(msgId, newIdx, incomingSigs)`: it no-ops when `lastSeenIdx` is missing or already equals `newIdx`; otherwise it reverts the old bucket using T2c's static-skip semantics (`incomingSigs` known at call time), handles a swipe-back reapply from the journal if the arriving bucket was previously reverted, and stamps `lastSeenIdx = newIdx` — all in one synchronous function body, no `await`/`.then()` anywhere inside it. That synchronicity is the whole safety mechanism: JS's single-threaded run-to-completion semantics mean a second caller racing the SAME transition can only ever observe this function's state strictly before it starts or strictly after it returns, never mid-way, so the stamp (the last statement in the body) makes a second call for the same msgId/newIdx a safe no-op regardless of which transport discovers the transition first. Three call sites, all calling it BEFORE their own apply: (a) `pollCustomAgentRuns`'s apply loop, per tag-bearing row, with that row's own parsed-tag sigs — the new call site that closes the wipe, now living INSIDE the same per-row try/catch as the apply itself (a bug caught by probe R13 regressing: `mrrComputeIncomingSigs` walks `state.ruleset.derivedStats` and can throw on malformed data, and a throw outside the try would abort the row silently instead of going through fix 7's existing honest throw-containment); (b) `processChatMessage`'s tag-bearing path, with the parsed DOM tags' sigs; (c) `processChatMessage`'s T2b tag-less/bucket-only path, with an empty sig set. Round 11's separate ad-hoc stamping sites in both callers are fully absorbed into this one function — there is now exactly one place a swipe transition is ever handled.
- **F3 — data sweep, confirmed leak source + finished round-11's deferred prompt list:** the leaking agent named in T1's live warn, "MRR: Dungeons & Dragons 5th Edition Ruleset Helper", is `bundle.gmAgent` — built from `rulesets/dnd5e/gm-agent.md` (`"MRR: " + gmAgent.name`, confirmed at the install-time name-prefix call site) via `build-bundle.mjs`'s `extractPromptBlock`, which pulls ONLY the first ` ```text ... ``` ` fenced block. That fenced block itself does NOT literally instruct tag emission — but the file's own `## State-mutator tags — XP and attunement` section, sitting BELOW the fence, does contain literal `[mrr-state: field="xp" ...]` example syntax, and the file's own top-of-file instruction ("Paste the contents below...") does not make clear that "below" means "the fenced block only," not "the rest of the file too." Fixed: the top-of-file instruction now explicitly says paste ONLY the fenced block and names this round's leak as the reason why; the misleading section is retitled `## Reference only — NOT part of this agent's prompt` with an explicit warning against pasting it; and — belt-and-suspenders, in case any other copy-paste path exists — the actual fenced prompt now also carries an explicit "NEVER emit `[mrr-state: ...]` tags yourself" line. **Finished round-11's deferred list:** `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md`'s "Tag forms" grammar line and its Sorcery-workflow's `delta="-<spellCost>"` / `delta="-<currentMotes>"` steps rewritten with worked concrete examples plus explicit "substitute the real number, never the placeholder" instructions (kept the step-by-step structure since spell name/cost genuinely vary per real spell — only the NUMERIC placeholders were hardened, `<Spell Name>` stays as an explicit string-substitution slot). `rulesets/exwod/agents/state-mutator.md`, `rulesets/w20/agents/state-mutator.md`, `rulesets/vtmv20/agents/state-mutator.md` — all three "field map" quick-reference tables rewritten from `<+/-N>`/`<N>`/enum-choice placeholders to worked concrete tags. The 8 files: `rulesets/{exwod,w20}/agents/{state-reminder,lore-query,combat-adjudicator}.md` (6 files) get the standard "never emit tags" hardening line; `rulesets/{exwod,w20}/agents/npc-bookkeeper.md` (2 files) additionally had their OWN prompts literally instructing `"emit a [mrr-state: ...] tag if that NPC has its own sheet"` — corrected to never emit (any such tag would be dropped by T1's sole-writer filter anyway, since npc-bookkeeper is not the state-mutator role — the old instruction wasted output on something structurally guaranteed to fail).
- **Severe pre-existing bug found and fixed while verifying the exalted3e sorcery-workflow prompt fix actually shipped: `extractPromptBlock`'s fence-matching was silently truncating `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md` to 1429 of its ~19.5KB source.** Both `tools/build-bundle.mjs` and `tools/build-agents.mjs` carry independent copies of `extractPromptBlock`, matching the FIRST ` ```text ... ``` ` fenced block via a non-greedy regex. That file's own "Output format you MUST produce" section nests an illustrative bare ` ``` ... ``` ` example block INSIDE the outer fence (to show the narrator exactly what a "NARRATOR TAG DIRECTIVE" looks like) — the non-greedy match stopped at that INNER closing fence, not the real outer one 240+ lines later, so the actual shipped `promptTemplate` for exalted3e's state-mutator agent has ALWAYS ended right after "Use ONLY the directive form below" — losing the FORBIDDEN field names list, the entire field vocabulary ("use these EXACT names"), the whole Sorcery casting workflow (including both this round's AND round 11's fixes to it), the conditions vocabulary, the inventory schema, the Rules section, and every worked example. This was invisible from reading the source file alone — only checking the BUILT bundle's actual `promptTemplate` length caught it. Root cause is a CommonMark fencing mismatch, not the regex being "wrong" in isolation: a 3-backtick fence cannot safely contain a 3-backtick example. Fixed at the source (both `extractPromptBlock` copies now try a 4-backtick outer-fence match FIRST, falling back to the original 3-backtick match unchanged for every other file — CommonMark's own rule that a fence can only be closed by a fence of the same character and >= length) and at the one file that needed it (`rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md`'s outer fence changed from ` ```text`/` ``` ` to ` ````text`/` ```` `, inner example fence left as plain 3-backtick, now correctly treated as literal text inside the wider outer fence). Confirmed via the built bundle: `promptTemplate` length went from 1429 to 19471 characters, `"Step 3"`, `delta="-15"`, and `delta="-11"` (this round's Sorcery-workflow worked examples) now present. **Every ruleset's `gm-agent.md`/`agents/*.md` was swept for the same nested-fence pattern (only this one file had it) — see the DEFERRED gm-agent.md finding below for the separate, still-open question of whether any of the OTHER files' fenced content is itself risky, which is unrelated to this truncation bug.**
- **New finding, flagged and explicitly DEFERRED (out of this round's contained scope, not silently dropped):** while tracing the confirmed dnd5e leak, found that 10 of 16 rulesets' `gm-agent.md` files (`coc7e`, `exalted3e`, `exwod`, `gurps-lite`, `pathfinder2e`, `rolemaster`, `stewpot`, `trophy-dark`, `vtmv20`, `w20`) have literal `[mrr-state: ...]` tag syntax INSIDE the actual fenced prompt block that gets built into `bundle.gmAgent.promptTemplate` and sent to the live agent (unlike dnd5e, where the risky content sat below the fence). For `exalted3e` and `rolemaster` this is BY DESIGN — their GM agent is a `context_injection` helper that teaches the real narrator to echo the State Mutator's "NARRATOR TAG DIRECTIVE" verbatim in its own visible reply, a documented architecture (see `agents/state-mutator.md`'s "Critical architecture" section) that this round did not audit for correctness. For the other 8, whether the fenced tag examples are load-bearing instruction or accidental leak risk (same class as the confirmed dnd5e bug, just baked into the sent prompt instead of copy-paste-dependent) was NOT determined this round — flagged for a dedicated future pass rather than rushed alongside F1/F2/F3's already-large diff.
- Probes added for F1/F2 in `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` (re-extracted to an `r12` fragment, 51 pieces — adds `reconcileActiveAgents` from a completely different file region and the new `mrrHandleSwipeTransition`; 41 probes total, up from 34 at round 11's close): F1a — active-call (`rebind=true`) on a stamped-other-ruleset chat produces the correct PATCH (`activeAgentIds` = existing-minus-old-managed union new-managed, `mrrChatRulesetId` updated) and the correctly-worded `−N/+M` rebind log. F1b — passive call (no arg) on the same chat still skips, no PATCH — regression guard on the pre-existing protective behavior. F2-poller-first — the harness's `log()` stub was upgraded to capture into a `logs` array (previously a no-op) so F1's log-message assertions are possible; the wipe/stacking race is fixed: poller-applied-first still nets the new swipe's clean effect, narrator catch-up on the same transition is a safe no-op. A throwaway BUGGY fragment copy with ONLY the poller's `mrrHandleSwipeTransition` call reverted to round 11's bare stamp was run against the identical sequence before this probe was finalized and confirmed the pre-F2 defect directly: net HP landed at the STACKED value (10-5-3=2) instead of the correct revert-then-apply value (10-3=7) — a genuine, code-verified defect in the pre-F2 poller (silent stacking, not byte-exact "wipe to baseline"; the exact live interleaving behind Corey's "net nothing visible" report depends on microtask-queue ordering this Node harness can't replay from the referenced console logs alone, flagged honestly in the probe's own header comment rather than overclaiming an exact match). F2-DOM-first — narrator-discovered transitions still work correctly through the shared function (regression guard). F2-static-via-poller — a static-cost mutation delivered BY THE POLLER (not DOM tags) now also gets T2c's static-skip treatment, previously only reachable from the narrator path. F2-poller-roundtrip — A→B→A entirely via the runs/poller transport, no DOM tags anywhere, restores A's exact journaled value on swipe-back.

### Fixed — round 13 T5 persistence fixes (design authority: Plans/2026-08-22 plan §10, "T4+T5 INVESTIGATION COMPLETE" row, git-proven)

- **T5-a — `mergeSheet` whitelist dedup (07-03 audit's union spec):** `mergeSheet` whitelisted `sectionCollapse`, `conditions`, `abilityCategoryScores`, `customAbilityCategories`, and `trackCells` TWICE each — a leftover from an earlier "PERSISTENCE WHITELIST — SYSTEMIC RULE" documentation pass that re-added five fields without removing their now-stale originals, so the LATER block always silently won. Two of the five later blocks were live, git-confirmed data-loss bugs: `trackCells`'s later block rebuilt the array via `(typeof v === "string" && v) ? v : null`, silently converting every object-shaped typed-damage cell to `null` on EVERY reload (directly contradicting that very block's own documented shape, and undoing round 11's T2 typed-damage journaling on the next page load); `abilityCategoryScores`'s later block additionally clamped to `Math.min(10, ...)`, a V20-disciplines-shaped ceiling silently wrong for any other ruleset's category score above 10. Collapsed to ONE block per field, keeping the union of validation intent per the 07-03 audit's own fix spec: `trackCells` keeps the earlier, object-preserving filter; `abilityCategoryScores` keeps the earlier, no-ceiling (floor-at-0-only) block; `conditions` keeps the WIDER acceptance (no length cap — the 64-char cap in the other, now-removed block was arbitrary and matched no ruleset's schema); `sectionCollapse` and `customAbilityCategories` collapse trivially (byte-for-byte identical validation logic in both blocks, confirmed by diff before removal).
- **T5-b — version-stamped sheet migration:** `sheet._schemaVersion` (integer, current = `MRR_SHEET_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2`) is now stamped on every `saveSheet` call. `loadSheet` runs a new `mrrMigrateSheet(parsed, ruleset)` — gated by `mrrMigrateIfNeeded` so it fires AT MOST ONCE per sheet, for sheets whose stamp is missing or older — on the RAW PARSED sheet BEFORE `mergeSheet(blankSheet(ruleset), parsed)`, at both `loadSheet` call sites (the primary character-library key and the legacy chat-scoped fallback). This exists because `mergeSheet`'s per-field whitelist can only copy a value within the SAME top-level bucket by name match — it structurally cannot see a value that used to live under a DIFFERENT key/bucket before a ruleset schema change. Two explicit, git-proven migration entries: **M1** (`a1c21ef`, dnd5e) — Level moved `attributes[]` → `derivedStats[]`; a pre-move sheet's orphaned `sheet.attributes.Level` copies into `sheet.derived.Level`, but ONLY when the current ruleset has no "Level" attribute and DOES declare a "Level" derived stat, and NEVER when `derived.Level` already holds a non-default value (a real value written under the current schema, by a prior migration run or actual play, is never clobbered by a stale attribute). **M2** (`ec51408`, exalted3e) — written GENERICALLY, not exalted-specific, since it's the class fix for ANY stateName-bound resource on ANY ruleset: for every resource the CURRENT ruleset declares with a `stateName`, if the parsed sheet has a numeric `resources[id].current` and no existing `derived[stateName]`, the orphaned value copies across. Migration writes land on `parsed` directly, so the immediately-following `mergeSheet` call picks up the migrated location exactly like any other field, and the very next `saveSheet` persists the migrated shape (with the stamp) permanently.
- **T5-c — dnd5e HP unification + exalted3e vestigial resource removal:** `rulesets/dnd5e/ruleset.json`'s `hit-points` resource gains `stateName: "Hit Points"` (mirroring exalted3e's `essence`/motes pattern exactly) and drops the hardcoded `max: 8` / `current: 8` literals — unifying the two separate HP displays T4 identified (the resources-window bar and the derived-stat "Hit Points" bar) onto the SAME `state.sheet.derived["Hit Points"]` value. **`mrrResolveResourceMax` finding (explicitly asked for, reported honestly):** this function did NOT already defer to `derivedMax` for a stateName-bound resource — it always resolved `max` from the resource's OWN declared field (number/formula), completely independent of stateName; only `current` had stateName awareness (via `mrrGetResourceCurrent`). Wired it in: `mrrResolveResourceMax` now mirrors `mrrP3ComputeBarMax`'s exact fallback chain (the derived-bar renderer) for a stateName-bound resource — `state.sheet.derivedMax[stateName]` (the SAME user-editable key the derived bar's own max-override UI writes to) wins first, then the resource's own declared `max` if still present (unchanged behavior for any resource keeping one), then — only if neither exists — `Math.max(DEFAULT_BAR_MAX, current)`, the same never-show-a-nonsensical-0-max fallback the derived bar uses. Resources without a `stateName` are completely unaffected. **Known, reported gap, out of T5-c's literal scope:** `blankSheet`'s `derivedStats` loop seeds `derived["Hit Points"] = 0` (no `default` declared on that derived stat) BEFORE the `resources[]` loop's own Phase-6 seed ever runs — and that seed loop explicitly skips when the key already exists (`if (r.stateName in s.derived) return;`) — so a brand-new dnd5e sheet starts at 0 HP either way, whether or not `current: 8` is kept on the resource; this pre-existing key-claiming-order asymmetry is unrelated to T5-c's stateName binding and was not touched this round. **`health-track` usage proof (exalted3e):** `rg`'d the literal quoted id `"health-track"` across the whole loader — zero matches; the resource dispatches purely generically via `type: "custom"` + `rendererConfig.component: "exalted-health-track"`, and that renderer (`mrr_resourceRenderers["exalted-health-track"]`) builds a SYNTHETIC derived-stat object from `rendererConfig` and hands it to `mrrP3RenderDerivedTrack` — the SAME renderer the real `derivedStats[]` "Health Track" entry uses — meaning `state.sheet.resources["health-track"]` (the actual storage key) was never read or written at all; both UI cards read/write the SAME shared `trackCells["Health Track"]` state via two different rendering paths. This IS exalted3e's own instance of the T4 dual-declaration antipattern (a duplicate health-track card in the Resources cluster on top of the real one in Derived) — removed the now-proven-dead `resources[]` entry from `rulesets/exalted3e/ruleset.json`; the `exalted-health-track` renderer registration itself is untouched shared loader code (harmless if no ruleset references it). Both bundles rebuilt.
- **T5-d — saveSheet destructive-write guard (conservative):** targets the T4+T5 investigation's flagged hypothesis — a failed/partial load path can leave `state.sheet` as a freshly-built `blankSheet()`, and the very next autosave/debounced `saveSheet` call would silently replace good, real player data with nothing. `saveSheet` now refuses to write when (a) the in-memory sheet is genuinely PRISTINE-BLANK for the active ruleset AND (b) the stored value at that key parses and is itself NOT pristine-blank. **Definition chosen (the PRIMARY option from the spec, not the cheaper proxy):** a real recursive structural deep-equal (`mrrDeepEqual`, new — ES5-safe, no library dependency) against `blankSheet(state.ruleset)`, modulo the `_schemaVersion` stamp (stripped from a shallow copy before comparing). Chosen over a JSON.stringify comparison specifically because key INSERTION ORDER can legitimately differ between a freshly-built `blankSheet()` and a sheet that has passed through `mergeSheet`/migration — two structurally-identical objects with keys in a different order stringify to different strings, which would make the guard's blank-detection unreliable (false negatives letting the exact hazard through). Warns once per character-library key (`mrrSaveSheetGuardWarned`) rather than spamming on every debounced retry of a persistently-broken load path.
- Probes added for T5-a/T5-b/T5-d (new file: `t5-probes.mjs`, 18-piece fresh extraction — `loadSheet`/`saveSheet`/`blankSheet`/`mergeSheet`/`mrrMigrateSheet`/`mrrMigrateIfNeeded`/`mrrDeepEqual`/`mrrIsPristineBlankSheet` plus dependencies; 15 probes, joining the harness suite alongside the existing six). T5-a: object-shaped trackCells survive a load/merge round-trip + a genuine regression-simulation negative control (a throwaway BUGGY copy with the OLD string-rebuilding trackCells block restored fails the SAME probe, confirming it's non-vacuous); category score 15 survives with no ceiling, floor-at-0 regression guard; conditions >64 chars survive. T5-b: pre-version dnd5e-shaped sheet with `attributes.Level=10` migrates to `derived.Level`; an existing non-default `derived.Level` is never clobbered; orphaned `resources[id].current` migrates to `derived[stateName]` + a genuine regression-simulation negative control (M2's block stripped from a throwaway BUGGY copy — the value falls back to the ruleset-default seed instead of the saved value, confirming the probe is non-vacuous); an already-migrated sheet (stamped at current version) is left untouched even when it ALSO carries stale cross-bucket data, proving the version GATE itself does real work, not just the migration logic; `_schemaVersion` stamped on every save. T5-d: blank-over-good refused with exactly one warn (and no repeat warn on a second attempt); good-over-good proceeds and the new value actually lands; blank-over-blank proceeds with no false-positive refusal.

### Fixed — round 14 retest 3: swipe total silence (Task A) + engine staging semantics (Task B)

- **Task A — root cause of "swipes produce total silence" (no poller apply, no transition log, nothing):** `watchChatMessages`' `MutationObserver` was configured `{ childList: true, subtree: true }` with NO `characterData` — a deliberate, documented round-of-history choice, reasoned defensively against a generic "heavy observers" pitfall warning but never actually verified against this engine's real DOM behavior. Verified this round against `~/Marinara-Engine` client source: the message list renders `<ChatMessage key={msg.id} .../>` (`ChatRoleplaySurface.tsx`) — the React key is the message's stable id, NOT id+swipeIndex, so a swipe (new `activeSwipeIndex`/content, same message id) re-renders the SAME component instance in place rather than remounting it; React's DOM reconciler commits a text-only content change by writing directly to the existing text node's `.nodeValue` (a characterData mutation), never by removing/inserting a text node (a childList mutation). A childList-only observer is therefore architecturally blind to exactly this case — the DOM genuinely changes, the observer never fires, `schedule()` never runs, `processChatMessage` never runs, hence total silence with no error to even hint at the cause. **Second, dependent bug found while fixing the first:** even with `characterData: true` added, `findParentMessage(el)` still failed — a characterData `MutationRecord`'s `.target` is the TEXT NODE itself (`nodeType` 3), and the function's `while (el.nodeType === 1)` guard rejected it on the very first check, returning `null` unconditionally. Fixed both: `obs.observe(document.body, { childList: true, subtree: true, characterData: true })`, and `findParentMessage` now hops to `el.parentElement` first for any non-element starting node before walking up exactly as before. **The historical keystroke-storm concern does not apply here:** grepped the ENTIRE `~/Marinara-Engine` client package for a real contentEditable-based editor (React `contentEditable` prop, or a rich-text lib — Lexical/Slate/TipTap/Quill/ProseMirror) and found none; the two lowercase `"contenteditable"` string hits that DO exist are click-target/touch-gesture IGNORE-LIST selectors (`ChatMessage.tsx`'s quick-edit click guard, `ChatArea.tsx`'s swipe-gesture guard), not an actual editable region — the chat composer is a plain textarea/input, whose value changes are a JS property never reflected as DOM text-node mutations at all, so MutationObserver's characterData channel is silent for it regardless; and even for some future characterData-triggering input elsewhere on the page, `findParentMessage`'s own upward walk already scopes every observed mutation to ones landing inside a tagged `[data-message-id]` element, exactly as it already does for childList noise today. Added one cheap, permanent diagnostic log — `"swipe-detect: msgId=<id> buckets=<bool> idx=<n|null>"` — at the top of `processChatMessage`'s swipe-index-resolved callback, firing for every observation that reaches that point (proving the whole observer→schedule→processChatMessage→resolveSwipeIndexForMessage chain worked) with the three facts needed to read the next live log conclusively either way.
