- **Task B — engine staging semantics, precisely verified (not assumed) against `~/Marinara-Engine` source:** `generate.routes.ts:929` `moveToActiveAgentRuns(messageId, swipeIndex)` — called at `:7868`/`:10152`, the INSTANT the assistant message becomes visible (`assistant_message_ready` sent), not at "generation end" broadly — moves the request's generation record from a single-slot blocking map (`activeGenerations`) into a per-chat Set (`activeAgentRuns`) purely so a new turn/swipe isn't blocked on background post-processing finishing. **This is in-memory generation-slot bookkeeping only — it does NOT gate when a run becomes visible to our poller.** The actual DB write we read (`agentsStore.saveRun`, `agents.storage.ts:317`) is called synchronously at `generate.routes.ts:8492`, immediately upon each post-processing agent's own completion, in the SAME request continuation — there is no "staged until next-turn-commit" delay for write visibility; the round-13/14 investigation's original framing ("only moved to the queryable store at next-turn commit") does not hold as a general statement and is corrected in `COUPLINGS.md` row 2. What DOES happen at next-turn commit (`:986-1000`, gated by an ACTUAL new user-authored message, not every regenerate) is a cancellation sweep — `run.abortController.abort()` for any `activeAgentRuns` entry whose `(messageId, swipeIndex)` no longer matches the message's current active swipe — but it's coarser than a hard kill: `executeAgent` (`agent-executor.ts:723`) takes no `AbortSignal` at all, and the post-processing save loop has no per-iteration aborted-check, so an agent already executing or already reaching `saveRun` when the abort fires completes and saves regardless; the signal is only honored at a few coarse checkpoints. Net effect: usually less abandoned-swipe noise to dedup (good, as hypothesized), not a hard guarantee. A swipe's fresh generation DOES run agents — confirmed, same pipeline, no special-casing. **Genuine, unfixable-from-our-side gap found during verification:** `agent_runs`' schema (`db/schema/agents.ts:22-37`) has NO swipe-index column, only `messageId` — the engine internally pins which swipe a slow agent's result belongs to (captured once, specifically so a mid-flight swipe doesn't misattribute server-side) but never persists that pin onto the row we read. Our `swipeMap`-at-apply-time resolution already handles the common, sane case correctly (a run finishing late while the CURRENT swipe is still the one it was computed for applies cleanly, no misattribution, since the shared transition function already no-ops when `priorIdx === newIdx`); the unresolvable case (a run computed for a swipe since abandoned via regenerate-to-regenerate, which engine-side cancellation doesn't catch because it's gated to real user turns) has no fix available to us and is documented as an accepted risk. Written into `COUPLINGS.md` row 2 (which also corrects a stale round-11 addendum describing the pre-round-12 bare `lastSeenIdx` stamp mechanism F2 superseded) and the poller's own header comment in the loader.
- Probes added: `t14-observer-probes.mjs` (new file, 6 probes) — extracts the CURRENT `findParentMessage` and the observer's exact dispatch-loop body, plus (via `git show HEAD:...`, not hand-typed) the actual pre-round-14 `findParentMessage` for a genuine regression simulation: the OLD function returns `null` for a characterData record's text-node target (reproducing total silence, both in isolation and through the full dispatch loop) while the NEW one correctly resolves it and reaches `schedule()`; regression guards confirm element-node targets (existing childList case) and unrelated-page text nodes still resolve exactly as before. `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` gains one Task-B(b) probe (42 total, up from 41): a run arriving on a later poll cycle for a swipe that never changed applies cleanly into that still-current bucket with no spurious revert, `lastSeenIdx` and the journal bucket both landing correctly.

### Fixed — round 15 URGENT hotfix: "regenerate does nothing" (round-14 characterData regression)

- **Confirmed root cause (Corey live, tree `27110a50`): `hideStateTagsInElement`'s `node.innerHTML = wrapped` rewrite, now reachable off a characterData-only observation (round 14), detaches whatever text node React is actively streaming into — React's subsequent `.nodeValue` writes land on the orphaned node, so the visible message text freezes while generation silently continues server-side.** `hideStateTagsInElement` only fires when a COMPLETE `[mrr-state: ...]` tag is present and not yet wrapped — so the freeze is specifically gated to tag-bearing regenerations, matching the live report exactly (mutator fires, tags are in play). The debounce (`schedule()`'s monotonic-token pattern, 1500ms) DOES already reset per additional mutation of any origin — confirmed by reading it, no change needed there, already exceeds the requested ≥800ms quiescence bar — but "quiet for 1500ms" is not the same guarantee as "the stream is actually finished": a real generation can have a natural pause ≥1500ms mid-stream (between sub-agent phases, thinking segments, etc), and round 14 made EVERY streamed token (not just childList-shaped ones) count toward resetting that timer, making such a quiet-but-not-done window reachable in a way the original childList-only design never exposed to `hideStateTagsInElement`.
- **Fix (minimal, surgical, per spec):** mutation origin is now tracked through the whole pipeline. The observer callback checks each `MutationRecord.type`; `schedule(msgEl, isChildListOrigin)` OR-merges that flag into a new per-message `pendingOrigin` map across the debounce window (if ANY observation in the window was childList, the whole window counts as childList — `addedNodes`-driven `schedule()` calls are always childList-origin, since `addedNodes` only exists on childList records); when the debounce timer fires, the merged flag is read, cleared, and passed as `processChatMessage(msgEl, isChildListOrigin)`'s new second argument. `hideStateTagsInElement(node)` at the tail of `processChatMessage` is now gated `if (isChildListOrigin)`. Swipe-detection (bucket check, `resolveSwipeIndexForMessage`, `mrrHandleSwipeTransition`, tag application) is completely UNCHANGED and still runs for both origins — only the destructive DOM rewrite is now childList-gated, exactly as designed. The initial-DOM-sweep `schedule()` call (extension load, static already-rendered messages) passes `true` explicitly to keep its pre-round-14 behavior; its OTHER call path (`hideStateTagsInElement(el)` for already-processed messages) was already a direct, unconditional call outside the debounce and is untouched.
- Probe added: `t15-freeze-probes.mjs` (new file, 4 probes). Reproduces the freeze mechanism mechanically — a mock message node whose `innerHTML` setter detaches a held "React text node" reference exactly like real DOM childList replacement does — confirming `hideStateTagsInElement`'s unconditional call really does detach the reference and really does freeze subsequent stream writes from reaching the live DOM. Confirms the fix: a characterData-only observation (`isChildListOrigin=false`) never calls `hideStateTagsInElement` at all — DOM untouched, stream stays live. Regression guard: a childList-origin observation (`isChildListOrigin=true`) still wraps the tag normally. Genuine regression-simulation negative control: the OLD (pre-round-15, via `git show HEAD:...`, not hand-typed) unconditional call site, run through the identical characterData-only scenario, reproduces the freeze — confirming the probe suite is non-vacuous.

### Fixed — round 23: EVERY MUTATION APPLIED TWICE — bucket-level channel claim (sole-writer per turn)

- **Live symptom (Corey, console log EX3_2052, 2026-08-23): "add 3 Bashing" put 6 on the track; "remove 5 motes" took 10.** Same shape on two consecutive turns, so not a one-off. The log names both culprits landing on ONE turn with nothing suppressed: `runs-poller: run ugXFf1z7e8gBkUmEigSaT mid=t-cUg1IZhGPAhUAF-ReI8 → anchor=Uqvcjd9_eYMp6Vo8dEU8D (resolved) journalKey=Uqvcjd9_eYMp6Vo8dEU8D:0`, then `runs-poller: applied 3/3 mutation(s) from custom-agent runs`, then later `state-mutator: applied 3/3 mutation(s) from message Uqvcjd9_eYMp6Vo8dEU8D:0`. Identical anchor, identical journal bucket, six applications, zero dedup collisions.
- **Root cause — the cross-transport dedup gate cannot structurally catch this, and never could.** `appliedMutationKeys` is keyed `anchorId::contentSig::occIdx`, and `mutationContentSig` serializes **every** parsed attr — including the free-text `reason=` the agent wrote and the value FORM it chose (`delta="+3"` vs `set="6"` vs `current="6"`). The GM narrator's inline `[mrr-state:]` tag and the State Mutator agent's run tag describe the same semantic mutation but are authored by **two different models on two different prompts**, so their reasons (and often their value forms) differ → different sig → different key → both apply. Round 18's BUG-2 block already documented the seam ("dedup gate only catches EXACT duplicate content"); it had simply never been reachable, because until round 20 fixed the read path the GM could not see the sheet and therefore never emitted write tags at all. Making the read path live is what armed it.
- **Why not fix it by matching content.** Free-text reasons cannot be normalized, and delta-vs-absolute cannot be equated without reading current state — which is itself mid-mutation at exactly that moment. Any string-level reconciliation here is a guess dressed as a comparison.
- **Fix — sole-writer per journal bucket, first-writer-wins.** New per-chat in-memory `mrrBucketChannelClaims` (bucket key → `"dom"` | `"poller"`). `applyStateTagsWithDedup(tags, anchorId, journalKey, channel)` takes a fourth argument naming the transport; at entry it computes `bucketKey = journalKey || anchorId` and, if that bucket is already claimed by the OTHER channel, suppresses the **entire** call — applies nothing, returns 0, logs one `cross-channel: bucket <key> already applied by <other> — suppressing <N> tag(s) from <channel>` line. Otherwise it proceeds exactly as before (per-sig exact dedup completely unchanged) and claims the bucket in its `finally`. Bucket-level rather than sig-level is the whole point: the gate does not care what the two models wrote, only that one turn's writes come from one transport.
- **Call sites (both, exhaustive — these are the only two in the file).** `processChatMessage`'s narrator apply passes `"dom"`; `pollCustomAgentRuns`'s per-row apply passes `"poller"`.
- **Lifecycle wiring, so the claim can never outlive its bucket.** Reset at both `loadProcessedMessageIds` chokepoints (null-chatId and new-chat), matching `appliedMutationKeys` exactly. **Deleted on revert** — `mrrRevertBucket` drops the claim in the same guarded block that calls `mrrClearDedupKeysForBucket`, and for the same reason: without it, a swipe-back whose tags arrive via the other transport would hit the sole-writer gate and silently apply nothing. **Re-set after a journal reapply** — the `reappliedFromJournal` paths bypass `applyStateTagsWithDedup` entirely, so both of them (`processChatMessage` → `"dom"`, and the poller's own `mrrHandleSwipeTransition` call → `"poller"`) re-claim the reapplied bucket explicitly, keeping a late echo from the other channel locked out.
- **Accepted trade-off, instrumented rather than hidden.** If the second channel genuinely carried a DIFFERENT mutation (a real extra write, not an echo), suppression loses it. The two cases are indistinguishable by content, so instead of guessing, a suppressed call runs its tags through the same normalize+sig pipeline and warns when any `anchor::sig::occIdx` is absent from `appliedMutationKeys` — naming the bucket and the unmatched sigs (truncated to 120 chars). This is deliberate live telemetry: if that warn ever appears in a real log, the decision gets re-opened with data behind it. The check is diagnostic-only and swallows its own throws (`normalizeStateAttrs` can throw on malformed ruleset data) so suppression stands regardless.
- **Known gap, documented not patched.** The claim keys on the bucket, so the channels only collide when they resolve the SAME bucket key. If the poller cannot resolve a swipe index it falls back to a plain-msgId bucket while the DOM path uses `msgId:idx` — different keys, no suppression. That is the same anchor-resolution failure the round-18/19 backstop already warns loudly about, and it does not get a second, quieter workaround here.
- **Invariants preserved.** With `MRR_RUNS_POLLER_MODE` `"off"` only the DOM channel ever writes, so claims are populated but the channel always matches — behavior byte-identical to pre-round-23. Regenerated swipes (no run exists, DOM-only) land on a fresh `msgId:newIdx` bucket that is unclaimed, so the narrator applies normally. Loader-only: no bundle data touched.

### Fixed — round 24: CHARACTER-SHEET BLEED ACROSS RULESETS on chat switch + the ruleset never followed the chat

- **Live symptom (Corey, 2026-08-23): switching between a D&D chat and an Exalted chat "resets" the character sheet — and the reset has two distinct shapes.** Same-named fields take the OTHER ruleset's stored number (Strength on the Exalted sheet shows the D&D character's Strength); fields with no analog in the other system floor to their blank minimum (Exalted **Perception → 1**, **Brawl → 0**). Third report, same root: the active ruleset does not follow the chat — the user has to switch rulesets by hand every time.
- **Root cause — three cooperating defects, all code-verified.** (1) `state.ruleset` has **exactly one** assignment site in the whole loader — `init()`, fed by the global LS key `LS_RULESET`. **Auto-switch on chat enter was never built.** Commit `fd6d755` (2026-05-16) added the per-chat metadata stamp `mrrChatRulesetId`, but only as AGENT-pollution protection (`reconcileActiveAgents` skips or rebinds a chat whose stamp disagrees); its own commit message explicitly deferred *"Phase 2 (auto-switch global ruleset on chat enter)"*. (2) `watchRouteChanges`' chat-change branch loads the NEW chat's character **through the OLD chat's ruleset** — `state.sheet = loadSheet(state.chatId, state.ruleset)` — and `loadSheet` does `mergeSheet(blankSheet(ruleset), parsed)`, where `mergeSheet` is a per-bucket **name-matched** whitelist (`if (name in base[k])`). A field name present in both systems survives carrying the other system's number (symptom 1); everything else is dropped and the blank default stands (symptom 2). (3) `characterKey(id)` = `mrr-character-<id>` is ruleset-**agnostic by design**, and `saveSheet` writes the merged, wrong-shaped sheet straight back to it. The writer that seals the damage is the `flushSave()` at the TOP of the chat-switch handler, which fires on every chat LEAVE: enter an Exalted chat while D&D is active, leave it, and the D&D-shaped merge is persisted over the Exalted character's real record.
- **Part 1 — `mrrCheckChatRulesetStamp(chatId)` finishes fd6d755's deferred Phase 2.** Reads the chat's metadata through the same fetch shape `reconcileActiveAgents` uses (`GET /chats/:id`; `metadata` arrives as a JSON **string** from the engine route and is parsed before reading). Four outcomes: no stamp / stamp matches → **confirm** the chat; stamp differs and the ruleset **is** in the library → `activateFromLibrary(stamp)` then a full `window.location.reload()` on the existing `RELOAD_DELAY_MS` pattern (reused verbatim — every ruleset switch in this file already reloads; no fourth, softer switch mechanism was invented); stamp differs and the ruleset is **not** in the library → warn once and **hold** (nothing merged, nothing written) with an instruction to activate it manually; stamp differs but the user **just deliberately switched** → honor the click (see below). Two call sites: `init()` right after `state.chatId = getChatId()` resolves, and `watchRouteChanges`' chat-change branch right after `state.chatId = newId`. The function is self-guarding (already-confirmed / held / fetch-in-flight all return immediately), so the route poller also calls it **every tick** — that tick is the retry path, so a transient metadata-fetch failure resolves on its own instead of latching saves shut for the session.
- **Reload-loop sentinel.** `mrr-autoswitch-guard` (LOCAL-only key: `{chatId, stamp, ts}`, 30s TTL) is written immediately **before** the reload. If the same `(chatId, stamp)` mismatch is still present after that reload — i.e. activation did not stick — we refuse to reload a second time, warn loudly, and hold. Cleared on every confirm.
- **Part 2 — the save latch.** New `mrrRulesetConfirmedChatId`; while `state.chatId` is truthy and does not equal it, `saveSheet` refuses outright (warn-once per chat+character+reason). Confirmation happens only in the settled paths above. Ordering in the chat-switch handler is load-bearing and was verified: the outgoing `flushSave()` still runs **first**, under the OLD chat's confirmation (so the chat being left is persisted exactly as before); only then does `state.chatId` swap and the latch reset — and the reset sits **above** the handler's `!state.ruleset` / `!newId` early returns so it can never be skipped. A refusal sets `mrrDeferredSaveWanted`, and confirm replays it through one `flushSave()` so a user edit made during the (sub-second) check window isn't lost. A HELD refusal deliberately does **not** set that flag — a hold doesn't resolve on its own, it resolves when the user activates the other ruleset, which reloads anyway.
- **Part 3 — `sheet._rulesetId`, a stamp on the record (defense in depth).** `saveSheet` stamps it **after** the round-13 T5-d pristine-blank guard, immediately beside the `_schemaVersion` stamp and for the same structural reason. `loadSheet` checks it **before** the merge (the merge is the thing that does the damage): a record whose `_rulesetId` names a different ruleset is **not merged, not written, not deleted** — `loadSheet` returns a `blankSheet` instead, records the hold in `mrrSheetHold[characterId]`, and warns once; `saveSheet` then refuses to write for that character until the hold clears, which happens on the next successful matching load. Applied on both `loadSheet` branches (modern character-library key and the legacy chat-scoped fallback). **Legacy records carry no `_rulesetId` and cannot be judged** — Parts 1+2 close that window operationally; no heuristic was invented for unstamped records, since guessing a ruleset from a sheet's field names is exactly the name-matching that caused the bug.
- **Verifications performed for the new top-level key (stated as required).** `mergeSheet` — confirmed there is **no** generic `Object.keys(override)` copy anywhere in its body; it is a strict by-name whitelist, so `_rulesetId` is silently dropped from the merged result and cannot leak into a sheet's data or be carried across a merge. `mrrIsPristineBlankSheet` — **required a change and got one:** it strips `_schemaVersion` from a shallow copy before deep-equalling against `blankSheet()`; without stripping `_rulesetId` too, every previously-saved sheet would have failed that comparison purely on key count, silently disarming the T5-d blank-over-good guard. Both stamps are now stripped. Character export/import — `collectBundle` reads the stored record verbatim and `applyBundle` writes it back verbatim (both already carry `_schemaVersion` through unchanged, so the extra key is tolerated identically); `applyBundle` additionally already refuses any bundle whose `ruleset.id` differs from the active one, so an imported sheet's stamp always matches on arrival, and `importFromRpExtension`'s mrrp- records are unstamped and therefore unaffected. The character-delete path treats the record as an opaque blob and is likewise unaffected.
- **Interaction found while wiring the call sites, NOT in the original spec — an unbounded reload loop, now closed.** `init()` used to end with `reconcileActiveAgents(true)`, whose rebind branch **PATCHes the chat's `mrrChatRulesetId` to whatever ruleset is currently active**. Fired concurrently with the new stamp check, the two read and write the same field in opposite directions: reconcile re-stamps the chat to the active ruleset while the check switches the active ruleset to the old stamp — and each reload flips the disagreement to the other side. The `{chatId, stamp}` sentinel cannot catch that, because the stamp **changes every lap**. Resolution: the unconditional `reconcileActiveAgents` calls in `init()` **and** in `watchRouteChanges`' chat-change branch are removed; reconcile now fires from `mrrConfirmChatRuleset`, i.e. exactly once per **confirmed** chat. Behavior is preserved on both sides — on a confirmed chat the `rebind` argument is unread anyway (it is only consulted inside reconcile's own `stamp !== rulesetId` branch), and a new chat's default `activeAgentIds` still gets reconciled, just a beat later and with a guarantee it adds the RIGHT ruleset's agents.
- **Second interaction, same class: the deliberate-switch conflict.** The Library "Switch" button (and the paste and bundle-install paths) activate a ruleset and reload while the open chat is still stamped for the OLD one — so Part 1 would have woken up after that reload and switched the user straight back, undoing a deliberate choice and feeding the ping-pong above. New LOCAL-only one-shot marker `mrr-ruleset-switch-intent` (`{rulesetId, ts}`, 30s TTL) is written by all three **user-driven** activation paths; on a mismatch, Part 1 consumes it and — instead of switching away — confirms the chat so the reconcile fired by confirm **rebinds** the chat to the newly-chosen ruleset. The marker is deleted on any read, matched or not, so a stale one can never suppress a later genuine auto-switch. It is set at the three call sites rather than inside `activateFromLibrary`, because the auto-switch path calls that helper too and must not mark its own reload as a user choice.
- **Third interaction: the two ASYNC sheet writers, which the latch alone does not make safe.** Both `pollCustomAgentRuns` and the debounced narrator-message processor can mutate `state.sheet` while the latch is shut. Left ungated, each would apply mutations in memory, have the resulting `saveSheet` refused — and **still persist a "processed" marker** (`processedRunIds` / `processedMessageIds`), so the auto-switch reload would discard the mutations and the marker would prevent them ever being applied again: silent, permanent loss of a State Mutator turn. Both are now gated on the same `mrrSheetWriteBlockReason()` helper the save latch uses. The poller **stands down** before any fetch (nothing marked, next 1.5s tick retries; one log line per chat+reason). The message path has no natural retry — MutationObserver only fires on DOM change — so it **re-arms its own 1500ms debounce**, bounded to 20 tries (~30s); past the cap it drops the observation **unprocessed and unmarked**, so the next reload's initial DOM sweep picks it up cleanly. Skipping a mutation is recoverable; marking it applied when it wasn't is not.
- **Known limitation, documented in code and here, deliberately NOT fixed:** the same library character **deliberately** loaded under two different rulesets still shares ONE `mrr-character-<id>` record. Per-ruleset character records (`characterId × rulesetId`) are the structural fix and are queued for the Phase-B replan. This round makes the **accidental** cross-ruleset write impossible; the deliberate one remains design debt.
- Loader-only change (`extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js`); no ruleset, bundle, or agent data touched. `node --check` clean.

### Fixed — round 25: THE STATE MUTATOR WAS GUESSING THE DICE — moved to `post_processing`, rewritten to copy-and-cite

- **Live symptom (Corey, journal-proven, 2026-08-23): the sheet and the story disagree on every random number, and only on random numbers.** The mutator emitted `-11` with the rationale "dice showed 7 and 4" while the GM narrated `-12`; the following turn the mutator wrote `-13` against a narrated `-15`; a saving throw the mutator recorded as "rolled 14" was narrated as 16. Deterministic costs (Exalted mote spends, spell slots) agreed — but only by luck of rules-math, since both models were computing the same fixed figure independently.
- **Root cause — architectural, not prompt quality. The mutator ran `pre_generation`, i.e. BEFORE the GM narrated the turn.** For any outcome decided by dice, the number *did not exist yet* when the mutator ran, so it did the only thing it could: it invented one. No amount of prompt tightening could have fixed this; the information was not in the room. Every prior round's placeholder-hardening work was treating the symptom (`delta="+N"`, `delta="-<rolled 2d10 total>"` — the model announcing that it did not have a number) rather than the cause.
- **Fix — the mutator is now a `post_processing` agent.** Post-phase agents receive the completed response as `context.mainResponse` (`generate.routes.ts:8258` `pipeline.postGenerate(completedResponse, …)` → `agent-pipeline.ts:503-515` → `runPostProcessingAgents` → `executePhase(agents, "post_processing", …)`), so the mutator now reads the same text the player reads and copies numbers out of it instead of racing the narrator to produce them. Engine facts verified against `~/Marinara-Engine` (read-only): post-phase custom agents have **no regenerate exclusion** (pre-gen is gated `!input.regenerateMessageId`; post is gated only on agent presence, `hasPostProcessingAgents` at `:7941`), so swipes and regenerates produce fresh post-phase runs; post runs are saved against the **assistant** message id (`:8439` `resultMessageId` → `:8486` → `agentsStore.saveRun` `:8492`), which is exactly the DOM-visible anchor the extension's journal wants.
- **Where the phase is authored, and the trap in it.** Phase is a `**Phase:** <value>` line in the agent `.md` prose body, parsed by `extractPhase()` in both `tools/build-agents.mjs` and `tools/build-bundle.mjs`. The build tools read the phase from the **override** file when one exists, and an override with **no** `**Phase:**` line silently falls back to `pre_generation` — so the shared baseline's declaration does not reach any ruleset that overrides the role. Every override intended to flip needed its own line. Documented in the shared agent's "Per-ruleset overrides" section so the next author does not rediscover it live.
- **Latent build-tool bug found and fixed while flipping the phase: the allowed-phase enum contained a value the engine does not have.** Both `extractPhase()` copies carried `ALLOWED = {"pre_generation", "post_generation", "parallel"}`. The engine's enum is `["pre_generation", "parallel", "post_processing"]` (`packages/shared/src/schemas/agent.schema.ts:8`, `packages/shared/src/types/agent.ts:20`); `post_generation` exists in the engine **only** as an SSE *event* label (`generate.routes.ts:8185`), never as a phase. So the one correct spelling of the post phase was not accepted and would have fallen through to `pre_generation` while looking like it worked. Both copies now mirror the engine enum exactly.
- **Injection settings, bundle side.** `mrrInjectionSettings(role, phase)` previously returned `{}` for any non-pre-gen phase. It now returns `{ resultType: "context_injection" }` and never emits `injectAsSection` off the pre-gen path. Rationale for each half: `injectAsSection` only ever gated the pre-gen preset-marker path and the loader's own eligibility filter rejects `phase !== "pre_generation"` before it ever reads the flag, so shipping it would be a claim the runtime cannot honor. `resultType`, by contrast, is **more** load-bearing in the post phase, not less: `resolveAgentResultType` (`agent-executor.ts:3171-3179`) reads `settings.resultType` first, and a post-processing agent resolving to `text_rewrite` would make the engine **overwrite the narrator's message** with the agent's tag output (`agent-pipeline.ts:150`, `generate.routes.ts:4764`). Custom types miss the fallback map and default to `context_injection` — which is what we want and what the runs poller reads (`resultData.text`) — but "correct by default" is not a contract, so it is now stated explicitly. Side effect, intentional: parallel-phase overlays (`anima-banner-monitor`, `charm-cooldown-tracker`) also gain the explicit `resultType`, same value they resolved to implicitly.
- **No loader change was required, and none was made.** Two loader mechanisms were audited and both already handle the post phase correctly: (1) the "Add agent sections to active preset" assist filters candidates through `mrrInjectableManagedAgents`, which rejects `a.phase !== "pre_generation"` *and* `settings.injectAsSection !== true` (mirroring `runtime-agent-sections.ts:117-124`), so a post-phase mutator is excluded automatically by two independent conditions; (2) the runs poller's anchor map maps "a visible assistant message → itself", so post-phase runs resolve to their own anchor with **zero** deferrals and land in a swipe-aware journal bucket — the case round 18's `mrrBuildMsgAnchorMap` comment already names explicitly. One factual comment in the loader is now stale (`RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` ~:16112, "Every MRR ruleset installs its State Mutator as `phase: pre_generation` — all sixteen bundle.json files, verified by grep") — flagged, not edited, since loader edits belong to the validator's lane.
- **Prompts rewritten to copy-and-cite discipline** (`agents/state-mutator.md` shared baseline, `rulesets/dnd5e/agents/state-mutator.md`, `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md`). New governing rule: *the narration is your only source of numbers.* Copy every figure verbatim from the narration or from a `[dice: …]` tag in it; never roll, compute, re-derive, or re-add a modifier the GM already applied; cite the source in `reason=` (`reason="GM narrated 15 poison damage"`), which is not decoration — it is how the player audits a wrong value, and writing it forces the model to check the number really is in the text. When the narration establishes a change but states **no** number ("badly wounded"), emit nothing for that field: an omitted mutation is recoverable (the player sees the sheet did not move and can ask), an invented one is not. Non-numeric changes (conditions, inventory) are still emitted, since prose fully determines them. And mutate only the turn you were handed — prior turns were already applied, so re-emitting them double-applies.
- **The exalted3e "NARRATOR TAG DIRECTIVE" architecture is retired.** It existed solely because a pre-generation agent cannot write to the sheet and its only lever was talking the narrator into echoing tags. A post-processing agent's output is read directly by the runs poller, so the mutator now emits its own tags and nothing needs to echo them. Emitting a directive block is now on the prompt's FORBIDDEN list explicitly — the narrator has already written the turn and will never see the output.
- **Placeholder hardening round 2 — the curly-brace form.** Live evidence this session: `delta='+{summed 2d8 total as a concrete integer}'`, correctly dropped by the loader's validator but absent from the forbidden list. Added to the explicit "no placeholders, ever" section in the shared baseline, dnd5e, and exalted3e (alongside the existing letter, angle-bracket, and ellipsis forms), with the reason spelled out: a brace is not a slot anything downstream fills in, and writing an instruction to yourself inside braces is not the same as carrying it out. Also added to `rulesets/vtmv20/agents/state-mutator.md`'s existing hardening line (that file's phase is deliberately unchanged — see below).
- **T3-EXPANDED closed (the explicitly-deferred item).** `rulesets/exalted3e/agents/state-mutator.md`'s Sorcery workflow still taught with angle-bracket placeholders (`delta="-<spellCost>"`, `delta="-<currentMotes>"`) and carried its own placeholder-tolerant "Tag forms" grammar line — i.e. the one section of that file still modeling the exact failure the rest of it forbids. Rewritten around a single concrete running example (Death of Obsidian Butterflies, lorebook `Cost: 15 sorcerous motes`) so all five steps show real integers: `delta="+5"` for narrated successes, `delta="-15"` for the cast, `delta="-3"` for the per-round leak, `delta="-11"` for an abort at 11 accumulated. Added an explicit out for the abort case: if the accumulated total cannot be established, emit the conditions-remove tag and **no** mote tag — a dangling pool the player can zero by hand beats a wrong subtraction. Verified against the built bundle: no placeholder shapes survive outside the FORBIDDEN list itself.
- **GM-side dice doctrine** (`rulesets/dnd5e/gm-agent.md`, `rulesets/exalted3e/gm-agent.md`, `rulesets/ose/gm-agent.md`, `rulesets/rolemaster/gm-agent.md`; there is no shared GM source — every ruleset authors its own). Three rules, phrased per-file for whether that agent *is* the narrator (ose, rolemaster) or advises it (dnd5e, exalted3e): (1) when the chat has tool use on, resolve **every** random outcome by calling the engine's server-side `roll_dice` tool and narrate the returned number verbatim — never invent a roll result; (2) a `[dice: … -> total]` (or `[mrr-roll: …]`) tag in the player's message is **authoritative** — never reroll, alter, replace, or re-add a modifier it already folded in, mirroring the engine's own game-mode doctrine; (3) **always state resolved numbers explicitly in the narration** — damage dealt, amount healed, roll totals, resource spends — because the state system now reads them out of the prose. The `roll_dice` tool is real and server-side (`packages/server/src/services/tools/tool-executor.ts:307`), offered to the main generation whenever the chat's `enableTools` is on, and default-enabled once that toggle is set.
- **Consequential GM-prompt corrections, not in the original scope but required by the phase flip.** exalted3e's and rolemaster's GM prompts opened with a "Tag-emission contract" instructing the narrator to echo the mutator's directive block verbatim — text that became false the moment the mutator moved to post-phase, and that would have left the narrator hunting for a block that never arrives while still emitting its own `[mrr-state:]` tags (the DOM channel), i.e. deliberately arming the double-write that round 23's sole-writer claim exists to suppress. Both rewritten into a **State contract**: state every resolved number explicitly, name the pool and damage type, do **not** emit tags yourself. ose's "defer to … The State Mutator for the tag-emission protocol" line got the same correction. rolemaster's in-prompt "State-tag vocabulary" block (six literal `[mrr-state: …]` examples inside the shipped prompt) became a "Tracked values — say these out loud" checklist of the same fields as prose targets, removing tag syntax from a prompt whose model is now told not to emit tags.
- **Docs.** `docs/INSTALL.md` gains a sixth setup step (**Chat Settings → Function Calling → "Enable Tool Use"**) with the reasoning: without it the narrating model invents every random outcome by picking a plausible number. Same step added to `rulesets/{dnd5e,exalted3e,ose,rolemaster}/INSTALL.md`, tuned per system. All five note that granting `roll_dice` to individual agents in the Agents UI is **optional and unnecessary** — the GM main model gets the tool from the chat toggle, agent-attached tools additionally require a per-agent grant the bundle cannot ship (the agent-import route strips `settings.enabledTools`), and the **State Mutator needs no dice at all** since it copies rather than rolls. Every "the State Mutator deliberately gets no preset section" passage updated from "we choose not to" to "it cannot take one" with the phase reason.
- **Scope boundaries held.** `rulesets/exwod/` and `rulesets/w20/` agent files untouched per the standing all-systems-pass ruling — both keep their own `state-mutator.md` overrides with no `**Phase:**` line, so both stay `pre_generation` and their bundles were not rebuilt (byte-identical on disk). `rulesets/vtmv20/agents/state-mutator.md` is the **fourth** override and is deliberately held in the same class: it received only the additive curly-brace hardening, keeps `pre_generation`, and keeps its narrator-directive framing — flipping it without also rewriting `rulesets/vtmv20/gm-agent.md` would have shipped a half-migrated bundle for an untested system. The remaining eleven rulesets have no override and inherit the shared baseline, so they flip with it. `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` not modified; `tools/build-extension-package.mjs` not run.
- **Verification.** `build-bundle.mjs` + `build-agents.mjs` re-run for all fourteen in-scope rulesets; `validate-bundles` and `validate-rulesets` green across all sixteen (including the two untouched). Built-artifact spot checks confirm `phase: "post_processing"` with `settings: {"resultType":"context_injection"}` on the mutator in dnd5e / exalted3e / ose / rolemaster / pathfinder2e, `pre_generation` retained in vtmv20 / exwod / w20, and the copy-and-cite / curly-brace / `NO STATE CHANGE` markers plus the `roll_dice` doctrine present in the shipped `promptTemplate` strings rather than only in the sources.

### Fixed — round 26: TURN-EDITING BUTTONS DEAD IN EVERY CHAT — the tag-hiding `innerHTML` rewrite was cloning React's message subtree

- **Live symptom (Corey, 2026-08-23, convicted by an extension-off test): regenerate, the swipe arrows, and edit were all dead — in every chat and both game systems.** The buttons rendered, hover worked, clicks produced *nothing*: no console output, no network attempt, no error. It survived a hard reload. Disabling the extension and reloading restored them instantly. Long-standing, predating rounds 24–25 — earlier sessions had noticed swipe failures and misattributed them to whichever fire was burning at the time.
- **Root cause: `hideStateTagsInElement`'s `node.innerHTML = wrapped`.** The function ran on chat *message* elements whenever they contained a complete `[mrr-state: ...]` tag not yet wrapped. Assigning `innerHTML` serializes the element and re-parses it, replacing the entire subtree with fresh DOM — so every React-rendered element inside, including the message's hover action bar (swipe / edit / regenerate), came back as a pixel-perfect clone with **no React fiber behind it**. Visible, correctly styled, and completely inert; no handlers to fire and therefore nothing to log. Two call sites drove it: the tail of `processChatMessage` (round 15 gated it to childList-origin observations, which fixed that round's streaming freeze but left the button-killing rewrite alive), and the initial page-load sweep — the latter is why the damage reappeared on **every** reload for every historical tag-bearing message.
- **Same knife as rounds 14/15, different victim.** Those rounds established the class — an `innerHTML` rewrite detaches the text node React is streaming into — and gated the call rather than removing it. The gate only ever protected the streaming text; the action bar was being clobbered the whole time by the childList-origin and initial-sweep paths the gate deliberately allows.
- **Fix: the destructive rewrite is deleted outright, not relocated.** `hideStateTagsInElement` and both call sites are gone, and nothing replaces them — no other DOM write was substituted. Round 25's move of the state mutator to `post_processing` on 13 of 16 rulesets (plus the ban on GM narrators emitting `[mrr-state:]` tags) means new messages carry no tags in chat DOM at all, so the cosmetic hiding had almost nothing left to hide. The now-unused `.mrr-state-tag` CSS rule was removed from `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.css` and the embedded copy in the loader.
- **Accepted cosmetic regression:** the three held rulesets (`vtmv20`, `exwod`, `w20`, still `pre_generation`) and all historical messages now show raw `[mrr-state: ...]` tags in the chat transcript. Traded deliberately for working turn-editing UI everywhere. Engine-native `regexScript` hiding is the intended replacement but is **not** this round: it would also blind the DOM tag detection the three held rulesets still depend on, so it needs its own design pass.
- **Detection, parsing, journaling, and apply logic are untouched.** The removed function was display-only; the `mrr-state-tag` class it wrote existed solely as its own "already wrapped" guard and is read nowhere else in the tree. `processChatMessage`'s `isChildListOrigin` parameter and `watchChatMessages`'s `pendingOrigin` flag are now vestigial — kept (and commented as such) rather than unwound, to avoid touching the observer scheduling in a fix round.
- **Audit:** every other `innerHTML` write in the loader targets an element the extension created itself (`marinara.addElement` → `document.createElement`) — the `el()` attribute helper, the panel resize-handle glyph, and the sheet toggle button. No write touches a `[data-message-id]` subtree or any other engine-owned React DOM. No `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, or `replaceChildren` anywhere.
- **Verification.** `node --check` passes; loader shrinks 1,048,308 → 1,044,954 bytes (−3,354), buying back headroom under the engine's hard 1 MiB package limit. Package not rebuilt (validator's lane).

### Changed — round 27a: the packaged loader is comment-stripped at build time (390 KB of headroom recovered)

- **The problem: the source and the shipped artifact were the same file, and only one of them had a budget.** The engine's 1 MiB personal-extension JS ceiling applies to the *packaged* loader. Because `build-extension-package.mjs` inlined `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` verbatim into all four packaged copies, every documentation comment in the source was also spending engine budget. Round 26 landed with roughly 3.6 KB of headroom and an earlier build this session actually tipped over the cap and failed. The repo's documentation culture lives in those comments — 1,096 block comments — so trimming them out of the *source* was never the right answer.
- **The fix: a comment-strip step in the packaging pipeline.** `build-extension-package.mjs` now runs the loader through `terser` (new devDependency) with **compression and mangling explicitly disabled** and `format.comments = false`. That is an AST round-trip whose only effect is comment removal plus whitespace normalization: identifiers keep their names, control flow is untouched, and the output stays beautified so a shipped artifact is still readable and diff-able when debugging a user's install.
- **Result: 1,044,954 B source → 653,689 B packaged (−391,265 B, −37.4%), leaving 394,887 B of headroom** under the 1,048,576 B ceiling. Measured split: 396,822 B of that is comment text; reformatting costs 5,557 B back. The zip drops to 416,359 B.
- **The source file is never written by the build.** This is load-bearing, not just hygiene: `tools/embed-css.mjs` keys off the `EMBEDDED_CSS_BEGIN` / `EMBEDDED_CSS_END` block comments, and those markers only ever have to survive in the source. Nothing in the loader inspects its own text at runtime — no `Function.prototype.toString`, no self-parsing — which is what makes stripping safe for the shipped copy. (The one `new Function(...)` in the loader builds an expression from a substituted formula string, not from source text.)
- **Literal-preservation proof.** The strip was verified by walking the terser AST of the source and of the stripped output and diffing every string, template segment, regex, and numeric literal in order: **7,378 literals, zero differences.** That is the direct evidence that `EMBEDDED_CSS` and the giant embedded prompt fences come through byte-identical, which regex-based comment stripping could never have guaranteed.
- **All four packaged copies are covered by the single strip** — the inlined `config.js` in `Extensions/<name>/manifest.json`, the same inline blob inside `marinara-extensions.json`, the sibling `Extensions/<name>/extension.js`, and the single-file fallback `RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` — because they all package one shared string. `node --check` passes on both on-disk artifacts, and both inline blobs pass the validator's plain-`Function` and 2.4.3 async-wrapper parse checks.
- `--no-strip` is available as a debug escape hatch that ships the commented source verbatim. Building without the `terser` devDependency installed is a hard failure with a `bun install` pointer rather than a silent fall-through to an oversized package.
- **`validate-extension-package.mjs`:** now reports `source → packaged` bytes and remaining headroom on every run, and warns if the packaged blob is not smaller than the source (the signature of a skipped strip). No byte-parity check between source and package existed or was added — they differ by design now. The parity that *does* still hold, and is still enforced, is sibling `extension.js` == inline `config.js`: both are the same stripped string.

### Removed — round 27b: dead code (the chat sheet-sync no-op, vestigial origin plumbing, and the legacy render family)

Source loader **1,044,954 → 988,005 bytes (−56,949)**. Nothing here changes behavior: every removal is either a confirmed server-side no-op, a parameter nothing reads, or a function with no reachable call site. Verified by an AST symbol walk, not by grep counts — every removed identifier is provably absent from the parsed program and every keeper provably still present.

- **`syncSheetToChat` + `buildSyncFields` deleted (−12,040 B).** Not merely unused — a **confirmed no-op against the server since engine 2.0.9**. `PATCH /chats/:id` parses its body with `createChatSchema.partial().parse(...)` (`chats.routes.ts:1001`), and `createChatSchema` (`chat.schema.ts:11-19`) declares exactly `{name, mode, characterIds, groupId, personaId, promptPresetId, connectionId}` — no `customTrackerFields`. A plain Zod object *strips* unknown keys instead of rejecting them, so the PATCH returned 200, stored nothing, and the field read back undefined forever. Round 21 established this and retracted the old "57-field sheet sync to chat succeeded" confirmation (it had read an HTTP 200 as a write) but kept the code pending a design call. Round 27 makes the call: it was burning a GET **plus** a PATCH on every debounced save, and printing "synced N fields" while doing nothing. Gone with it: the `Sync sheet to chat fields` toolbar button (**the one user-visible change in this entry** — a button whose entire effect was a lie), and the chat leg of `scheduleAutoSync`.
- **`scheduleAutoSync` is now a TWO-surface fan-out, not three.** The two survivors are load-bearing and deliberately untouched: `syncSheetToAgents` (the overlay agents' promptTemplate sheet block — this is the *only* path by which the sheet reaches the model, so it is the read path) and `syncFieldReferenceToLorebook` (the managed lorebook field-reference entry that reaches the main narrator). The designed replacement channel for the deleted leg is game-state `playerStats`, plan items B7/B8; a tombstone at the old definition site records that pointer. `COUPLINGS.md` row 5 is rewritten from "⚠️ no-op" to resolved, and its `/chats/:id/metadata` half is unaffected — that is still how `activeAgentIds` and the ruleset stamp round-trip.
- **Vestigial `isChildListOrigin` / `pendingOrigin` plumbing unwound (−1,043 B).** Round 15 added an origin flag to gate a cosmetic `hideStateTagsInElement` call; round 26 deleted that call outright and left the flag threaded through for one round. Removed here: `processChatMessage`'s second parameter, `watchChatMessages`' `pendingOrigin` map, the `wasChildList` threading through `schedule()`'s timer body, and the now-pointless `isChildList` computation in the MutationObserver callback. All five `schedule()` call sites drop their origin argument. Detection, journaling, and apply logic never read any of it.
- **The dead legacy render family deleted — 21 functions, 1,040 lines (−44,070 B).** `renderSheet` has dispatched unconditionally to `mrrP3RenderSheet` since the Phase-3 cutover, whose promised "step 2" deletion never ran; the classic renderers have been unreachable for months. Flagged by the 2026-07-03 code review (§3-D1), executed now. Roots with zero call sites anywhere: `renderAttributes`, `renderSkills`, `renderSaves`, `renderDerived`, `renderStates`, `renderBackgrounds`, `renderInventory`, `parseDiceNotation`, `renderIntimaciesSection`, `fillTagTemplate`, `escapeHtml`. Helpers reachable only from inside that set — each proven by resolving its call site's *enclosing function*, not by counting greps: `renderAttrRow`, `renderSkillRow`, `renderCustomSkillRow`, `renderSpecialtyRow`, `renderSaveRow`, `renderValue`, `renderBar`, `renderTrack`, `ensureTypedTrack`, `renderBackgroundRow`.
- **Deliberately NOT deleted, and why** — the 07-03 list named these too, but each has a live call site, so none of them are dead code:
  - **`renderConditions`** — called from `mrrP3RenderSheet`, the *live* Phase-3 render path. This is exactly the trap a prior round fell into (deleting a "zero code reads" entry that turned out to be a render dispatch). The 07-03 plan's D1b wants it repointed at `mrrP3RenderConditionsSection`; that is a behavior change and needs its own round.
  - **`buildItemBag`** (← `showItemBag`), **`buildSpellbook`** (← `showSpellbook`), **`buildIntimaciesPanel`** (← `showIntimacies`), and **`LS_INTIMACIES_POS`** (← `buildIntimaciesPanel`). These three sit in `else` branches behind always-true `typeof mrrP3* === "function"` guards, so they are unreachable in practice — but removing them means editing a live guard, which is a behavior change, not a deletion. Left for the D1b round.
- **`tools/token-migration-map.json` deleted** — the Phase-4 oklch design-token migration record, read by no tool and referenced only by one stylesheet comment (now rewritten to say the file is gone and point at the CHANGELOG for its summary) and by historical CHANGELOG entries. The empty `examples/` directory is also removed. Both were called out in the plan's B13 cleanup list.
- **Doc reconciliation.** `AGENTS.md`'s function-inventory "Sheet rendering" and "Sync" rows described the deleted families; both rewritten to the live surfaces. Four doc-comment headers on live `mrrP3Render*` primitives titled themselves with the classic names (`/* renderBar — resource bar ... */` above `mrrP3RenderBar`) — retitled, since those names no longer resolve to anything.
- **Verification.** `node --check` green on the source loader; AST symbol walk confirms all 26 removed identifiers absent and all 12 keepers present; `tools/build-extension-package.mjs` and `tools/validate-extension-package.mjs` both PASS. **The r19/r24 probe suites assert `syncSheetToChat` behavior (X1 probes) and will need adapting** — flagged for the validator's lane, not fixed here.

### Fixed — round 28b: GM dice doctrine — a regenerated swipe must ROLL AGAIN, not reuse the last version's numbers

- **Live evidence (Corey, log `DND_2307`):** on regenerated swipes the GM model sometimes skipped the `roll_dice` tool entirely and narrated *identical* "rolls" across versions — the same heal four times running, paired save totals repeating verbatim. The existing doctrine forbade inventing a number and forbade rerolling the player's authoritative `[dice: ...]` tag, but said nothing about what a *regenerate* does to the GM's own rolls, and the model resolved that silence by treating the previous version's numbers as still valid.
- New clause added to the dice doctrine in `rulesets/{dnd5e,exalted3e,ose,rolemaster}/gm-agent.md`: *"Every VERSION of a turn rolls fresh. On a regenerate or a swipe, all previous rolls for this turn are VOID — they belong to a version that no longer exists. Call `roll_dice` again for every random outcome in the new version. Never narrate a roll you did not obtain from the tool during THIS generation, and never reuse a number from an earlier attempt at the same turn."* Identical wording in all four so the behavior is one rule, not four dialects. `exwod`, `w20`, and `vtmv20` are deliberately untouched this round.
- Affected bundles rebuilt (`tools/build-bundle.mjs`); the only delta in each `bundle.json` is the `gmAgent.promptTemplate` line. `validate-rulesets` 16/16 and `validate-bundles` 16/16 green; the doctrine is confirmed present in the four rebuilt bundles' prompt templates and confirmed absent from the three that were out of scope.
- **Docs — the stale-GM-card trap, found the same night.** A GM prompt pasted by hand into a character card (or a hand-made agent) is **not** updated by a bundle re-import: a card still carrying a pre-2.4.0 prompt was emitting the old inline tags and had none of the dice doctrine. Added a re-paste instruction to `docs/INSTALL.md`'s Updates section and to the Updating section of all four affected rulesets' `INSTALL.md` — re-paste from `gm-agent.md` (the block between the triple backticks) on every bundle update. The bundle's *managed* agents still update in place automatically; only hand-pasted copies need the manual step.

### Fixed — round 28: THE TWO BINDINGS ROT ON THEIR OWN — a standing, settings-tag-anchored reconciliation pass (audit F3 + F4)

Closes the reconciliation fix the round-21 ledger correction explicitly deferred to the replan ("a re-typed agent orphans its marker and silently recreates the read-path outage"). Both defects are engine-source-confirmed, and both were previously papered over by a standing instruction to the user; that instruction is now unnecessary.

- **F3 — a reinstall orphaned every preset marker section, silently.** An agent's `type` is immutable after create (`agents.storage.ts` `update()` has no `type` field) and **collision-suffixed** on recreate: `getUniqueCustomType` (`:124-136`) appends `suffixFromId(id)` — the row id's first 8 `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` chars — then `-2`, `-3`, … on further collisions. Our round-20 preset markers key on that type on **both** sides: `markerConfig.agentType` *and* the `{{agent::<type>}}` macro in the section body (`assembler.ts:845`, `:878`). So uninstall → reinstall mints new types, every marker section points at a type nothing has, and on engine 2.4.0+ the preset **owns placement** — unplaced agent output is discarded with no warning. The agents keep running, keep costing tokens, keep showing healthy run rows, and the narrator sees none of it.
- **F4 — applying a chat-preset wiped our chat stamp.** `chat-presets.storage.ts` `applyToChat` (`:300-325`) rebuilds chat metadata wholesale — `baseDefaults + presetMetadata + a short preserved allow-list + appliedChatPresetId`. No `mrr*` key is on that allow-list, so `mrrChatRulesetId` is **erased**; and because preset metadata is portable between chats, a *different* chat's stamp can be carried in. `activeAgentIds` survives only when the preset itself doesn't carry that key.
- **The fix, in one sentence: agent identity truth moves to the settings tags.** `mrrManaged` / `mrrRulesetId` / `mrrAgentRole` are written into every row the installer creates, so they survive a reinstall verbatim; types and chat metadata do not. New `mrrReconcileAgentBindings()` re-derives both bindings from the tags instead of trusting either stored key.
- **Marker reconciliation.** For each `agent_data` section in the chat's active preset whose `agentType` matches **no live agent** but does parse as one of our historical types, the section is PATCHed to the live type — `content` and `markerConfig.agentType` rewritten together (a mismatch between the two yields an empty section, since the assembler feeds the macro through `ctx.macroCtx.agentData[agentType]`). `identifier` is deliberately left stale: `updatePromptSectionSchema` omits it (`prompt.schema.ts:186-188`) and it is inert for marker resolution. Sections pointing at a live type are left alone; anything not matching our naming scheme is **never touched**.
- **How a role is parsed out of a collision-suffixed type.** Candidate forms are `base(role)` or `base(role) + "-" + S`, where `base` is `mrrAgentTypeForRole(role)` and `S` matches the engine's suffix grammar `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,8}(-[0-9]+)?$`. **Longest base wins**, because main's base (`mrr-overlay-v1`) is a prefix of every other role's base — without that rule `mrr-overlay-v1-lore-query` would resolve to *main* with `lore-query` misread as a suffix. Only roles that exist **right now** are candidates, so a marker for a role the bundle has since dropped resolves to null and is left in place. A type whose live target already has a section is skipped with a log rather than duplicated (the engine mints one token per type and consumes it on first substitution, so a second section could never fill).
- **Stamp re-derivation (F4).** "No stamp" was previously read as "never stamped", which hands the chat to whatever ruleset happens to be active — the exact cross-ruleset bleed round 24 exists to prevent. `mrrCheckChatRulesetStamp` now asks the chat's own managed agents first: if every managed agent named in `activeAgentIds` (by type **or** by legacy row id) agrees on a ruleset, the chat is re-stamped with it and fed through the identical decision path as if it had been stamped all along. Disagreement, or no managed agents at all, leaves the previous behavior untouched. Deliberately runs **before** the confirm, so `reconcileActiveAgents` — which stamps an unstamped chat with the *active* ruleset — sees the restored value instead of racing it. A stamp that disagrees with the chat's own agents is reported, not overwritten (it may legitimately be a carried-in one).
- **Trigger points, all idempotent and gated.** (1) `installBundle`'s tail, right before the reload — the reinstall that minted the new types is exactly when the markers need repointing, and this is the moment we still know which ruleset was installed; (2) `mrrConfirmChatRuleset`, the round-24 confirmation chokepoint, which is the first moment a chat's ruleset is settled; (3) a self-throttled watcher on the route-poll tick, `mrrWatchAppliedChatPreset`, which notices a chat-preset apply on an *already-confirmed* chat (the stamp check returns early there and can no longer see it) by comparing the engine's own `appliedChatPresetId`, then forces a re-run plus a **passive** `reconcileActiveAgents()`.
- **Fetch economy.** Steady state is a string compare: the pass is gated on `(chatId, rulesetId)` and a second trigger joins the in-flight promise rather than starting a second one. A run costs `GET /chats/:id` + `GET /agents`, plus `GET /prompts/:id/full` only when the chat actually has a preset, plus one PATCH per genuinely orphaned section. Stamp re-derivation costs one `/agents` fetch at most once per chat entry, and zero when the chat has no `activeAgentIds` to derive from. The preset watcher is one GET per minute, and only while a chat is confirmed and no install is in flight.
- **Cache interaction.** `mrrResolveMutatorConfigId` caches the state-mutator's **row id** per ruleset and only re-resolves when the *active ruleset* changes — which a reinstall does not do, so it would have kept handing the sole-writer filter a dead id. The pass now fingerprints the ruleset's managed rows (`id:type`, sorted) and clears that cache when the fingerprint changes, and unconditionally on an install. The round-23 bucket claims, the round-24 latch, and the sole-writer filter itself all key on config ids/settings resolved per-poll, so they pick the new id up on the next cycle with nothing else to reset.
- Preset marker repair is skipped with a one-time explanation when the chat's preset is the stock read-only "Marinara Universal" (every mutating route returns 409 via `rejectStockPresetMutation`), and when the chat has no preset at all — attaching one stays the consent-gated job of "Add agent sections to active preset", never a side effect of reconciliation.
- **New functions** (all in `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js`): `mrrChatMeta`, `mrrManagedRoleTypes`, `mrrLiveAgentTypes`, `mrrRoleForOrphanType`, `mrrManagedAgentSignatureFor`, `mrrInvalidateMutatorCache`, `mrrDeriveRulesetFromChatAgents`, `mrrDeriveAndRestampChat`, `mrrReconcilePresetMarkers`, `mrrReconcileAgentBindings`, `mrrWatchAppliedChatPreset`. `mrrCheckChatRulesetStamp`'s post-stamp logic is factored into an inner `decide(stamp)` so a recovered stamp takes the identical path; no other signature changed.
- **Verification.** `node --check` green. 28 throwaway probes over freshly extracted copies of the new pure helpers: 16 on the role-parse grammar (pristine base, base + suffix, base + suffix + attempt, longest-match beating main on every role slug, a dropped role resolving to null, an over-long remainder rejected as an illegal suffix, third-party and `mrr-overlay-v2` never matched) and 12 on role/type/signature/derivation (string-vs-object `settings`, malformed JSON settings, legacy-row-id derivation, disagreement → null, foreign-only → null, signature changing across a reinstall).

### Added — round 22: countable track ladders in the agent-facing sheet block

- **Context: retest 10 was the milestone.** The read path is live in production — the narrator quoted motes, Willpower, Anima and Join Battle verbatim, confirming the round-20 preset-marker architecture end to end. One content gap remained, and Corey's GM card diagnosed it itself: the track family reached the agents as damage TOTALS only ("0 bashing / 0 lethal / 0 aggravated"), never as the per-level BOX STRUCTURE. Ox-Body Technique adds extra -1/-2/-0 boxes to the base ladder, so with only totals the narrator cannot count how much punishment is left; it fell back to assuming the base template and honestly said so.
- **Ladder source of truth (the finding).** Three separate stores, and the sheet DOES distinguish base from bonus — so the base/bonus split can be reported exactly rather than guessed:
  - **base ladder** — `derivedStat.track`, an array of `{label, penalty}` cells declared by the ruleset (exalted3e: 7 cells, `-0/-1/-1/-2/-2/-4/Incapacitated`, penalties `0/-1/-1/-2/-2/-4/-99`);
  - **bonus levels** — `state.sheet.extraTrack[<track>]`, same cell shape, stored on the SHEET; this is where Ox-Body additions live;
  - **fill state** — `state.sheet.trackCells[<track>]`, positionally aligned to base+bonus **after** a penalty-DESCENDING sort (penalties are negative, so descending is least-hurt-first), holding the damage type's LABEL (`"B"`/`"L"`/`"A"`) or `null`.
  The new serializer mirrors `mrrP3RenderDerivedTrack`'s comparator exactly — if the two ever diverge, fill state would be reported against the wrong levels, which is worse than reporting nothing.
- **Before this change the track contributed NOTHING to `buildSheetForPrompt` except a grammar line.** `Health Track` sits in `sections.hidden` so it is filtered out of the Derived Stats snapshot, and the Resources snapshot skips `type: "custom"` entries — so the only track text in the block was the state-mutator field reference. The totals the narrator was quoting came from the ruleset's own agent prompt, not from the live sheet.
- **New `mrrBuildTrackLadderLines()`**, emitted immediately before the field reference (structure first, then grammar). One compact line per track, e.g. for a 2-box Ox-Body character with 2 bashing and 1 lethal:
  `- Health Track — 9 boxes total (7 base +2 bonus): -0 x1 [1 empty] | -1 x4 (+2 bonus) [2 bashing, 1 lethal, 1 empty] | -2 x2 [2 empty] | -4 x1 [1 empty] | Incapacitated x1 [1 empty] — deepest filled level: -1 (penalty -1); totals 0 aggravated, 1 lethal, 2 bashing`
  Consecutive cells sharing label+penalty group into one level; bonus boxes are counted per level AND in the header. The block is prefixed with an explicit "COUNT THESE BOXES — do not assume the system's default ladder" instruction, which is the specific failure being corrected. "Deepest filled level" is reported from the ladder's own `penalty` field as DATA, not as a rules ruling about wound penalties.
- **Untyped tracks (the A6.8 gap), reported not fixed.** fate-core's Physical/Mental Stress are `renderAs: "track"` with **no `damageTypes`**. The serializer handles them correctly — box structure plus an "N of M marked" count instead of a type breakdown, with marked cells rendered as `marked` rather than leaking the stored sentinel. But the underlying gap is real and untouched, as instructed: `damageTypesFor` returns null for these, so `mrrP3RenderDerivedTrack`'s `onCellClick` early-returns and **untyped track boxes are unclickable in the UI**, and `syncTrackCellsToTyped` bails so `state.sheet.track[name]` is never populated for them. Nothing can currently write fill state for an untyped track through the UI; the serializer will report it faithfully once something can.
- **Purity.** The serializer reads state and never writes it. It deliberately does NOT call `ensureTrackCells` (which mutates `state.sheet.trackCells`) — prompt-building must not be a side-effecting operation. Padding is done locally on a copy, and probe R22-P6 asserts the sheet is byte-identical before and after.
- **Reach and size.** `syncSheetToAgents` PATCHes the block into the promptTemplate of **every managed agent for the active ruleset** (filtered on `mrrManaged` + `mrrRulesetId`), so all installed MRR agents receive it. Measured size delta on the EX3 Ox-Body fixture: **435 bytes** (~109 tokens) for the whole ladder block including its header — small enough to ride every prompt, and probe R22-P8 pins it under 500.
- **Loader-only.** No bundle data changes; `git diff --name-only rulesets/ | grep bundle.json` = 0. Validators remain 16/16.
- Probes added: new harness `r22-track-probes.mjs`, eval-extracted via `assemble-r22.mjs`. 9 probes against the REAL shipped `exalted3e` and `fate-core` ruleset files: base ladder; the Ox-Body case with mixed bashing/lethal; an **exact-string** assertion on the full emitted Ox-Body line (R22-P2b) so the countable format is pinned verbatim; the COUNT-THESE-BOXES header; a deep aggravated mark; untyped fate-core stress; purity; the no-tracks no-op; and the size delta. 255 probes across the suite, up from 246.
- Gate outputs: `node --check` clean; both `new Function` wrapper evals parse clean; `validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` 16/16; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14; forbidden-API grep = 0; all eleven probe harnesses green (255 total).

### Fixed — round 21: LEDGER CORRECTION PASS + audit truth-and-safety items (no design changes)

A Fable audit re-verified every coupling claim against engine source and overturned three
of them. This round lands **only** the truth-and-safety corrections; every design-decision
finding (preset-stamp strategy, agent opt-in, RP/game-mode split, B2-R re-spec, marker
reconciliation architecture) is deferred to Corey's replan. Every claim below was
re-verified independently at source before being written into the ledger.

- **C1 / ROW 2 — retracted: "regenerate runs agents, same pipeline, no special-casing" (round 14).** False, and false at every version back to 2.0.9. `shouldRunPreGen` is `(hasPreGenAgents OR reviewedAgentInjections.length > 0) && !input.regenerateMessageId` (`generate.routes.ts:4965`) and is the sole gate on the whole pre-generation block (`:5032`); the sibling knowledge-router gate carries the same condition (`:4960-4964`). **Pre_generation agents do not run on a regenerate/swipe** — the turn's cached `extra.contextInjections` are replayed instead, so the narrator still sees agent context while no fresh run is created. Consequence, stated no further than the evidence: a swipe normally yields no new custom-agent run, so the runs poller cannot be the per-swipe mutation signal — inline narrator tags on the DOM path are the only per-swipe signal that exists. This does not invalidate round-18's re-anchoring or round-19's latch fixes; it does mean B2-R was specced against a generation shape that does not occur. Re-derivation is a replan item.
- **C1 / ROW 3 — retracted: round-20's "in-place type migration"; my own probe false-passed it.** `agentsStore.update()` (`agents.storage.ts:238-256`) builds `updateFields` from `name`, `description`, `phase`, `connectionId`, `imagePath`, `promptTemplate`, `settings`/`resultType` — **no `type` field at all**. A PATCH carrying `type` is accepted and the type silently ignored; only delete-then-create can change it. Probe R20-F1-2 asserted that `findManagedAgent` still locates legacy agents by settings tags and that `mrrAgentTypeForRole` derives correct types — it never asserted persistence, so it passed while the sentence citing it was wrong. Same class of false-pass this project has been burned by twice. The probe is **re-scoped and renamed** to exactly what it demonstrates, with the failure documented in its own comment. Verified hazard added: `getUniqueCustomType` (`:124-136`) mints `<type>-<idSuffix>` when a row already holds the requested type, so **type strings are not stable identifiers across reinstalls** — and since round-20's marker sections key on TYPE, a re-typed agent orphans its marker and silently recreates the read-path outage. Reconciliation fix deferred to the replan.
- **C1 / ROW 5 — retracted: "57-field sheet sync to chat succeeded".** It read an HTTP 200 as a write. `PATCH /chats/:id` parses via `createChatSchema.partial().parse(req.body)` (`chats.routes.ts:1001`), and `createChatSchema` (`chat.schema.ts:11-19`) declares exactly `{name, mode, characterIds, groupId, personaId, promptPresetId, connectionId}` — no `customTrackerFields`. A plain Zod object strips unknown keys, so the call returns 200, stores nothing, and the field reads back undefined. True at every version since 2.0.9. `syncSheetToChat` is now marked in-code as a **confirmed no-op**; the code is retained (removal is a replan call) and the surface is marked DEPRECATED pending B7/B8. The round-19 captured-chatId fix stays — it closed a real cross-chat read-modify-write, and simply now guards a call whose write half the server discards. The `/chats/:id/metadata` half of the row is unaffected and still works.
- **C2 — docs + inert payloads.** `docs/INSTALL.md` gains the RP preset marker step (step 5), mirroring README's wording. All 11 ruleset guides already scoped the requirement to roleplay; they now also say **why** — the preset assembler is skipped entirely for game and conversation chats (`generate.routes.ts:2457`), so those modes keep the older depth-0 injection fallback and are genuinely unaffected. Stale cite `chats.routes.ts:349` corrected to `:1090` (2 sites). Removed the inert entry-level `tags` arrays from all three lorebook ENTRY writes: `createLorebookEntrySchema` (`lorebook.schema.ts:157-183`) has no `tags` field — plural `tags` is a BOOK field (`:148`), an ENTRY carries singular `tag` (`:203`) — so every entry-level array we have ever sent was silently stripped, and nothing read them back (the only tag read in the loader is `lb.tags` on the book). Book-level tags retained and verified. The comment that credited plural-`tags` for fixing the entry install is corrected: single-entry POSTs were the fix; that half was always false.
- **C3 — custom-tool description guard.** `description` is `z.string().min(1).max(500)` (`custom-tool.schema.ts:14`); the old `typeof === "string"` check let `""` through, making the POST a guaranteed 400 that failed the entire tool install for any bundle omitting a description. Empty/whitespace now falls back to a generated one-liner. One docs line added (docs/INSTALL.md, README, install guide): chat tools require the chat's own **enableTools** toggle — deliberately **not** auto-set (design call, replan).
- **C4 — lorebook `entryLimit`. Schema finding: it IS settable.** `lorebookBaseSchema.entryLimit` is `z.number().int().min(1).max(1000).default(100)` (`lorebook.schema.ts:117-122`; LIMITS at `defaults.ts:91/93/95`), and the engine sets it the same way in its own migration (`entryLimit: Math.max(100, descriptions.length)`, `extended-descriptions-migration.ts:312`). Managed books now install with `min(1000, max(150, entries + 20))` — exalted3e ships **133** entries and was being silently truncated at the default 100. Sent on both create and update (one shared body), so a reinstall also lifts a book previously created at 100. Not addressed, and flagged rather than assumed: `tokenBudget` can truncate independently of entry count — a sizing question for the replan.
- **C5 — note only.** `findSheetContainer`/`hideBuiltInAttributesPanel` now carry a comment documenting that they anchor on English string literals, and that on a non-English locale the sheet floats unanchored and the built-in Attributes panel is never hidden (cosmetic/layout degradation, no crash, no data loss). Fix needs a locale-independent anchor and is deferred.
- **No bundle rebuilds.** C4 changes the install-time request body, not bundle data; `git diff` confirms zero `bundle.json` changes. Validators remain 16/16.
- Probes: C3 and C4 were extracted into named, testable helpers (`mrrCustomToolDescription`, `mrrLorebookEntryLimit`) so they could be probed against the real loader rather than asserted in prose. 4 new probes — **R21-C4** (sizing and clamping across empty/133/2000), **R21-C4b** (exalted3e's real shipped bundle would no longer truncate), **R21-C3** (empty, whitespace, null, undefined, kept, and 500-clamped), **R21-C2** (no entry-level `tags` remain on any entry write; book-level retained) — plus the re-scoped R20-F1-2. 23 probes in this harness, 246 across the suite.
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` clean; both `new Function` wrapper evals parse clean; `validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14; forbidden-API grep = 0; all ten probe harnesses green (246 probes total, up from 242).

### Fixed — round 20: THE READ-PATH FIX — agent output never reached the narrator (preset-owned placement, v2.4.0+)

- **The defect.** Since engine v2.4.0 a roleplay preset OWNS agent placement. A pre_generation agent's context injection is delivered ONLY by substituting a token minted for an `agent_data` marker section whose `markerConfig.agentType` matches the agent's TYPE (`generate.routes.ts:2465-2492`). With no match and the preset assembler having run, `splitRuntimeHandledAgentInjections` is called with `omitUnmatched: presetOwnsAgentPlacement` (`:5274`, flag set true at `:2589`) and the output is moved to `omittedInjections`, then dropped (`:5276-5278`) — **silently, with no depth-0 fallback** (that path exists only when the assembler did NOT run). Net effect: our agents ran every turn, billed tokens, and wrote healthy-looking rows into `agent_runs`, while contributing **nothing** to the narrator's prompt. Regression window `91ba44fd2`; long-term-memory has an explicit escape hatch at `:5443`, custom agents have none.
- **F1 — PER-ROLE AGENT TYPES, and a correction to what `activeAgentIds` has always meant.** Every MRR agent shared one `type` (`mrr-overlay-v1`). Two engine mechanisms are type-keyed and both broke under that: per-chat activation (`perChatAgentSet` is built VERBATIM from `chatMeta.activeAgentIds`, no id→type mapping anywhere — `generate.routes.ts:1521-1522` — and gated `if (hasPerChatAgentList && !perChatAgentSet.has(cfg.type)) continue;`, `agent-resolution.ts:405`), and injection placement (one token per type, consumed on first substitution, so only the FIRST of seven injections could ever land). **The finding:** `reconcileActiveAgents` has always pushed agent-config ROW IDS, and a row id can never match `cfg.type` — the only other `perChatAgentSet` read (`:503`) iterates `BUILT_IN_AGENTS`, whose `.id` IS the type, so no id-keyed path exists at all. Our agents ran only because the ENGINE's own toggle writes TYPES (list built via `agentConfigsByType.get(a.id)`, `ChatSettingsDrawer.tsx:1485-1499`; appended at `:3589`); one shared type is exactly why one toggle switched on all seven. Our row ids were inert passengers whose only effect was making `hasPerChatAgentList` true — which switches the per-chat filter ON. Fixed: gmAgent keeps `mrr-overlay-v1` for continuity, additionalAgents get `mrr-overlay-v1-<role-slug>`, and reconcile unions TYPES on both the forward and rebind paths (rebind also drops the old ruleset's stale legacy row ids). Discovery is unaffected — `findManagedAgent`, the uninstall sweep and the sole-writer filter all match SETTINGS tags, never `type`, which is what lets a migration reinstall update the seven legacy agents in place with zero orphans (probe R20-F1-2).
- **F2 — injectability settings, stamped at build time.** Every bundle's agents now carry an explicit `resultType: "context_injection"` (runtime-critical: `runtime-agent-sections.ts:122`) and `injectAsSection` (client-side: it is what puts an agent in Preset Editor → Add Section → Agent Sections, `PresetEditor.tsx:1245-1254`, a strict `=== true` boolean check). **Mutator call: `injectAsSection: false`, deliberately.** Its output is `[mrr-state: ...]` tags (or the literal "NO TAG DIRECTIVE") addressed to THIS EXTENSION, which reads them through the runs poller — a path entirely independent of preset placement, so injecting them buys the read path nothing. And it carries real risk: feeding raw tag syntax into the narrator's context invites the narrator to echo tags, which is not hypothetical — round 11 diagnosed a live triple-apply (−15 instead of −5) caused by non-mutator agents echoing tag text, the reason the sole-writer filter exists. The pre-input-transformer is also excluded (it rewrites the USER's input). Parallel-phase overlays are left unmarked because `:120` rejects any phase but `pre_generation` — marking them would be a lie the runtime ignores.
- **F3 — preset marker assist, offered and never silent.** New action in **Manage MRR Agents**: *Add agent sections to active preset*. It resolves the chat's real preset, refuses politely when there is none and no default (the server has NO default fallback, so sections would be inert), offers to attach the default when appropriate, skips agent types that already have a marker (idempotent), confirms with the preset's actual name before writing, and turns the stock preset's 409 into "save as copy" guidance. **Two shape facts from the brief were wrong and would have shipped broken:** the route is `/api/prompts`, not `/api/presets` (`/api/chat-presets` is an unrelated subsystem, `routes/index.ts:70`); and sections must be WRITTEN with real booleans and a real `markerConfig` object — the strings `"true"` are what you READ BACK (`prompts.storage.ts:329-335` does no deserialization), while `createPromptSectionSchema` is `z.boolean()` with no coercion (`prompt.schema.ts:170-188`), so posting `"true"` is a 400. We also deliberately do NOT call `PUT /:id/sections/reorder`: it rewrites EVERY section's `injectionOrder` to `i*100` and would reshuffle a hand-tuned preset. Section body mirrors `PresetEditor.tsx:1265-1294` including `content: "{{agent::<type>}}"` — the assembler discards a marker section with empty content (`assembler.ts:844-890`). We apply the SERVER's eligibility rules ourselves (type in activeAgentIds, pre_generation, context_injection) because the editor's picker checks only `injectAsSection` and would happily create sections the server never fills.
- **F4 — cosmetic only, and labelled as such in the code.** `pickDefaultConnection` now prefers the connection flagged `defaultForAgents` (accepting boolean or string), keeping the single-connection fallback verbatim. This silences the every-turn "no connection configured" notice, which is a **billing/attribution notice, not an error** (`agent-resolution.ts:233-257`) and was a red herring throughout this investigation — it was never the cause of missing agent output.
