- **F5 — docs.** All 11 ruleset `INSTALL.md` files (of 16 rulesets; the other 5 ship no INSTALL.md), `README.md`, and `releases/1.0.0/INSTALL-GUIDE.md` gained the per-game preset requirement, stated plainly: **this step is not optional and skipping it fails silently** — agents run, bill tokens, and show healthy run history while the narrator sees nothing. Both the one-click assist and the manual Preset Editor path are documented, along with the roleplay-only scope, why the State Mutator deliberately has no section, and that the connection warning is harmless. All 16 bundles rebuilt (agent settings changed); validators 16/16.
- Probes added: new harness `r20-agents-probes.mjs`, eval-extracted via `assemble-r20.mjs` (12 pieces). 19 probes: **R20-F1-1..F1-5** (type derivation; the 7-agent migration reinstall with zero orphans; real-type reads; reconcile writing TYPES not ids while preserving foreign entries; rebind swapping types and dropping stale legacy ids), **R20-F2-1/F2-2** (all 16 built bundles carry correct settings; the injectable filter excludes mutator, parallel-phase and non-context_injection agents), **R20-F3-1..F3-6** (exact marker body with BOOLEAN `isMarker`; JSON-string `markerConfig` parsing; idempotency both ways; consent declined writes nothing; no-preset binds the default; 409 becomes guidance), **R20-F4** (connection preference), and the **R20-FUNNEL** set — a labelled mechanical model of the engine's delivery semantics reproducing the 1-of-7 collapse pre-fix (and 0-of-7 with no marker at all, Corey's actual state) versus full delivery post-fix.
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` clean; both `new Function` wrapper evals parse clean; `npm run validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14; forbidden-API grep = 0; all ten probe harnesses green (242 probes total across the suite, up from 223).

### Fixed — round 19 retest 8: "cut once, then never again" — the runs poller's in-flight latch had no release path (BUG-3)

- **Retest-8 confirmed round 18's re-anchoring works at the detection layer**: D&D logged `swipe-detect: msgId=iWGvlYHPTpyJ7Vc3jW6Pm buckets=true idx=1 jChat=oU2GjllG jBuckets=7` — a bucket found on a real DOM assistant message at a nonzero swipe index, which is exactly what BUG-2 was supposed to make possible and what seventeen prior rounds never achieved. The remaining symptom was different: the FIRST mutation applies, then the poller emits **nothing at all** for the rest of the session — zero `applied` lines, zero of round 18's per-run diagnostics.
- **Root cause: `runsPollInFlight` is a latch set at the top of `pollCustomAgentRuns` and cleared ONLY in the promise chain's terminal `.then`. Two paths reach neither.** Both reproduced end to end against the real extracted loader (probes R19-S1-NEG / R19-S2-NEG), and both produce the observed symptom exactly:
  - **H1 — a request that never settles.** Nothing in this poller has a timeout, in contrast to `hydrateStore`, which races `marinara.storage.get()` against 8000ms precisely because a hung `get()` bricked init (round-5 FIX-8). One stalled `/agents/runs` or `/chats/:id/messages` request and the poller is dead for the session.
  - **H2 — a synchronous throw between `runsPollInFlight = true` and the promise handoff.** The prologue is not promise-protected: `loadProcessedMessageIds` and `loadProcessedRunIds` both touch storage (round 16 *added* the first of those to this exact window), and a synchronously-throwing `apiFetch` lands in the same gap. The throw escapes into `watchRouteChanges`' interval callback with the latch still true. Note the latch is global, so a throw while polling one chat kills polling for every chat.
- **Neither hazard was introduced by round 18** — H1 has existed since the poller was written, H2 was widened by round 16 — but either one reproduces retest-8's symptom, which is why the round-18 defer branch could not be cleared by inspection alone. It has now been cleared by direct test (below).
- **Fix, two halves, no new dependencies.** (a) A stall deadline: if a cycle has held the latch longer than `MRR_RUNS_POLL_STALL_MS = 30000` (20 poll ticks), the next call force-releases it, warns once, and proceeds — self-healing rather than permanently dead. Deliberately a deadline check rather than a timer: the poller has no injected timer API, and a deadline cannot itself be cancelled or garbage-collected. (b) A `try`/`catch` around the entire synchronous prologue (now factored into `mrrPollCustomAgentRunsInner`) that releases the latch and warns instead of letting the throw escape. A monotonic `runsPollCycleToken` keeps a **zombie** cycle — one that stalled, was force-released, and settles much later — from either clearing a newer cycle's latch or applying its stale results on top of the live one (probe R19-S3).
- **The defer branch is EXONERATED as the cause, by test rather than by argument.** The round-18 defer→retry loop was traced end to end for polls 2..N and reproduced across three consecutive turns (probe R19-M1): a deferred run stays unprocessed, so it re-enters the tag-bearing-candidates list on every subsequent poll, which forces a fresh `fetchSwipeIndexMap` call and therefore a freshly rebuilt anchor map every cycle — there is no path where a poll cycle skips the fetch and leaves deferred runs unexamined. Turns 1, 2 and 3 each defer while the reply is pending and each apply under their own `A<n>:0` bucket once it lands. **Engine ordering also verified, not assumed:** `listMessagesPaginated` (`chats.storage.ts:1236-1270`) selects `desc(createdAt), desc(id)` then `.reverse()`s, returning the newest N in ASCENDING order with a correct absolute `rowid` — so the just-inserted assistant row is always present and always sorts last, and "at or after" can never land on nothing while the reply exists. `listCustomRunsForChat` (`agents.storage.ts:410-431`) is `desc(createdAt)` limited to 200 max, so the newest run is always inside the `?limit=50` window.
- **First-cut poisoning check: none found.** Audited every piece of state the first successful resolution writes — `mrrMsgAnchorMap`/`mrrMsgAnchorLastId`/`mrrMsgAnchorChatId` are wholly overwritten by each fetch, `mrrRunAnchorDeferrals` is read only inside the defer branch and never gates resolution, `processedRunIds` is per-run-id, and `mrrProcessedTextMemo` is DOM-path only. The multi-turn probe (R19-M1) is the positive proof: turn 2 and turn 3 resolve and apply exactly like turn 1.
- **Deferral is no longer silent (the diagnostic gap that made this round necessary).** A deferred run previously produced no output whatsoever, so "every run is parked waiting for its assistant anchor" and "the poller is dead" were *both* just silence in the console — indistinguishable. `finishRunsPoll` now emits one line per poll cycle that has at least one deferral: `runs-poller: N run(s) deferred awaiting assistant anchor (poll K/200)`. The 200-deferral backstop now warns **every time it trips** (no longer warn-once, and no longer sharing the `none` case's latch), naming the run, the elapsed wait, and the fact that the bucket can never be swipe-reverted — it means a real mutation lost its revert anchor, which should never be quiet. A null `runId` also no longer shares one deferral counter across unrelated runs.
- **Fresh-chat ruleset stamp (`reconcileActiveAgents: rebound chat sVw9… from dnd5e to exalted3e` on a brand-new chat) — investigated, NOT a stale-chat-id bug, no code change.** `reconcileActiveAgents` captures `chatId` once at entry (`:14090`), before its `Promise.all`, and both PATCH sites (`:14136`, `:14172`) use that capture — the read and the write are guaranteed to address the same chat. Two mechanisms can legitimately put a `dnd5e` stamp on a chat the user considers brand new. (1) **Ours, by design:** the backfill branch (`:14163` `needsPatch = added > 0 || !stamp`, `:14170`) stamps ANY unstamped chat with the currently-active ruleset, and `watchRouteChanges` (`:17709`) fires it within one 1500 ms tick of the chat becoming active — so a chat created while dnd5e was active IS a dnd5e chat within seconds, and the later rebind is round-12's F1 fix working correctly. The `state.chatId` lag after chat creation points the *wrong way* to cause this: for up to 1500 ms `state.chatId` still names the OLD chat, so we can never stamp a NEW chat prematurely. (2) **Engine-side, shared ownership:** chat settings profiles snapshot the WHOLE chat metadata (`ChatSettingsDrawer.tsx:3890-3896`, `metadata: { ...metadata }`) and sanitize it through a **denylist** (`chat-preset.ts:46`, ~40 keys, no `mrr*` entry), then **replace** chat metadata on apply (`chat-presets.storage.ts:313-336`) — and the starred profile auto-applies to every new roleplay/conversation chat (`ChatSidebar.tsx:671`, `HomeNewChatLauncher.tsx:58`, `use-start-chat-from-character.ts:64`). So a profile saved while a dnd5e chat was open carries our stamp forward onto every later new chat. Chat *creation* itself is exonerated (`chats.storage.ts:788-802` builds a fixed literal; only `conversationTimeZone` is inherited), as are character cards, templates, and row reuse; `mrrChatRulesetId` appears nowhere in the engine tree. Branch (`chats.routes.ts:3777-3781`) and scene-fork (`scene.routes.ts:240-247`) clone metadata through the same denylist shape. **Discriminating check for Corey's actual chat, before any code is written:** `GET /chats/sVw9…` → a set `metadata.appliedChatPresetId` means path (2); `GET /chat-presets` → any profile whose `settings.metadata.mrrChatRulesetId === "dnd5e"` is conclusive proof of (2). Both clean ⇒ path (1), which is not a defect. Not fixed this round: neither cause is a clear-cut bug in our code, and the real design question — whether a chat-scoped key belongs in an engine namespace whose default classification is "profile-scoped and portable" — deserves a deliberate decision rather than a reflex patch.
- **Fixed en route (adjacent to BUG-3, found during the same audit): `syncSheetToChat` cross-chat read-modify-write.** It read `state.chatId` THREE separate times — the GET (`:13261`), the PATCH inside the `.then` (`:13268`), and the log (`:13273`). A chat switch during the GET meant chat A's `customTrackerFields` were read, merged with the active character's fresh slice, and then PATCHed onto **chat B**, clobbering chat B's own tracker fields (including any foreign/user-created ones) with chat A's merged set. Now captured once at entry as `syncChatId`, exactly the pattern `reconcileActiveAgents:14090` already used. Completing the write against the captured chat is correct — the sync was initiated for that chat — so consistency alone fixes it; no bail needed. Probes R19-X1 / R19-X1-NEG (the negative control PATCHes `/chats/chatB` after GETting `/chats/chatA`, reproducing the clobber).
- Probes added: `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` re-extracted to `assembled-b2r-fragment-r19.js` (61 pieces — round 18's 58 plus the latch var block, `mrrNow`, `mrrPollCustomAgentRunsInner`, and `ROUTE_POLL_MS`, the last of which the extraction itself caught as a genuine missing dependency of the new backstop warn). All 80 round-18 probes re-verified green. 8 new: **R19-S1/S1-NEG** (hung request — recovery vs. permanent death), **R19-S2/S2-NEG** (prologue throw — recovery vs. permanent death), **R19-S3** (zombie cycle isolation), **R19-M1** (three-turn defer-then-apply regression guard — the "cut once, never again" scenario), **R19-L1** (the per-poll deferral line), **R19-L2** (the loud backstop warn), plus **R19-X1/X1-NEG** for the `syncSheetToChat` fix above. Three new mechanically-derived negative controls (`make-r19-buggy.mjs` steps 5/6/7 — two single-line disables of the latch-release mechanisms and one exact three-usage revert of the chat-id capture, never hand-typed). 90 probes total in this file (up from 80).
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` clean; both `new Function` wrapper evals parse clean; `npm run validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14; forbidden-API grep = 0; all nine probe harnesses green (223 probes total across the suite, up from 213).

### Fixed — round 18 retest 7: ORPHANED BUCKETS — the runs poller was anchoring every mutation to the wrong message (ROOT CAUSE, engine-verified)

- **The bug seventeen rounds of instrumentation were circling.** Retest-7's D&D evidence was a journal that saved 6 healthy buckets under the correct chat key while EVERY observed DOM message logged `skipped — no tags, no buckets`. Both facts were true: the buckets existed, and no DOM message could see them, because they were filed under message ids that no rendered assistant message carries.
- **ENGINE GROUND TRUTH (traced at `~/Marinara-Engine` HEAD 2.4.4-dev, every `agentsStore.saveRun` call site read).** `saveRun`'s `messageId` (`services/storage/agents.storage.ts:295-325`) is **not** uniformly the assistant message. It is bound **per agent phase**, and — contrary to the working hypothesis going into this round — the split is **phase-vs-phase, not roleplay-vs-game** (no `chatMode` guard exists on any of these blocks):
  - custom **`pre_generation`** agents → `generate.routes.ts:5197-5215`: `const latestUserMessageForPreGenRun = [...allChatMessages].reverse().find((m) => m.role === "user"); ... messageId: preGenRunMessageId` — **the triggering USER message**.
  - custom `parallel`/`post_processing` agents → `generate.routes.ts:8414` (`const messageId = (lastSavedMsg as any)?.id ?? ""`), consumed at `:8442`/`:8486` — the assistant reply.
  - custom lorebook read-behind agents → `generate.routes.ts:4858-4862`, `:8276` — a deliberately **historical** (much older) message id.
  Every MRR ruleset installs its State Mutator as `phase: "pre_generation"` (all sixteen `bundle.json` files), so **every** mutator run this poller has ever read was stamped with the user turn's id. `processChatMessage` discards any node without the `mari-message-assistant` class long before its bucket check, so a user-turn-keyed bucket is structurally invisible to swipe detection — it can never be reverted, and it never even produced a "skipped" diagnostic line to hint at itself.
- **Why EX3 looked healthy and D&D did not.** EX3's narrator emits inline `[mrr-state:]` tags, so its DOM path produced correctly-keyed buckets (`jBuckets=11`) that masked its equally-orphaned poller buckets. D&D is poller-only, so nothing masked it. The round-17 brief's read that "EX3's revert machinery is working and merely waiting on the engine" was half right: its DOM-path revert is fine; its poller-path buckets were orphaned exactly like D&D's.
- **No provisional ids.** Ruled out explicitly (the `__streaming__` saga made this worth checking): message ids are server-generated inside the insert (`services/storage/chats.storage.ts:1299-1311`, `const id = newId()` then `db.insert(messages)`), never client-supplied and never rewritten. Runs are persisted with the real row id; there is no temp→real swap anywhere in the engine's run path.
- **Fix — re-anchor the poller to the DOM-visible assistant message, by ROLE, not by phase.** A run-anchor map now rides along on the *same* `GET /chats/:id/messages` request `fetchSwipeIndexMap` already makes (no extra call, same coalescing, same recent window): `mrrBuildMsgAnchorMap` walks the window once in reverse and maps every message id to the nearest **visible assistant** message at or after it. A run whose `messageId` already IS a visible assistant message resolves to itself (the `post_processing` case — a no-op); anything else maps forward. This is the engine's own convention for pre-generation runs — `generate.routes.ts:7896-7900` anchors built-in Narrative Director pre-gen runs to "the first saved assistant message from this turn," with that reasoning in its comment; the engine simply never applied it to *custom* pre-gen agents. Keying on `role` rather than on a hard-coded phase table means the fix stays correct if the engine ever moves which phase anchors where.
- **"Visible" is load-bearing:** `generate.routes.ts:7182-7197` can create an assistant row with `extra.hiddenFromUser: true` (the command-only anchor) and `ChatRoleplaySurface.tsx:2231` does `if (isMessageHiddenFromUser(msg)) return null` — it is never rendered, carries no `data-message-id`, and would orphan a bucket just as thoroughly as a user turn. Skipped as an anchor candidate.
- **The dedup anchor moves with the journal key — required, not stylistic.** Two independent invariants force it. (1) `applyStateTagsWithDedup`'s cross-transport contract (FIX-1, round 5): the DOM path anchors on the assistant's `data-message-id`, so "a tag present in BOTH sources applies exactly once" holds only if this transport passes the *same* id — under the un-remapped raw mid it silently did not, and **an inline narrator tag plus the mutator's own copy of it double-applied** (a second live defect this fix closes, reproduced in probe R18-C1-NEG). (2) `mrrRevertBucket` derives the dedup anchor to clear from the *bucket key* (`mrrClearDedupKeysForBucket(mrrAnchorFromBucketKey(bucketKey), ...)`, `:14862`) — if the key's anchor portion and the dedup anchor ever disagree, revert silently fails to clear `appliedMutationKeys` and the swipe-back reapply is swallowed by the dedup gate. The two are required by construction to be the same string, so one resolved value feeds both.
- **Four documented resolution outcomes, each deliberate.** `resolved` → remap. `window` (id outside the fetched recent window, or map unusable) → keep the pre-round-18 plain anchor, unchanged legacy degrade. `defer` (the run's message is the newest in the chat and has no reply yet — the **normal** state for a pre-gen run, since the engine persists it at `:5205` strictly before creating the assistant row at `:7354`) → leave the run UNPROCESSED and retry next poll. `none` (later messages exist but none is a visible assistant) → degrade to the legacy anchor immediately rather than wait five minutes for something that will never arrive. A `defer` is bounded by `MRR_RUN_ANCHOR_MAX_DEFERRALS = 200` (~5 min at `ROUTE_POLL_MS`); past the cap the mutation applies under the raw anchor with a warn rather than being lost — an orphaned bucket still mutates the sheet correctly (only swipe-revert is lost), whereas deferring forever would silently drop the mutation, which is strictly worse.
- **Defensive degrade on a missing `role` field.** If not one row in the messages response carries a string `role`, the anchor map is marked unusable and the poller keeps exactly its pre-round-18 behavior (with one warn) — the same posture every other degrade in this file takes. Deliberately not a deferral: guessing would defer every run for 200 polls before applying anyway.
- **Instrumentation (TASK 2), shipped independently of the fix.** (a) `pollCustomAgentRuns` now logs one line **per applied run** — `runs-poller: run <runId> mid=<rawMessageId> → anchor=<remapped> (<status>) journalKey=<key>` — logged before the apply so a throwing row still testifies to the key it was about to use. (b) A new once-per-session orphan detector: when N distinct DOM observations have ALL taken the "no tags, no buckets" early return while zero got past it and the journal nonetheless holds buckets, one line prints the journal's unmatched bucket keys against a LIVE `document.querySelectorAll("[data-message-id]")` scan, each rendered id suffixed `(a)` for assistant / `(o)` otherwise — so "matched a user turn" and "matched nothing at all" are distinguishable at a glance. The DOM side is read live rather than accumulated because the initial sweep's already-processed branch re-wraps messages without ever calling `processChatMessage`, and an accumulated set would manufacture false orphans.
- **COUPLINGS.md row 2 corrected.** Its "messageId == narrator `data-message-id`" line — a 2026-08-22 dump-stage eyeball — never actually held for a `pre_generation` agent. That un-audited claim is what let this survive seventeen rounds.
- Probes added: `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` re-extracted to `assembled-b2r-fragment-r18.js` (58 pieces — round 17's 53 plus the anchor-map var block, `mrrBuildMsgAnchorMap`, `mrrIsHiddenFromUser`, `mrrResolveRunDomAnchor`, `mrrMaybeLogOrphanedBuckets`). All 62 round-17 probes re-verified green against the new extraction with **zero fixture changes** (the `role`-missing defensive degrade is what preserves them — a real design property, not a test accommodation). 18 new probes: **R18-A1/A2/A3/A4** cover the anchor map (forward mapping, hidden-message skip, rowid-normalized ordering, all five resolution outcomes); **R18-B1/B2** prove the fix end to end (a user-turn-stamped run journals under the assistant id and is visible to DOM detection; a swipe on the reply actually reverts, 10−4 → swipe → 10−1 instead of stacking to 5); **R18-C1** proves cross-transport dedup still collides after re-anchoring; **R18-D1** proves an assistant-anchored run is unchanged by the remap; **R18-D2/D3/D4/D5** cover defer-then-retry, the deferral backstop, the immediate-degrade case, and the missing-`role` degrade; **R18-E1/E2/E3** cover both new diagnostics including the must-stay-silent case. **Three mechanically-derived negative controls** (`make-r18-buggy.mjs`, never hand-typed) reproduce the live defects against the pre-fix fragment: **R18-B1-NEG** (bucket keyed to the user turn, invisible to the assistant message), **R18-B2-NEG** (both swipes stack, 10−4−1=5 — the "swipe doesn't undo the damage" report), **R18-C1-NEG** (identical tag double-applies across transports, 10−3−3=4). 80 probes total in this file (up from 62).
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` clean; both `new Function` wrapper evals (bare + `"use strict"; async (marinara) =>`) parse clean; `npm run validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14 (unchanged since B1); forbidden-API grep (`storage\.(delete|clear)\(|apiFetch\("/extensions/`) = 0; all nine probe harnesses green (213 probes total across the suite, up from 195).

### Fixed — round 17 retest 6: `__streaming__` placeholder-id exclusion, DOM-path journal-scoping gap round 16 missed, journal-lifecycle instrumentation

- **TASK 1 (certain fix) — engine placeholder message ids.** Live evidence: `swipe-detect: msgId=__streaming__ skipped — no tags, no buckets`. Confirmed against `~/Marinara-Engine` client source: the engine stamps a synthetic, client-only `data-message-id` on the in-flight streaming message element while tokens are still arriving — it is not a real DB row, and is REUSED across every in-flight generation on that surface. Exactly two literals exist (a full-package `id: "__` grep found no others): `"__streaming__"` (RP mode, `ChatRoleplaySurface.tsx:384` — the surface both round-16/17 retest sessions ran in) and `"__conversation_live_stream__"` (the separate "conversation" chat mode, `ConversationView.tsx:903` — not yet seen live, but included defensively since `ConversationMessage.tsx` stamps the identical `mari-message-assistant` class + `data-message-id` attribute our selectors match on). Game mode's `GameSurface.tsx` renders its own message list with neither component — no placeholder risk there, still untouched. Server-side confirmed NOT at risk: every `agentsStore.saveRun(...)` call site across the engine's routes passes a real, persisted messageId — never one of these literals — so the runs-transport poller can never receive a placeholder id; this is purely a DOM-path concern. **Fix:** a new `mrrIsPlaceholderMessageId(msgId)` gate closes all three DOM consumption points before any memo/processed/journal/transition state is ever touched — `schedule()` (earliest point, also skips the debounce-scheduling churn a rapidly-streaming placeholder would otherwise cause), `processChatMessage()` itself (defense in depth), and the initial-sweep loop (extension load racing an in-progress generation).
- **TASK 2 — the evaporation persists past round 16 because round 16 only protected the POLLER path, never the DOM/narrator path.** Re-audited every assignment to `mrrMutationJournal`/`mrrMutationJournalChatId` and every caller of `loadProcessedMessageIds` (still exactly 3: `pollCustomAgentRuns`, `watchChatMessages`' init sweep, `watchRouteChanges`' switch detector) plus `watchRouteChanges`' own tick ordering (poller runs before the switch detector each tick, confirmed — `state.chatId = newId` at the switch detector precedes its own `loadProcessedMessageIds(state.chatId)` call synchronously, with no yield between, so no ordering gap was found there). **Static proof:** `mrrMutationJournalChatId` only ever changes via `loadProcessedMessageIds`/`mrrLoadMutationJournal`, and both are only ever called with `state.chatId`'s own current value — under the current single-threaded call graph, `mrrMutationJournalChatId` and `state.chatId` cannot diverge once genuinely synced, so the poller's SECOND async hop (after round 16's top-of-function load) is provably safe. **The one asymmetry the audit DID find:** `processChatMessage`'s async apply callback (the DOM/narrator path — this is EX3's `state-mutator: applied 2/2 from message RUlDFro:0` evidence line, confirmed via `log(...)` call-site grep to be exclusively this path, never the poller's) checks only `state.chatId !== scanChatId` — proving the active chat hasn't changed — but never verified the journal had actually been LOADED for that chat, unlike the poller which round 16 fixed. **Fix (structural, applied symmetrically):** `processChatMessage`'s callback now calls `loadProcessedMessageIds(scanChatId)` right after its stale-chat guard, mirroring round 16's poller-top fix exactly (idempotent when already loaded). `pollCustomAgentRuns` also gained an explicit verify-and-self-heal check immediately before its own apply (`if (mrrMutationJournalChatId !== pollChatId) { warn(...); loadProcessedMessageIds(pollChatId); }`) as a loud backstop per the brief's exact prescription — even though the static proof says it shouldn't be reachable, the warn (if it ever fires live) directly disproves that proof and pinpoints the divergence the audit missed.
- **Honest finding on EX3's specific evidence:** the diagnostic log's `buckets=` field is computed as `!isTagBearing && mrrJournalHasBucketsFor(msgId)` — for a TAG-BEARING message (EX3's narrator always is), this is `false` UNCONDITIONALLY by short-circuit, regardless of whether a bucket genuinely exists. EX3's pasted `buckets=false` line is therefore ambiguous on its own and does not, by itself, prove evaporation — it reads identically whether the bucket survived or not. **Fixed as part of the requested instrumentation:** the log's `buckets=` now uses a real, unconditional scan (`hasBucketsForLog`) for tag-bearing messages too, so the next live log is decisive rather than structurally uninformative in exactly EX3's case.
- **Requested instrumentation (TASK 2/3), all added:** (a) `mrrSaveMutationJournal` now logs `"journal: saved <bucketCount> bucket(s) → <lsKey>"` on every successful save. (b) The swipe-detect diagnostic now also prints `jChat=<mrrMutationJournalChatId> jBuckets=<Object.keys(...).length>` alongside the corrected real-scan `buckets=` field. (c) `mrrLoadMutationJournal` now warns loudly — naming both chatIds and both bucket counts — whenever a load is about to replace a non-empty in-memory journal with an empty persisted one (in both its null-chatId reset branch and its normal real-chatId branch), the exact "wipe moment" the lifecycle theory predicts. Combined with Corey's live localStorage journal-key dump (next round), these three should make the mechanism undeniable one way or the other on the next live run.
- **This round's honest status:** the DOM-path asymmetry is a real, structurally-fixed gap (round 16 never protected it) and is the leading candidate for EX3's continued evaporation, but the static audit could not construct a live divergence sequence with 100% certainty given the current call graph — consistent with the brief's own acceptable outcome ("the honest 'instrumented, next log decides'"). Filed as "fixed the found asymmetry + instrumented for certainty," not "root-caused with a reproduced live trace."
- Probes added: `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` re-extracted to `assembled-b2r-fragment-r17.js` (53 pieces — round 16's 51 plus `MRR_PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE_IDS` + `mrrIsPlaceholderMessageId`, now exposed). All 50 round-16 probes re-verified; **B1-3** updated (round-17's new backstop now independently self-heals the exact scenario B1-3 tests, so its assertion flips to prove that — a positive finding, not a broken control) and a fresh **B1-3b** added stripping BOTH round-16's and round-17's poller-side protections together, proving their union is what's actually load-bearing. 12 new round-17 probes: **R17-P1/P2** prove total-bail (zero fetch, zero log, zero state touched) for both placeholder ids, even with tag-bearing content; **R17-P3** confirms exact-match-only (no substring false positives); **R17-P4** confirms a real message right after a skipped placeholder observation applies completely normally; **R17-P5** proves the DOM-path fix's apply→journal→immediate-reload property (mirroring B1-1 for the narrator path); **R17-P6** is the negative control proving the DOM-path fix line is itself load-bearing; **R17-P7** confirms the new save-log line; **R17-P8/P8a/P8b** confirm the new wipe-warn fires exactly when it should (and doesn't when the outgoing was empty or the incoming was non-empty); **R17-P9** confirms the corrected `buckets=` field reports real ground truth for a tag-bearing message with a genuine bucket. 62 probes total in this file (up from 50).
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` / `new Function` parse / schema `JSON.parse` all clean; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14 (unchanged since B1); `console.log(` count = 2 (unchanged, the `log()` wrapper's own calls); `npm run validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; all nine probe harnesses green (184 probes total across the suite, up from 172).

### Fixed — round 16 retest 5: journal-lifecycle hole (BUG-1) silently wiped swipe-revert buckets; BUG-3 confirmed downstream, not a separate defect; BUG-2 confirmed correct (no fix needed)

- **BUG-1 (primary; confirmed root cause of D&D's total swipe-revert silence via evidence logs DND_1731/EX3_1726/EX3_1732): `pollCustomAgentRuns(chatId)` had no guard ensuring the mutation journal was ever loaded/scoped for `chatId` before journaling mutations.** `mrrLoadMutationJournal` (the ONLY place that ever loads/scopes `mrrMutationJournal` to a chatId) has exactly one caller, `loadProcessedMessageIds` — and until this round nothing guaranteed THAT had run for the poller's chatId before the poller proceeded. Concretely reachable at startup: `state.chatId` can be null very briefly while Marinara's own app is still initializing; `watchChatMessages`' init-sweep `loadProcessedMessageIds(state.chatId)` call, made with that null, explicitly sets `mrrMutationJournalChatId = null`. If `pollCustomAgentRuns` then fires with a NOW-valid chatId (as it does on every `watchRouteChanges` tick, unconditionally, every `ROUTE_POLL_MS` = 1500ms) before `watchRouteChanges`' own "chat changed" detection has run its real load, `mrrJournalMutation` still succeeds at pushing a bucket into the in-memory journal (`mrrCurrentJournalBucketKey` is set independently by `applyStateTagsWithDedup`, with zero chatId awareness) — but `mrrSaveMutationJournal` silently no-ops while `mrrMutationJournalChatId` is still falsy, so that bucket never persists. The instant the real per-chat load then runs, `mrrLoadMutationJournal` sees `mrrMutationJournalChatId` (still null) `!== chatId`, treats it as a genuine fresh load, and REPLACES the journal with whatever's on disk (nothing) — silently wiping the bucket the poller just created moments earlier. **Fix:** `pollCustomAgentRuns` now unconditionally calls `loadProcessedMessageIds(chatId)` (idempotent/cheap once already loaded, a real correct load the first time this chatId is ever seen by the poller) before doing anything else in the poll cycle. Also closed the two silent characteristics that made this hard to diagnose live: `mrrSaveMutationJournal` now warns when it runs with no chatId loaded (the write is discarded); `mrrJournalMutation` now warns when it fires with no active bucket key (naming the field, the entry is dropped).
- **BUG-2 (idx=0 timing) — confirmed NOT a bug; no code change.** Verified against `~/Marinara-Engine` source: `activeSwipeIndex` only updates on swipe NAVIGATION between EXISTING swipes (`PUT /:chatId/messages/:messageId/active-swipe`, `chats.routes.ts:3214-3220` → `storage.setActiveSwipe`, `chats.storage.ts:1790`, an immediate synchronous DB write) or at generation COMPLETION for a fresh regeneration (`chats.addSwipe(messageId, fullResponse)`, called only once the FULL streamed response text is known — `generate.routes.ts:7278` — implementation `chats.storage.ts:1744-1788`, which bumps `activeSwipeIndex` in the same write that inserts the new swipe row). It never updates incrementally mid-stream. Our `idx=0` observations during an active regeneration are therefore exactly correct/expected, matching the engine's own timing precisely — not a defect.
- **BUG-3 (D&D's total swipe-detect silence) — verified downstream of BUG-1, not a separate defect.** `processChatMessage`'s early-return gate (`if (!isTagBearing && !hasBuckets) return;`) sits BEFORE the pre-existing `"swipe-detect: msgId=... buckets=... idx=..."` diagnostic (inside `resolveSwipeIndexForMessage`'s async callback) — so a tag-less message (D&D's State Mutator output never lands in chat DOM, unlike EX3's narrator-echo architecture) whose runs-transport bucket had evaporated (BUG-1) hit a hard early return before the function ever reached the existing diagnostic, producing total silence with zero trace. Fixing BUG-1 alone makes a D&D-shaped message detectable again — verified by probe (see below), no additional detection-logic code needed. Added one new, cheap, once-per-msgId-per-session diagnostic in the early-return path itself (gated by a new `mrrSkippedDiagnosticWarned` tracker): `"swipe-detect: msgId=X skipped — no tags, no buckets"` — so a future BUG-3-shaped silence is self-explaining in the console instead of invisible. The existing diagnostic log is intentionally left in place, not moved above the gate (would fire on every user-message/narrator-chrome observation across the whole page — too chatty).
- **Interference check (requested, nothing found):** verified none of our `apiFetch` calls (poller tick, swipe-index resolution, agent reconciliation, auto-sync) are triggered synchronously by the user's own swipe/regenerate click — we have no click handlers on Marinara's UI at all; all our network activity is independently timed and debounced/coalesced (`ROUTE_POLL_MS` = 1500ms matches the observer's own 1500ms debounce). Nothing found that could interfere with the engine's own swipe/regenerate click path.
- **Scope note:** both retest sessions (D&D and EX3) ran in RP mode; the game-mode DOM-marker hypothesis originally floated for BUG-3 was dropped before investigation per Corey's clarification. Game mode remains untested for this journal-lifecycle path — not touched this round.
- Probes added: `b2r-revert-probes.mjs` re-extracted to `assembled-b2r-fragment-r16.js` (51 pieces, up from round 12's 51 — adds `mrrSkippedDiagnosticWarned`; footer now also exposes `mrrLoadMutationJournal`/`mrrSaveMutationJournal`/`mrrJournalMutation` directly plus `getJournalChatId`/`setJournalBucketKey`/`getSkippedDiagnosticWarned` accessors), all 42 existing probes re-verified with zero regressions, plus 8 new round-16 probes (50 total): **B1-1** reproduces the coordinator's exact ask — apply→journal→immediate-reload cycle keeps the bucket; **B1-2** reproduces the EXACT startup-race scenario from the brief (null-chatId init sweep → poller fires first with a now-valid chatId → later legitimate load) and confirms the bucket survives; **B1-3** is a genuine negative control — the identical B1-2 scenario replayed against a byte-for-byte copy of the r16 fragment with ONLY the fix's one line mechanically removed, proving the fix is load-bearing: the bucket is created in memory, the new warn fires, and the later legitimate load silently wipes it, exactly as the static trace predicted; **B1-4**/**B1-5** confirm the two new warns fire correctly and discard/drop the write as designed; **B3-1** confirms the new skip diagnostic fires exactly once per msgId even across repeated re-observations; **B3-2** confirms a tag-bearing message never reaches the skip diagnostic; **B3-3** (the money probe) reproduces the D&D architecture end-to-end — a tag-less message's bucket created via the runs-transport poller, with BUG-1's fix in effect, is later found via the normal swipe-detect log on DOM observation, not the skip diagnostic, confirming BUG-3 needed zero detection-logic changes beyond the diagnostic itself.
- Gate outputs after this round: `node --check` / `new Function` parse / schema `JSON.parse` all clean; `grep -c "localStorage\."` = 14 (unchanged since B1); zero forbidden greps; `npm run validate-rulesets` / `validate-bundles` both 16/16; all nine probe harnesses green (172 probes total across the suite, up from 164).

### Added — multi-mechanic dice: `resolution.additionalModes[]` + `resolutionId` routing (B18; Corey, 2026-08-23: "wire in multiple dice sets and let the agent choose which mechanic is relevant")

- A ruleset was previously locked to exactly ONE `resolution.mode`. It can now declare named, self-contained alternate mechanics via `resolution.additionalModes[]` — each `{id, label, mode, config, whenToUse}` where `mode`/`config` reuse the exact same per-mode shape `resolution` itself supports for that mode (any of the nine typed modes, mixed and matched). `skills[]`/`derivedStats[]` items gain an optional `resolutionId` binding a sheet roll button to a specific mechanic (or the literal `"primary"`); a roll made under a non-primary mechanic gets `mode=<id>` spliced into its `[dice: ...]` tag immediately after the `[dice: ` prefix, so the GM agent reads what actually happened instead of remembering what should have. **The dice never leave deterministic code** — the agent only ever requests a mechanic by id (via an auto-taught routing table, see below) and reads the resulting self-describing tag; the widget does 100% of the RNG and math, exactly as before.
- **Schema-structure finding:** `resolution`'s nine mode configs are inlined directly in its `oneOf` array with no `$defs`/`definitions` section to `$ref` — restructuring that already-shipped, validator-tested block (16 rulesets depend on it) was judged out of scope for a same-night, zero-regression build. Per the design's own explicit fallback, nine new `$defs.resolutionConfig*` entries HONESTLY DUPLICATE each mode's field set (properties + required, minus the `mode` const) rather than faking a `$ref` that doesn't exist — flagged with a `$comment` in the schema and a maintenance-hazard note in `COUPLINGS.md`'s Data-contract section: whoever next edits a mode in `resolution.oneOf` must update the matching `$defs.resolutionConfig*` by hand. `additionalModes[].id` uniqueness and `resolutionId` cross-references are validated in `tools/validate-ruleset.mjs` (`extraChecks`), not expressible as pure JSON Schema.
- **Widget:** `buildDice()` renders a mechanic selector (`<select>`, idiom-matched to the widget's existing controls — no prior tab/select pattern existed inside the dice widget itself to reuse) whenever `resolution.additionalModes` is non-empty; selecting a mechanic re-renders the SAME per-mode builder/roller functions the primary mode already uses (`buildRollUnderWidget`/`rollRollUnder`, etc.), parameterized by a new `mrrActiveResolutionConfig()` helper — no roller math was forked or duplicated. `state.diceActiveModeId` tracks the current selection; `state.diceBuiltModeId` tracks which mechanic the mounted widget DOM was actually built for, so switching mechanics (via the selector, or via a `resolutionId`-bound roll button) forces a rebuild instead of silently reusing the wrong widget's inputs. Weapon-attack and save rolls (`quickRollAttack`/`quickRollForSave`) are never `resolutionId`-routable by schema — both now explicitly force `diceActiveModeId` back to `"primary"` before prefilling, so a stale mechanic selection from an earlier skill roll can never leak into an attack/save's inputs or tag.
- **`[dice: mode=<id> ...]` tag injection is a single chokepoint** inside `finalizeRoll` (every roll function's tag funnels through it before reaching the player), gated on both a non-`"primary"` active mechanic AND the tag literally starting with `"[dice: "` — item-use/spell-cast/narrate tags built from other `finalizeRoll` call sites use different prefixes and are untouched. Primary-mode rolls and the 15 rulesets with no `additionalModes` never see `diceActiveModeId` set to anything but `"primary"`/unset, so this is a true no-op there (P1 zero-regression, probed against a seeded sweep replayed byte-for-byte against HEAD `cbcbc35`'s pre-B18 rollers).
- **Prompt teaching:** `buildSheetForPrompt` (overlay-agent prompts) and `buildFieldReferenceContent` (the narrator-visible field-reference lorebook entry) both append a compact routing block — mechanic id, label, `whenToUse`, and the skills/derivedStats bound to it — via one shared `mrrMechanicRoutingLines()` helper, ONLY when `additionalModes` is non-empty. Absent for all 15 other shipped rulesets; present only for OSE post-pilot.
- **Fix found and made along the way:** `quickRollForDerived`'s roll-under target derivation read `derived.formula` through the arithmetic-only `evalFormula` — correct for CoC/GURPS-style derived stats whose `formula` really is a small expression, but OSE's Open Doors/Listen/Surprise `formula` fields are prose GM hints ("X-in-6, roll 1d6, success on a result <= this value...") that `evalFormula` correctly fails to parse, silently prefilling a target of 0. Added a fallback to the stat's own stored/autocalc'd value (the number actually shown on the sheet) when the formula doesn't evaluate — zero-diff for every derivedStat whose `formula` DOES evaluate.
- **OSE pilot** (`rulesets/ose/`): `roll-under` mode's `diceFormula` is generic `XdY` notation (schema pattern `^[1-9][0-9]*d[1-9][0-9]*$`), so it supports a d6-in-X check (`"1d6"`) with zero roller-math changes — confirmed, not assumed, before wiring. Two `additionalModes` added: `thief-skills` (`roll-under`, `diceFormula: "1d100"`, bound to all six Thief skills) and `door-checks` (`roll-under`, `diceFormula: "1d6"`, bound to Open Doors/Listen/Surprise, which also each gained a self-referencing `rollFormula` so their roll buttons render at all). `resolution.mode` stays `single-roll` (d20 attack/save math) as primary — unchanged. The `lorebook.json` "Off-Widget Subsystems" entry is rewritten as "Dice Mechanic Routing," teaching the new on-widget flow instead of the old hand-adjudication instructions; `ruleset.json`'s `summary` updated to match. Bundle/agents rebuilt (`npm run build-bundles`/`build-agents` for `ose`); `agents.json` is byte-identical (additionalModes don't touch agent generation).
- Docs: `docs/AUTHORING-PHASE-6.md` §1 gains an "`resolution.additionalModes[]` — multi-mechanic dice (B18)" subsection (plus a pointer from the section's opening warning box); `docs/AUTHORING.md`'s Step 1 cross-links it. `releases/1.0.0/docs-for-ai/03-RULESET-SCHEMA.md`'s "Known schema limits" bullet ("One `resolution.mode` per ruleset") is marked LIFTED with the mechanism and the OSE example, plus the honest residual limit (a mechanic that isn't one of the nine typed modes still has no automation path).
- **CORRECTED round 9 (adversarial review findings 5/6/17 — honesty gap, still amending this entry, not released yet):** this entry's first bullet's "any of the nine typed modes, mixed and matched" and the schema-structure bullet's "nine new `$defs.resolutionConfig*` entries" were WRONG. Verified against the live loader, not assumed: only `dice-pool` and `roll-under` are actually wired end to end — tag-covered by the literal `"[dice: "` prefix `finalizeRoll`'s `mode=<id>` injection guards on, AND genuinely parameterized by `mrrActiveResolutionConfig()` in both builder and roller. `d100-percentile`/`dice-pool-sum`/`stance-modal-pool` ARE config-converted but tag under `"[d100: "`/`"[mrr-roll: "` — not `"[dice: "` — so an additionalMode using one of them would silently never carry a `mode=<id>` tag. `single-roll`/`2d6-stat`/`fate-ladder` are neither tag-covered nor config-converted; `narrative-handled` has no roller. The schema's `additionalModes[].mode` enum is now restricted to `dice-pool`/`roll-under` only (the other seven `$defs.additionalMode*`/`resolutionConfig*` wrapper defs removed, not just hidden); `mrrMechanicRoutingLines`' agent-facing text now says so explicitly ("more modes as they're converted"); OSE's pilot (`roll-under` only) is unaffected. Convert-or-restrict: no additional rollers were converted this round — restriction only.
- `MRR_STATE_TAG_VERSION` unaffected (still `1`, stamped B2-R/round 7) — B18 adds no new tag grammar, only an optional `mode=<id>` token inside the existing `[dice: ...]` tag shape.
- Probes: `b18-multimode-probes.mjs` (eval-extraction, 50 pieces from the live loader + 46 from `git show cbcbc35:...` for the P1 baseline) — 32/32 green, covering zero-regression (P1, with both a desynced-seed negative control and a real reintroduced-regression negative control), additionalMode roll math + tag correctness (P2, including a same-ruleset primary-mode roll carrying no `mode=` tag), `resolutionId` routing + widget-rebuild-on-switch (P3), schema/validator rejection of bad `mode` enums/duplicate ids/dangling `resolutionId`s with a valid-fixture negative control (P4), and routing-table presence exactly matching `additionalModes` presence across all 16 shipped rulesets (P5).

### Fixed — round-9 adversarial review batch (B2-R interaction bugs, B18 honesty gaps, poller/storage/journal correctness — not released yet)

- **B2-R headline bug (findings 1-4, one converging fix):** revert correctly restored the SHEET but left the cross-transport dedup gate (`appliedMutationKeys`, keyed off the RAW tag attrs' content signature) still remembering the reverted swipe's tag as applied — an incoming swipe with a CONTENT-IDENTICAL `[mrr-state:]` tag (different prose, same field/value) was silently swallowed, never re-applying, never re-journaling. `mrrRevertBucket` now also deletes the matching dedup keys per reverted entry. ~~Honest caveat, found while building this and reported rather than silently smoothed over: the mechanism the fix prescribes (`mrrBuildReplayAttrs(entry,"forward")` → the same normalize+sig pipeline the gate uses) reconstructs an ABSOLUTE attrs shape, which only matches the original tag's signature when that tag ALSO used an absolute form (`current=`/`value=`) — a `delta=` original tag computes a different signature entirely. Added a second candidate signature (the algebraically-equivalent `delta = next - prev`) for non-state numeric fields so a delta-authored original tag is ALSO matched whenever the mutation wasn't itself clamped; deleting a candidate whose key doesn't exist is a harmless no-op.~~ **SUPERSEDED round 10 (fix A) — see below; the reconstruction approach never matched real tags at all (`reason=`/`target=` participate in the sig, sign forms, alias vs canonical field names) and has been replaced outright.**
- **Journal storage — delta kind DELETED (finding 1b, clamp-loss fix):** every mutation now journals `{kind:"set", prev, next}` unconditionally — `prev`/clamped-`next` are already in scope at every write site regardless of whether the original write was `delta=` or absolute. The old `delta`-kind entry recorded the ORIGINAL signed delta and re-derived a fresh clamp on revert, which LOST information whenever the write itself clamped: HP at 3 with `delta="-8"` clamps to 0; a delta-kind revert re-applied `+8` against the live (already-0) value and landed on 8, not the original 3. No migration shim — this is a session-fresh feature that has never run live on Corey's machine; `mrrLoadMutationJournal` discards (with one summary warn) any bucket still holding a pre-fix delta-kind entry rather than reinterpret it.
- **States get a real "clear" (finding 1c):** a state's first-ever set journals `prev: null` (already true before this round); reverting it now dispatches an internal-only `attrs.__mrrStateClear === true` sentinel through `applyStateMutation`'s states branch (`delete sheet.states[name]`, sole-writer preserved) instead of trying to "set" the state to the string `"null"` or rejecting for having no valid label. Provably unreachable from tag text: `parseStateAttrs` only ever assigns STRING capture-group values, so even a tag author typing `__mrrStateClear="true"` would produce the string `"true"`, which fails the sentinel's strict boolean `=== true` check.
- **Per-entry revert outcomes (finding 1d):** a bucket's `reverted` flag is now `true` only when EVERY entry inverted successfully this pass; a failed entry is flagged `entry.revertFailed = true` and excluded from any later swipe-back reapply (a defensive per-entry filter in `mrrReapplyBucket` — currently unreachable given the "reverted only when all succeeded" invariant, but guards against future changes to when entries get appended, e.g. a runs-transport mutation landing in an already-reverted bucket).
- **`finalizeMutation` split (finding 5d):** audited its four duties — `saveSheet` (which itself schedules the existing 1.5s-debounced 3-surface autosync: chat `customTrackerFields` / overlay-agent prompts / field-reference lorebook), `renderSheet`, a rolling `state.mutationLog` append, and `showMutationToast`. During a revert/reapply replay (`mrrJournalSuppressed`), all four are now skipped inside `finalizeMutation` itself — `mrrRevertBucket`/`mrrReapplyBucket` batch ONE `saveSheet` + ONE `renderSheet` after the whole bucket loop instead of once per entry (the single `saveSheet` call still fires the debounced autosync exactly once), and the mutation-log/toast are suppressed entirely rather than batched — a revert/reapply is bookkeeping, not a new player-caused event worth N fresh notifications. `mrrJournalSuppressed` itself now save/restores its prior value around both functions (matching the existing bucket-key pattern) instead of blindly assuming false on exit (finding 5e).
- **Runs-poller cross-chat + throw-containment (finding 7):** `pollCustomAgentRuns` had no stale-chat guard at all — a chat switch during its async swipe-index fetch let the eventual apply still write against whatever chat happened to be active when the promise resolved. Now captures `pollChatId` at poll start and checks `state.chatId !== pollChatId` before applying, mirroring `processChatMessage`'s existing guard. `processedRunIds[runId] = true` for a tag-bearing row moved from BEFORE the apply (marking a mutation "processed" before it had actually landed — permanently losing it on a chat-switch or a throw) to AFTER `applyStateTagsWithDedup` returns for that specific row, in its own try/catch — a throw on one row warns honestly, does not mark it processed (retried next poll), and does not abort the rest of the batch; `finishRunsPoll()` runs in a `finally` so batch bookkeeping survives regardless. Finding 7c (journal-key drift — a second swipe landing mid-batch journals a later row under a now-stale index) accepted and commented, not fixed: the window is one batch's synchronous iteration and self-corrects on the next poll's fresh fetch.
- **Storage — stale-wins on quota failure (finding 4b):** `lsSet`/`lsDel` used to stamp the local ts-map REGARDLESS of whether the physical `localStorage` write actually succeeded — a quota-exceeded or private-mode failure left the OLD value on disk but gave it a FRESH timestamp, making a stale local copy look newer than the server's at the next merge and win, silently discarding whatever legitimately-newer data the server held. Both now stamp the ts-map only when the underlying write (`lsSetRaw`/the now-boolean-returning `lsDelRaw`) actually succeeded; a failure warns once per key per session instead. The server-side cache push still runs regardless — independent of whether the local mirror wrote successfully.
- **Storage — stale in-memory state after multi-tab reconcile (finding 4a):** `mrrReconcileFromOtherTabs` adopted a newer `localStorage` value into the cache (fixing what a FRESH `lsGet()` would return) but never touched already-loaded in-memory copies (`state.sheet`, `state.characters`, `state.activeCharacterId`) — the next autosave from THIS tab would write the stale in-memory sheet straight back over the just-adopted newer value from the other tab. Now tracks which keys the sweep actually adopted and, when one is the active character's sheet key or the active chat's roster/active-char-pointer key, reloads the affected state through the normal `loadSheet`/`loadCharacters`/`loadActiveCharacterId` path and re-renders — never a parallel ad-hoc merge.
- **Smaller (findings 11-20, various):** removed the unimplemented `openEnded.firstRollOnly: false` path from the schema/docs (the roller only ever behaves as `true`); `state.diceActiveModeId`/`diceBuiltModeId` reset on ruleset switch and `finalizeRoll`'s `mode=` injection now verifies the id still resolves against the CURRENT ruleset's `additionalModes` before injecting (a stale id surviving a ruleset switch could otherwise tag a roll with a mechanic that no longer exists); `mrrProcessedTextMemo` now stamps on the composite-already-processed early return too, not just after a fresh apply, closing a repeated-refetch gap; `um=` (unusual-event/unusual-success) emission is now gated by a new optional `openEnded.unusualFaces: [int...]` schema field (no default — must be explicit) instead of firing unconditionally for any roll-high d100 ruleset — `rulesets/rolemaster/ruleset.json` sets `[66, 100]` (its bundle rebuilt), every other roll-high ruleset stops getting spurious `um=` tags; `evalFormula` now returns `null` (not `0`) on an unevaluable formula, and its one meaningfully-different caller (`quickRollForDerived`'s target fallback) was already written to treat only `null` as "didn't evaluate" — audited, no other caller distinguishes 0-vs-null; in-file comments citing `tools/b2r-revert-probes.mjs`/`tools/b18-multimode-probes.mjs` corrected to note the harnesses live in the session scratchpad, not the repo. Accepted, comment/warn-only, not fixed: finding 10 (journal growth — sized honestly as a few KB per long-lived chat, unbounded, same GC-less class as its sibling prefixes; `mrrSaveMutationJournal` now also checks its own `lsSet` return and warns on failure), finding 14 (a run row with no discoverable messageId degrading to a synthetic per-run anchor now warns per occurrence instead of silently losing cross-transport dedup and swipe-aware journaling for that mutation), finding 25 (a message still mid-stream when first observed can be marked processed against incomplete tag text — pre-existing, one comment line added at the composite-exact check, not solved).
- Probes: all five existing harnesses extended for these fixes (headline 1a admits-identical-swipe-tag probe with negative controls, 1b clamp round-trip, 1c first-set-revert-to-clear, 1d partial-revert-failure-no-double-apply, poller throw-containment, reconcile-active-sheet-refresh, ts-map no-stamp-on-quota-failure) — see the session scratchpad probe files for the full list; PASS/FAIL counts recorded in the executing session's report, not duplicated here.

### Fixed — round-10 surgical fixes (verification-pass follow-up, not released yet)

- **FIX-A (blocker, replaces round 9's dedup-key-clearing mechanism entirely):** the reconstruction approach couldn't match real tags on several axes at once (`reason=`/`target=` participate in the dedup signature but are never journaled; `"+N"` vs bare `"N"` sign forms; `set=`/`value=`/`current=` absolute variants vs `delta=`; a field ALIAS the agent typed gets journaled under its canonical name, so the reconstructed sig's `field=` differs from the original tag's). Correct fix: capture the EXACT dedup signature at the moment it's already computed. `applyStateTagsWithDedup` now threads it through a save/restored module-scoped `mrrCurrentApplySig` around each `applyStateMutation(...)` call (same save/restore pattern as `mrrCurrentJournalBucketKey`); `mrrJournalMutation` stamps it onto the entry it creates as `entry.sig`. `mrrClearDedupKeysForBucket` is now a one-line loop deleting `anchor + "::" + entry.sig + "::"`-prefixed keys per entry — no reconstruction, zero mismatch axes. `mrrBuildReplayAttrs` is unchanged and still used by `mrrRevertBucket`/`mrrReapplyBucket` for actual sheet replay — only its use for sig reconstruction is gone. **Multiple-entries-per-tag, confirmed:** one `[mrr-state:]` tag CAN produce more than one journal entry when `normalizeStateAttrs` splits a multi-type damage tag (e.g. `bashing="1" lethal="2"`) into multiple normalized attrs — but each normalized attr gets its OWN `applyStateTagsWithDedup` loop iteration, its OWN sig, and (since every one of `applyStateMutation`'s three journaling branches returns immediately after its single `mrrJournalMutation` call) AT MOST one journal entry — so the mapping is exactly 1 normalized-attr : 1 sig : ≤1 journal entry, never many-entries-one-sig or the reverse.
- **FIX-B (blocker, new bug introduced by round 9's finding 5c):** the memo stamp on the composite-already-processed early return is now gated `if (idx !== null && idx !== undefined)`. When idx is unresolvable (fetch failure or out-of-window), `isMessageProcessed`'s prefix-scan fallback returns true for ANY swipe of that msgId ever processed — even a brand-new swipe whose tags never actually applied. Unconditionally stamping the memo there (5c's bug) permanently blocked recovery: every future re-observation of that exact (never-applied) text would short-circuit at the fast-path gate instead of retrying the fetch, which is the only thing that can ever resolve idx and let the real check run. Pre-5c behavior (always retry when idx is unknown) is restored for that case; 5c's fix for its own original bug (repeated fetches on a genuinely-already-applied re-observation) is unaffected.
- **FIX-C:** the poller's stale-chat guard existed only at the SECOND async hop (`fetchSwipeIndexMap(...).then`) — a chat switch during the FIRST `apiFetch` (fetching the runs list itself) was unguarded and had a real failure mode: `loadProcessedRunIds(chatId)` had already loaded/created the NEW chat's `processedRunIds` set before the callback fires, so the baseline-seeding branch would seed the OLD chat's run ids into the NEW chat's set and mark the new chat's `runsBaselineExists` true without it ever having been genuinely baselined — that chat's first real poll would then see its own entire pre-existing run history as "new" and replay it in one sweep. The guard is now duplicated at the top of the first `.then` too (not hoisted out — each hop independently guards its own async gap).
- **FIX-D:** `mrrClearDedupKeysForBucket(...)`'s call site in `mrrRevertBucket` is now wrapped in try/catch (warn on catch) — an uncaught throw there used to skip the `mrrSaveMutationJournal` + batched `saveSheet`/`renderSheet` that follow it, silently losing the revert's own journal persistence and sheet render even though the sheet-side revert loop had already succeeded.
- **Nits:** `mrrJournalMutation`'s dead `kind` parameter removed (it always hardcoded `entry.kind = "set"`; the 3 call sites no longer pass the now-meaningless `"set"` literal). `mrrLsWriteFailWarned` is now namespaced by operation (`"set:" + key` / `"del:" + key`) — previously a `lsSet` failure warning for a key silently suppressed a LATER, distinct `lsDel` failure warning for that same key (and vice versa), since both shared one un-namespaced flag per key.
- **Doc (ship-with, no code — queued for Corey's morning call):** added a prominent, impossible-to-miss limit callout to this file's B2-R section (above) and to `COUPLINGS.md` row 15 — swipe-revert does NOT undo typed-damage-TRACK hits (Exalted-class systems' health boxes) or any other array/object mutation; only numeric fields and states are journaled today.
- Probes: `b2r-revert-probes.mjs`'s headline probe (R8) re-derived to use REAL taught-shape tags (`reason=`, `target=`, a `"+N"` healing delta, a `set=` absolute write, and an aliased field name) proving the exact-sig capture clears all of them — the round-9 version's tags were too simple to have caught the reconstruction mismatch it was supposedly proving fixed. New probes for FIX-B (fetch-miss on a new swipe leaves the memo unstamped; a later successful fetch still applies) and FIX-C (a chat switch during the first hop does not contaminate the new chat's baseline). Full gate suite + all six harnesses green — see the executing session's report for probe-by-probe output.

### Added — Rolemaster (RMFRP) ruleset (Phase C MVP)

- New ruleset `rulesets/rolemaster/`: mechanics-only RMFRP core — open-ended d100 (the new `direction: "high"` + `openEnded` resolution extension), nine-step maneuver difficulty ladder, seven-band result ladder (+ UM 66/100), resistance-roll procedure, combat-in-narration (OB/DB/AT structure, crit severity vocabulary), 10 stats (Primary/Development grouping per the book), 26 skills with book-verified stat triads, hits/Power Points/Exhaustion. Professions, spells, talents, and setting content are deliberately NOT included — `USER-LOREBOOK-GUIDE.md` specifies how users plug their own class/spell lorebooks into the shipped agents (key-prefix conventions, graceful degradation, house-rule precedence). No reproduced table contents; structure and procedure only.

### Added — server-backed character/pref storage via `marinara.storage` (Phase 2, B1)

- Character sheets (`mrr-character-*`), the per-chat character roster (`mrr-chars-<chatId>`) and active-character pointer (`mrr-active-char-<chatId>`), the active ruleset id, and the spellbook lorebook-id cache now sync through `marinara.storage` when the host engine supports it (2.4.x+), with `localStorage` kept as a current, always-written local mirror — see the classification table and adapter comments at the top of `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` (~line 273) for exactly which keys sync and which stay device-local (panel position/size, processed-message/run dedup sets, the ruleset library, and a few other keys are deliberately excluded). The roster/active-pointer keys were added on review: without them a second browser can't see which characters exist for a chat or which is active, even with the sheets themselves synced.
- Hydrate-once in-memory cache: one `marinara.storage.get()` at startup, then every existing `lsGet`/`lsSet`/`lsDel` call site (~66, zero call-site changes) reads/writes that cache synchronously for synced keys and falls through to plain `localStorage` for everything else — sheet reads stay synchronous everywhere.
- Background writes are debounced (≤ 1s) and patch only the keys mutated since the last flush; a failed patch retries with backoff (2s/8s/30s) and, on exhaustion, isolates and quarantines the offending key to local-storage-only for the session. A 900,000-byte size guard runs before every patch against the projected merged record; over budget, character-family keys (sheets/roster/active-pointer) fall back to local-only.
- One-time, resumable, copy-not-move migration on first activation with server storage present: existing `localStorage` values are copied into `marinara.storage` per key (never overwriting an existing server entry — a stored tombstone counts as present), gated by a per-device `mrr-migrated-v1` flag.
- `marinara.storage.clear()` (which removes the *entire* extension record, not a single key — corrected round 5: this loader's full-page runtime names the whole-record-wipe method `clear()`, not `delete()`; see `COUPLINGS.md` row 1b) is never called; per-key deletion uses a `patch({key: null})` tombstone, treated as present-but-absent on every read and by migration/merge.
- Degradation warnings (a size-guard trip, a quarantined key, or hydrate failing over to `localStorage`) are now visible in the extension's UI, not just the console: a lazily-created `div.mrr-warn-strip` at the top of the main sheet panel, live-updated for warnings fired mid-session, with a session-local × dismiss.
- **Timestamped envelopes + newest-wins hydrate merge (Corey override — this feature had not shipped yet, so this amends the entry above rather than adding a new one):** the original "server always wins once present, no timestamps" precedence was replaced before release because crash recovery must never resurrect older character data. Every synced-key value stored server-side is now `{"t": <epoch ms>, "v": <raw string value>}` (tombstones are timestamped too); `localStorage` itself is untouched, with the LS-side timestamp tracked in one local-only sidecar, `mrr-ts-map` (never itself synced — syncing it would let the shallow server-side merge whole-map-clobber another browser's edits). On every hydrate (not just the one-time migration), the newer side wins per key — a browser that saved locally but crashed before its background patch landed correctly reclaims and re-flushes that edit (stamped with its *original* save time, not a fresh one) instead of silently losing it to a stale server copy.
- `COUPLINGS.md` row 1 updated with the verified `get`/`patch`/`clear` contract (shallow-merge patch, null-stores-as-tombstone, 1,000,000-byte cap on the merged record, whole-record-nuke `clear`, and the envelope/newest-wins-merge contract).
- **Round-5 critical-fix batch (adversarial review; still amending this entry — none of B1/B1-T had shipped):** a hung `marinara.storage.get()` no longer bricks `init()` forever — `hydrateStore()` now races it against an 8000ms timeout and degrades to local-only on either a rejection or a timeout, and a late real response after the timeout has already won is inert by construction. The in-memory cache used to go stale across tabs — a second tab never refreshed from `localStorage` after its own initial hydrate, even after another tab saved newer data locally; a reconcile now runs on `visibilitychange`→visible and adopts any `localStorage` value newer than the cache holds (without re-pushing it server-side — the tab that made the edit already owns that). Editing a quarantined key used to leave its local timestamp stale, so the newest-wins merge could silently discard the edit at the next hydrate; the local timestamp now always updates on write regardless of quarantine. An over-budget sync batch used to wedge ALL keys in that batch, including small, well-behaved ones, behind one oversized character sheet; only the character-family keys are quarantined now, and the rest of the batch is retried and still sent. Envelope validation on read is stricter — a malformed value (wrong shape, non-finite timestamp, or a `v` that isn't a string/null) is now treated as absent (falls back to the `localStorage` copy) rather than risking a non-string value leaking out of `lsGet`.

### Fixed — regexScripts rejected by the engine's catastrophic-backtracking check (live-bug, found during Phase A smoke)

- Every non-d20 ruleset's `regexScripts` (12 of 14; `dnd5e`/`pathfinder2e` ship none by design) used unbounded `\s*`/`\s+`/`\d+` quantifiers that the engine's `isPatternSafe()` heuristic flags as an unsafe pattern — the live Exalted 3e install hit this as a 400 on `POST /regex-scripts`. Root-caused to the shared generator (`tools/build-regex-scripts.mjs`, not per-ruleset authoring); bounded every quantifier to a finite `{n,m}` (whitespace up to 20 chars to tolerate LLM pretty-printed multi-line tags, digits up to 6). Verified safe against the actual engine validator function, not just re-tested against our own copy.
- Added `tools/lib/regex-safety.mjs`, a verified port of the engine's safety heuristic, wired into `npm run validate-bundles` — a future or user-authored ruleset with an unsafe `regexScripts` pattern now fails locally with a clear message instead of failing mid-import on a live engine.

### Fixed — Marinara 2.4.3 extension-loading compatibility (Phase A)

- Marinara v2.3.4 deleted the pre-existing `CustomThemeInjector` extension API entirely; v2.4.0+ reinstated extensions under a thinner `FullPageExtensionApi` v1 shape via the Full-page External Extension lane. Added a compat shim (top of `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js`) that reconstructs `addElement`/`on`/`apiFetch` on top of the new API, replicating the old engine's exact semantics, so all existing call sites work unchanged on 2.4.3 (and best-effort on pre-2.3.4 hosts via passthrough).
- Added the `x-marinara-csrf` header (required by the engine's 2.4.x CSRF hook) to every unsafe-method `/api` call — the compat shim's `apiFetch`, plus `apiPostRaw` and `apiDeleteRaw`.
- Routed the extension's three raw `fetch(` call sites (`apiDeleteRaw`, `apiPostRaw`, `fetchRulesetFromUrl`) through the new `mrrFetch` accessor so 2.4.3's Extensions UI can attribute MRR's network traffic.
- `tools/validate-extension-package.mjs`: added an async-arrow strict-parse check mirroring the 2.4.3 full-page wrapper; changed the 1&nbsp;MiB inline-JS size check from a hard failure to a warning (the engine removed that cap at 2.4.3).
- Added `COUPLINGS.md` — a repo-root ledger of every engine touchpoint (REST routes, DOM selectors, storage keys) with its coupling class, probe, and fallback. Any future commit touching an engine surface must update it in the same commit.
- Rewrote `docs/INSTALL.md` and the README quickstart for the 2.4.3 External Extensions import flow (both gates, Review-and-Run hash approval, per-edit re-approval) and documented the loose-`.js`-import trap (silently imports as a sandboxed Worker extension where MRR cannot run).
- `AGENTS.md` §8.5 rewritten to the 2.4.3 loading/trust/CSRF/packaging truth.

### Fixed — 2026-07-03 code-review correctness batch (`89f4c5c`)

- `syncSheetToChat` bails when no active character — an empty name prefix previously made `indexOf("")` match every field and wipe other characters' `customTrackerFields` on read-modify-write (P0-1).
- `ensureTrackCells` now truncates trailing-empty cells only; the old unconditional slice-to-totalLen destroyed filled overflow damage (P0-2).
- `removeActiveCharacter` reads/deletes the real `characterKey` home (with legacy `sheetKey` fallback) so ability-lorebook cleanup runs and the sheet can't resurrect for a future same-id character (P0-4).
- `deleteAbilityLorebookEntry` resolves the spellbook lorebook id before deleting, so a fresh-reload null `spellbookLbId` no longer no-ops and orphans the entry (P0-5).
- `castAbilityPool` refuses a cast the pools can't afford (mote/willpower/blood models) instead of clamping to 0 while still emitting a full-cost tag (P0-6).
- `buildSyncFields`/`buildSheetForPrompt` fold equipped-gear bonuses into synced + prompt numbers so injected stats match dice-widget rolls (P0-7).
- `g`-flag added to the sheet-block strip regex (removes all accumulated blocks); auto-sync skips while install/uninstall is in flight (D7).
- `buildDice` guards against a ruleset with no `resolution` block (D8).

### Fixed — dead State Mutator console-sniff replaced with runs-endpoint transport (`81c6117`, B1, ships inert)

- The State Mutator overlay's `[mrr-state:]` output never reaches chat DOM, so the narrator path can't see it. The old capture monkey-patched `console.log` to sniff the engine's State Mutator line — that line has been a prod-stripped debug-gated `console.warn` since engine v1.4.0 and has never fired on any 2.0.x+ build (impact review finding). Removed the console-sniff entirely; replaced with a poller on `GET /agents/runs/:chatId/custom` (persisted custom-agent runs, present since engine v1.5.7).
- Ships **inert by design**: `MRR_RUNS_POLLER_MODE` = `"off"` (default, byte-identical to prior narrator-only behavior) | `"dump"` (log one raw run row for live-smoke verification, apply nothing) | `"apply"` (full second transport). Two-stage so a second live state-writer is never activated blind.
- New shared `applyStateTagsWithDedup(tags, anchorId)` keyed on `anchorId::contentSig::occIdx` — both the narrator path and the poller route through it, so identical tags across turns/transports apply exactly once.
- Live activation and the apply-exactly-once proof are deferred to the live-smoke session (plan §4.A6) — needs the running engine.

### Changed — single-track consolidation, this is now the only Marinara RPG extension (v1.0.0)

- This extension now supersedes the separate RP-mode extension (`Marinara-RPG-RP-Mode-Extension`, the `mrrp-` namespace), which is retired/frozen. Marinara 2.0 made custom agents mode-agnostic, so one install runs in **both Game Mode and Roleplay Mode** — the wedge that forced two extensions (mode-scoped agents, the 50-char reputation cap, forced dice-tag format) is gone. The loader is mode-agnostic: it resolves the active chat the same way in both modes and injects one universal GM framing (no mode-branched prompts).
- Version bumped to **1.0.0** across `package.json` and the loader `EXT_VERSION` (was a `0.5.0` / `0.4.0` drift).

### Added — Marinara 2.0+ folder/manifest/zip packaging

- New `tools/build-extension-package.mjs` emits the 2.0+ folder/manifest import format into `releases/<version>/`, **byte-compatible with Marinara's own export** (`packages/client/src/lib/extension-transfer.ts`): `Extensions/Marinara-RPG-Extension/manifest.json` (`kind:"marinara.extension"`, config carries inline `js` + `jsPath:"extension.js"`; `css:null` since the loader self-injects embedded CSS) + a sibling `extension.js` + a `marinara-extensions.json` folder envelope, packaged as `Marinara-RPG-Extension.extension.zip`. The single-file `.js` import remains a supported fallback.
- New `tools/validate-extension-package.mjs` asserts the generated manifest + envelope + sibling files + zip against the engine contract (`extension.schema.ts` limits, `manifest-package.ts` shape), including an inline-`js` syntax-parse guard and sibling/inline consistency.
- Verified against the **Marinara 2.0.5** extension load mechanism (`CustomThemeInjector` now imports extensions as ES modules from a Blob with `marinara` supplied via `globalThis.__marinaraExtensionApis`); the loader is unaffected because the engine still wraps it as `function(marinara){…}` called with `this === globalThis`.

### Added — character + agent migration off the RP-mode extension

- Character import now accepts **both** `mrr-character-bundle` and legacy `mrrp-character-bundle` files (always re-saves `mrr-`). New **Import from RP-mode ext** path reads the installed RP-mode extension's raw `mrrp-character-*` localStorage directly (read-only) and re-saves under the `mrr-` namespace — recovering sheets that early `mrrp-` exports shipped empty.
- Ported the RP-mode **agent-manager** UI (manage / import / dedupe overlay agents) into the loader, on the `mrr-` namespace. Agent import accepts both `mrr-agents` and legacy `mrrp-agents` JSON on read; always writes `mrr-agents`.

### Fixed — six rulesets emitted unparseable `mrrp-` state/roll tags in Game Mode

- `coc7e`, `stewpot`, `gurps-lite`, `trophy-dark`, `pathfinder2e`, and `lasers-and-feelings` `gm-agent.md` prompts instructed the model to emit `[mrrp-state:]` / `[mrrp-roll:]` / `[mrrp-help:]` tags, but the GM loader's parsers match `mrr-` only — so those state mutations (sanity, ruin, hearth, HP, conditions) were silently dropped in Game Mode. Rewritten to `mrr-` and the bundles rebuilt.

### Changed — terminology + agents schema normalized to `mrr-`

- `tools/build-agents.mjs` now emits `"schema":"mrr-agents"` (was `"mrrp-agents"`) and no longer cross-references the retired RP-mode loader; all 14 `agents.json` rebuilt.
- The obsolete "50-character reputation cap" workaround note is removed from the `dnd5e`, `vtmv20`, `exalted3e`, and `fate-core` GM prompts (the cap is gone in Marinara 2.0+); replaced with a one-line "reputation is free-form in 2.0+" note. `AGENTS.md` updated to match.

> The four entries below are ported from contributor @Alatheus1's pull
> requests against the RP-mode repo (Marinara-RPG-RP-Mode-Extension #1–#4),
> adapted to GM-mode framing and the `mrr-` namespace. Original authorship
> credited via `Co-authored-by` trailers on the porting commits.

### Fixed — `parametersSchema` rejected by the host validator (ported from RP-mode #4)

- `tools/build-custom-tools.mjs` shipped `parametersSchema: {}` for the static reference tool baked into every ruleset bundle. Current Marinara v2.0 host builds reject that shape and silently drop the tool on install, so the per-ruleset `<id>_reference` tool never reached the model. The generator now emits `parametersSchema: { type: "object", properties: {} }` — the explicit JSON Schema shape the host expects. All `rulesets/*/bundle.json` rebuilt to pick up the fix.

### Fixed — character-bundle export/import round-trip (ported from RP-mode #3)

- `collectBundle()` / `applyBundle()` in `extension/RPG-Extension-GM-Mode.js` were still reading/writing the legacy chat-scoped `sheetKey(chatId, charId)`, missed by the v0.2.1 migration that moved authoritative sheet storage to the chat-independent `characterKey(charId)`. Symptom: exported `mrr-character-bundle` files shipped with `sheets: {}` for every character, and re-imports loaded blank sheets. Fix reads `characterKey` first (legacy `sheetKey` fallback), writes imported sheets to `characterKey`, and clears both keys symmetrically on overwrite. No schema change.

### Added — Exalted Versus World of Darkness reference ruleset (ported from RP-mode #1)

- `rulesets/exwod/` — *Exalted Versus World of Darkness* (Revised), a fan crossover running Exalted-style Chosen on the WoD 20th-anniversary Storyteller chassis. Hybrid of the `vtmv20` chassis (V20 d10 dice-pool, Talents/Skills/Knowledges, 7-level health track) and `exalted3e` Essence/Charm machinery (mote commitment, Anima Banner state-banner, Intimacies, Ox-Body extensible health track). Supports all nine playable Exalt types on a type-agnostic sheet. Ships `ruleset.json` + GM-mode `gm-agent.md` + 29-entry `lorebook.json` + five per-ruleset sub-agent overrides + `INSTALL.md`; built `bundle.json` / `agents.json` included.

### Added — Werewolf: The Apocalypse 20th Anniversary reference ruleset (ported from RP-mode #2)

- `rulesets/w20/` — *Werewolf: The Apocalypse 20th Anniversary Edition* (W20, 2013 Onyx Path). Built on the `vtmv20` chassis with W20 machinery: Primal-Urge / Enigmas / Rituals ability swaps, a three-pool Rage/Gnosis/Willpower economy (each capped by a Permanent-X stat set by Auspice/Breed/Tribe), permanent + temporary Renown (Glory/Honour/Wisdom), Rank, five-form shifting as a state-banner, and Frenzy/Harano/Spirit-World states. Ships `ruleset.json` + GM-mode `gm-agent.md` + 29-entry `lorebook.json` + five per-ruleset sub-agent overrides + `INSTALL.md`; built `bundle.json` / `agents.json` included.

## [0.5.0] - 2026-05-22

**BREAKING for v0.4.x users.** GM-mode now ships only the canonical sub-agent pool (no menu), auto-installed and enabled at ruleset import. Migration is uninstall-then-reinstall the ruleset. Two new reference rulesets (Blades in the Dark, Genesys), three new per-system parallel-phase overlays (Exalted's anima banner + Charm cooldowns, VTM's blood pool), and the Vector 8 per-chat scenario-default install path also land. Companion to RP-mode v0.3.0 which preserves the toggleable two-path model.
